# Session Log — Blood Debt

## Session 1 — 2026-07-11

Darkhawk Blondin, freshly arrived in the border town of Kessick's Ford, spotted a suspicious "hire notice" on the bounty board — strong backs wanted, no questions asked, pay up front. Intimidated the nervous notice clerk into talking: the posting traced to a mysterious "Vantor Trading Concern," the recruitment was a trap (come alone, unarmed, after dark), and several townsfolk who'd answered similar notices over the past month had never been seen again.

Scouted the Hollowmere estate outside town by daylight, then returned after dark and answered the "invitation" — armed, not alone, and openly skeptical. Talked his way past the gate guards just long enough to provoke a fight, then killed all four in the resulting skirmish (captain and one guard fled on foot; both put down with thrown javelins and a pursuit kill).

Found a boarded outbuilding on the grounds holding six kidnapped townsfolk, including **Tomas Reeve** — the missing merchant's son from another bounty notice. Freed them and sent them to town under Tomas's lead to alert the guard.

Infiltrated the manor itself (tripped a hidden door alarm in the process), ambushed and killed two responding guards in the entry hall, then cornered **Corwin Pell**, the operation's bookkeeper. Under pressure — and after being confronted directly about his complicity — Corwin turned: handed over a trafficking ledger, a sealed letter, payment evidence, guard counts, and the manor's layout, then agreed to turn himself in to Tomas and testify.

Descended to the cellar and cleared it: killed **the Keeper** (the cells' overseer) and three more guards, freed the last two "sorted" captives, and sent them to town as well. Searched the dead and found confirmation that "the Circuit" — the trafficking operation — has standing orders to *report, not engage* anyone connected to "the Ashpit incident," i.e. Darkhawk's own past.

Looted **Vantor's private office**: 280 gold, a hand-drawn regional map (Kessick's Ford, Hollowmere, and **Ravensmoor** named; four more locations marked only with the Circuit's fist-and-chain seal, unidentified), an unsigned letter referencing an unseen superior called **"the Warden"** and a quota for "Ravensmoor numbers before the season opens," and an engraved ceremonial dagger.

Made a deliberate, high-risk choice: piled all ten Hollowmere dead in the service tunnel — Vantor's actual point of entry — with a handwritten note warning him and the Warden that an Ashpit survivor is alive and hunting them. This trades anonymity for a direct psychological strike; expect the Circuit to respond accordingly.

Returned to Kessick's Ford. Tomas and Corwin both safe, the local magistrate now investigating Hollowmere officially (evidence and bodies left in place at Darkhawk's request). Secured Tomas's promise to protect Corwin and his daughter. Took a long rest above the Reeve family's counting house.

**Ends:** fully rested, 3 nights remaining until Vantor is due at Hollowmere.

## Session 2 — 2026-07-11 (continued)

Leveled up (milestone) to **Barbarian 2** — gained Reckless Attack, Danger Sense, and a jump to 27 max HP.

Spent the day in Kessick's Ford tying up loose ends. Told Corwin's daughter her father was a hero; Corwin, visibly moved, committed to testifying fully regardless of cost. Showed Halvard Reeve the recovered Circuit map: he recognized the fist-and-chain seal from a warehouse manifest at **Bramhollow**, a waystation ~2 days out on the Ravensmoor road, and identified it as one of the map's sealed nodes. Struck a standing mutual-aid pact with House Reeve — Halvard relays any Circuit-related intel his trade network picks up, in exchange for Darkhawk's help if House Reeve ever needs it.

Noticed a second sealed node on the map, roughly 3-4 miles **south of Hollowmere**, that Halvard had no explanation for — no manifest, no rumor, nothing his trade network ever touched. He flagged it as more concerning than Bramhollow specifically because it isn't hiding behind legitimate paperwork at all, and pointed toward local trapper **Mara Fenn** as someone who might know the terrain.

Found Mara at the Broken Wheel tavern. She independently confirmed the southern site: a "dead ground" she's avoided for two years (no game, no birds, occasional distant torchlight), and revealed the missing tannery lads used to drink at her regular table — giving her a personal stake. Hired her as a guide (20 gp) to investigate the site, departure set for night, after the swords were ready.

Picked up the reforged longswords from Perrin Dask — properly balanced now for two-weapon fighting, off-hand strikes hit with full Strength behind them instead of the weaker standard off-hand rule.

**Ends:** night has fallen, swords in hand, Mara Fenn ready at the smithy. About to depart for the dead ground south of Hollowmere.

## Session 3 — 2026-07-12

Guided by Mara, infiltrated the **Dead Ground site** south of Hollowmere — a hidden "proving grounds" built into old ruins where captives are forced to fight each other; winners "graduate" onward into the Circuit, losers often don't survive. Ambushed and killed all 6 guards there with Mara's help (bow support throughout). Freed three captives and sent them to Kessick's Ford.

Found a fourth, long-held prisoner: **Kaldur Stonewake**, an older Goliath. He recognized Darkhawk's carried trophy as **Thundertree clan** craftsmanship — clan territory in the high passes, north and east — giving Darkhawk his first real lead on his own identity. Escorted Kaldur back to town for care.

Looted the site: gold, 2 healing potions, a Trial record (11 captives "graduated" this year alone, 17 more blank/unaccounted for), and a duty log confirming guards rotate between Hollowmere, Bramhollow, and this site, and that **Vantor's visit would hit both Hollowmere and this site the same night, right after the Trial closed.**

Leveled up narratively confirmed at Barbarian 2 (Reckless Attack, Danger Sense — noted here for continuity, actually gained just before this session's field work).

Downtime in Kessick's Ford: showed Kaldur the recovered regional map — he confirmed Ravensmoor firsthand (a real market town, Circuit runs beneath it in "season") and flagged a possible second proving-grounds site, "the Western Yard" (unconfirmed, secondhand). Shared his own Ashpit backstory with Kaldur, who offered genuine solidarity — clan isn't only blood. Gathered stakeout supplies (cloaks, muffling wrap) with Mara's guidance. Halvard passed on a warning: someone's already asking around about a large Goliath mercenary, quietly, not via bounty.

**The stakeout:** watched Vantor arrive at Hollowmere's service tunnel with 2 guards, discover the pile of bodies and the note, and panic — overheard him order a rider to Bramhollow to report to **the Warden** personally, confirming Bramhollow is the Warden's direct reporting line, not just a waystation. Raced Mara to the Dead Ground site ahead of Vantor's party and watched him discover the second message pile there too, fully unraveling.

Confronted Vantor directly (unarmed approach, offer to talk instead of kill). It went sideways — his guards panicked, one fled with Vantor toward Bramhollow while the other held the line. Killed the blocking guard, lost the chase. Vantor's own words confirmed **Bramhollow is where he reports to the Warden.**

Pushed Mara for a forced 2-day march to reach Bramhollow ahead of Vantor — succeeded, but the toll caught up (failed CON save, gained Exhaustion 1). Set an ambush outside Bramhollow; concealment was rough (fatigue-driven bad rolls both sides), and the last guard spotted the ambush before it sprang. Fought and killed him with Mara's help.

**Captured Vantor alive**, bound, unharmed. Hid the dead guard's body, retreated to a hidden camp, and took a long rest — exhaustion cleared, full resources restored.

Leveled up (milestone) to **Barbarian 3**, choosing **Path of the Totem Warrior — Bear**: Spirit Seeker (ritual Beast Sense / Speak with Animals) and Totem Spirit granting full damage resistance (except psychic) while raging. HP jumped to 39.

Heading back toward Kessick's Ford, Mara scouted ahead and spotted a 5-rider search party sent from Bramhollow, already hunting for the overdue Vantor. Sent Mara ahead with the bound prisoner while Darkhawk held the road alone. Killed all five riders solo (with Bear Totem's full damage resistance carrying the fight), looted them, botched hiding the bodies (a passing cart likely found the scene), and took their horses to close the distance and catch up with Mara and Vantor.

Rode into Kessick's Ford and delivered Vantor directly to Halvard and Tomas. Under no illusions about his position, **Vantor fully confessed and agreed to turn king's evidence**: confirmed the Warden is a title, not a name he's ever matched to a face — orders arrive only via sealed letters through Bramhollow, never in person. Confirmed the Circuit spans far more territory than what's been seen so far. Admitted he doesn't know who ran Ashpit or who betrayed Darkhawk specifically — only that the incident is used within the Circuit as a cautionary legend. Explained his own entanglement (a debt at 19 he was never allowed to walk away from).

Filled in the last two blanks on the regional map: **the Western Yard** (another proving-grounds site, confirming Kaldur's secondhand rumor) and **Duskgate** (previously the map's last unknown mark — not a fighting site at all, but a private buyer market east of Ravensmoor where the highest-value "graduates" are actually sold). The map is now fully identified.

Handed Vantor to Tomas for the magistrate, with an explicit message: if the law doesn't deliver justice with what Vantor and Corwin have both given them, Darkhawk will be the justice instead. Closed the day out on a quiet, reflective exchange with Mara about law versus justice.

**Ends:** Vantor in custody and cooperating. Hollowmere, the Dead Ground, and the Bramhollow road are all resolved. Open threads for next time: the Warden's true identity, Bramhollow itself (still not directly investigated), the Western Yard, Duskgate, Ravensmoor/the Grand Circuit, and Thundertree clan.

## Session 4 — 2026-07-13

Visited Vantor in custody for a further debrief: rough guard estimates for every known Circuit site (Bramhollow ~8-10, Western Yard ~5-6, Duskgate ~10-12 higher-quality) and hand-sketched layouts for all three, honestly caveated as secondhand/imprecise. Confirmed with Mara: since Western Yard and any loud action at Bramhollow risk alerting the rest of the network, the plan shifted to **Bramhollow first, done quiet** — no bodies, no declaration this time, just extract proof of the Warden's existence and leave undetected.

Recruited **Joss Ferrow**, Mara's old friend running the ferry crossing, as a paid outside lookout (30 gp) for the Bramhollow job — watch and signal only, not a fighter, explicit about his limits given his family.

Sold the five horses and tack taken from the dead search party to Halvard Reeve for 175 gp. Tipped Mara 50 gp unprompted, acknowledging the guiding work was costing her actual trapping income.

Had Perrin Dask rebalance the ceremonial dagger into a real weapon (20 gp) — concealable backup, well-suited to a stealth job. Also commissioned Perrin's off-the-books **sky-iron** (meteoric ore) to be worked into both longswords for 200 gp: not magical, but the blades now count as magical weapons for overcoming resistance/immunity to nonmagical attacks.

Spent a rest day training stealth fundamentals solo (advantage on the first Stealth check at Bramhollow, one-time), gambling for fun at the Broken Wheel (down ~10 gp, correctly spotted a cheat and let it go), and picking up bar rumors: Ravensmoor's "season" reportedly starts at first frost, and local folklore already independently confirms Perrin's sky-iron story.

Mara returned from scouting Bramhollow with real intel — but also a complication: the site is unusually alert, running a tighter rotation than Vantor's numbers accounted for, and is specifically watching for a large Goliath matching Darkhawk's description. Confirmed the warehouse office's exact location (ground floor, southeast corner) and a ~20-minute pre-dawn window when the yard is thinnest.

**Revised plan locked in:** given Darkhawk's description is compromised, Mara goes into the office alone; Darkhawk and Joss hold overwatch outside. If Mara isn't out within **30 minutes**, Darkhawk moves in loud. Agreed to take one more rest day so Mara can confirm the timing window and go in at full strength.

Noted for the next milestone level-up: player wants to multiclass 1 level into Fighter (Second Wind + a Fighting Style, to be picked together when it happens).

**Ends:** resting in Kessick's Ford, one day out from the Bramhollow job. Plan is set: quiet infiltration, Mara solo on the office, Darkhawk + Joss on overwatch, 30-minute trigger.

## Session 5 — 2026-07-14

**Executed the Bramhollow job successfully and quietly.** Joss held outside lookout, Mara infiltrated the warehouse office alone within the 30-minute window and extracted ledgers plus two letters in the Warden's own sealed hand — real physical proof the Warden exists beyond Vantor's word. Darkhawk silently killed and hid one off-pattern patrol guard who nearly discovered the group; no alarm raised, no bodies left visible, no declaration. Joss's lookout contract concluded successfully.

Reviewed all recovered evidence together and made a key deduction: **"the Circuit" implies a closed loop, but every confirmed site (Hollowmere, Dead Ground, Western Yard, Bramhollow, Ravensmoor, Duskgate) is a satellite — intake, processing, distribution, or sale. None of them is the actual hub the Warden operates from, and Ashpit's location is never once stated in any recovered document.** Took this theory to Halvard (now quietly tracing Circuit money) and Magistrate Kell (now checking property/land records) — both agreed to investigate in parallel.

A tavern rumor, followed up in person, independently pointed the same direction: unusual land purchases (old mine workings, paid well above value, buyer untraceable, via a broker firm called **Ravenshollow** out of the regional seat) in the foothills of **the Greyhorn Range — Thundertree's home territory.** Mountain clan trade contacts confirm the Goliath clans there have grown noticeably more closed-off over roughly the same period. Strong circumstantial case that the Circuit's true center sits right where Darkhawk's own lost clan lives.

Arranged **Fallow Cross** as a forward operating base via Halvard — a House Reeve trade post between the Western Yard and the road toward Ravensmoor/Duskgate, staffed by warehouse keeper **Ressa Dane**, with courier **Pell Ashworth** running messages and evidence back to Kessick's Ford on a schedule.

Told Kaldur about the Greyhorn lead; he agreed to guide Darkhawk into the mountains once the more immediate Circuit threats are dealt with — his own clan (Ninebells) is different from Thundertree, but he knows how to move in that country without provoking a watchful clan.

**Leveled up (milestone, for completing Bramhollow):** multiclassed into **Fighter 1** — Second Wind, and the **Protection** fighting style (reaction: impose disadvantage on an attack against an ally within 5 ft while wielding a shield — gives the back-harness shield real tactical purpose). Rolled poorly for HP (+3), bringing max HP to 42.

Also picked up a steel shield with a quick-release back harness from Perrin and a rebalanced ceremonial dagger, and had **sky-iron** (rare meteoric ore) worked into both longswords — not magical, but the blades now count as magical weapons for overcoming resistance/immunity to nonmagical attacks.

**Hit the Western Yard as planned — loud and deliberate this time, by design.** Walked in visibly rather than sneaking, backed by Mara sniping from a ridge. Declared the kill count aloud before the fight (a calculated psychological strike) and fought openly, killing all 6 guards. Took the officer alive as intended, then delivered an explicit, personal message rather than another anonymous note: **"I am Darkhawk Blondin, left for dead by the Warden... 31 dead, only you left alive... there is a storm coming."** Released him to carry it back to Bramhollow. This was a deliberate strategic shift, reasoned through carefully beforehand: an unnamed "Ashpit ghost" rumor is more dangerous to Kessick's Ford than a named, specific target is, since retaliation with no clear target tends to land on the nearest soft target instead. Freed 2 more captives from the site's cells and sent them to Tomas as well.

Secured a small amount of official backing from Magistrate Kell before the operation: he can't send soldiers, but formally recorded that any Circuit retaliation should be understood as targeting Darkhawk specifically, not the town, and raised his own guardsmen's alertness.

**Ends:** at Fallow Cross, Bramhollow evidence and Western Yard both resolved. Darkhawk's identity is now openly known to the Circuit — no longer a rumor. Open threads: Duskgate (personally tied to Ashpit), Ravensmoor, the Greyhorn foothills/Ravenshollow/Thundertree hub lead, and the ongoing Halvard/Kell investigations.

## Session 6 — 2026-07-14/15

Planned the next move at Fallow Cross over breakfast: deliberately deferred Duskgate (too well-defended for two people right now) and the Greyhorn foothills (Kaldur not ready, lead still circumstantial), choosing to scout Ravensmoor first while it's still an "ordinary" town, before the first-frost season starts. Traveled there posing as **"Mr. and Mrs. Blondin,"** a couple looking to open a smithy and a provisioning business, deliberately keeping the cover close to real skills (Darkhawk's real time at Perrin's forge, Mara's real hunting/provisioning background) and moving at an unhurried, unsuspicious pace.

In Ravensmoor: cleared the gate easily, checked into the Hound's Rest, and got two names unprompted from separate locals — **Master Corlin Vey** (the town's land broker) and confirmation that land near the fairgrounds is priced strangely high for a town its size. A stranger in the tavern took real interest in the couple; a fake-drunk bait attempt (badly botched) spooked him into leaving fast and clean, and Mara lost his trail entirely — someone genuinely skilled at not being followed.

Met Vey in person: legitimate broker, but visibly uneasy about the fairground district specifically — a separate, unnamed "holding company" manages those buildings and he's learned not to ask questions. Scouting the district directly, spotted one building that doesn't match the "unused off-season" story (reinforced door, fresh wagon ruts, a recently-used candle-wax watch post) and a permanent watcher stationed near Vey's own office.

**Acquired Old Harlan's Forge** — a real, legitimate property at the edge of the fairground district, leased through Vey — giving Darkhawk and Mara a permanent, unsuspicious foothold to watch the suspicious building long-term. Discovered a well-maintained, deliberately unobtrusive **root-cellar hatch** around back of that building, separate from its main door.

Began rotating night watches on the hatch. Mara witnessed one man use it casually around midnight. Darkhawk later witnessed a muffled wagon delivering crates and an **empty locked cage** to the building in the dead of night — hard evidence that "unused off-season" is false, and that whatever this site does isn't limited to the twice-yearly fair.

**Major breakthrough — pieced together the Circuit's full structure:** intake sites (Hollowmere) feed proving grounds (Dead Ground, Western Yard) which cull and test captives; survivors get shipped to Ravensmoor, where "season" is itself another proving ground in front of buyers and promoters; the actual best of the best don't stay in the public circuit at all — they're sent somewhere private, whispered about like a legend rather than a location. **Ashpit isn't closed history. It's the ongoing, hidden top of the entire pyramid** — which may make it the same place as the Circuit's missing "true center" the Greyhorn foothills lead already pointed toward.

This realization triggered real, dangerous anger — the instinct to storm the hatch immediately. Held to the "no more surprises" pact instead: told Mara directly, let the anger be felt without acting on it (with her steady support), and returned to the plan.

While keeping watch alone that night, deliberately reached for suppressed childhood memory for the first time — recovered real, vivid fragments: cold mountain air, woodsmoke, a voice singing in Giant, the trophy given to him with ceremony, and the fractured moment of being taken, his own name called out behind him — audible in shape and rhythm, but the actual sound still wouldn't resolve. Confirmed to himself: finishing that memory requires standing in front of Thundertree in person.

Shared the memory fully with Mara. The relationship shifted from professional partnership to openly mutual and romantic — a kiss during the cover performance "came out sounding a little too real" for both of them, and they explicitly acknowledged it wasn't an act. Darkhawk asked Mara to be with him when he finally faces Thundertree; she committed without hesitation.

**Leveled up (milestone, belated credit for the Western Yard's full clear — a full fight plus the public identity reveal that was never actually credited before this):** **Fighter 2**, gaining **Action Surge** (once per rest, an additional action — doubles a turn's dual-wield attacks). Rolled poorly for HP again (+4), bringing max HP to 46.

**Ends:** at Old Harlan's Forge, Ravensmoor, mid-investigation. The hatch is being watched nightly. Duskgate and the Greyhorn foothills/Ashpit both loom as the next major destinations, with a strong working theory that they may be connected or even identical.

## Session 7 — 2026-07-15

Planning session with Mara over what's needed next: real numbers on the fairground building and confirmation of what's underneath it. Mara proposed patient nightly counting plus digging into who legally owns the building; Darkhawk pushed straight for breaking into wherever deeds are filed. Agreed target: Ravensmoor's town hall records office off the market square — civil building, one night watchman, lock strength unknown. Split up: Mara went to scout the town hall's rounds and locks solo; Darkhawk headed to **Ostmark & Daughter**, the best smithy in town, to look at armor and get eyes on the swords.

Met smith **Greta Ostmark** — sharp, no-nonsense, immediately clocked him as the stranger who'd taken over Old Harlan's Forge. Negotiated a trade for a used, fitted breastplate (AC 15): first offer (100 gp + the ceremonial dagger) was lowballed and rejected outright (nat 1 Persuasion). Sweetened it by adding the back-harness shield; Greta accepted — **100 gp + ceremonial dagger + shield for the breastplate**, fitting to be finished the next day. Trade-off noted: Protection fighting style is now unusable until a new shield is acquired.

Greta examined both longswords, recognized Perrin Dask's rebalancing work as equal in skill to the sky-iron treatment itself, and offered free maintenance going forward as professional courtesy.

**Major unprompted lead:** Greta revealed she's unknowingly been supplying cage-grade hardware (shackle stock, hinges, two heavy sliding bolts) to an anonymous buyer for roughly two years — paid in cash via a silent ~12-year-old courier, orders scaling up over time. The most recent order implies **two separate holding cells/doors**, and the site is almost certainly the fairground building they're already watching. She also kept one payment coin bearing the Circuit's fist-and-chain sigil, stored in her strongbox.

Darkhawk told her the full truth — Ashpit, the betrayal, the campaign against the Circuit so far (Hollowmere, the Dead Ground, Bramhollow, the Western Yard, Vantor's capture), and the real purpose of the Ravensmoor cover. Greta, shaken to learn her honest work had gone toward what sounds like a holding pen, committed fully to cooperating.

Pressed Greta further and got the rest of what she had: a physical description of the silent courier boy (small, dark hair cut rough as if by someone else, oversized gray wool coat year-round, comes and goes on foot from the fairground district's direction, never speaks — which Greta now reads as trained silence, not shyness, and both she and Mara are committed to getting him somewhere safe once this is over) and the fist-and-chain coin itself from her strongbox, now carried as evidence.

**Struck a deal with Greta:** Darkhawk becomes her open "apprentice" — true enough to hold up (real forge time under Perrin, a real second forge needing hands) that it doubles as unbreakable cover for time spent near the fairground district. Bigger payoff: the anonymous buyer's next hardware order is due within the week, and Greta will let Darkhawk carry that delivery himself instead of her usual quiet handoff to the boy — a legitimate reason to walk goods toward wherever the courier's chain actually leads, possibly the building itself.

Met back up with Mara, who'd finished scouting the town hall records office solo: one lightly-attentive night watchman on a ~40-minute circuit, a cheap alley-side door the day clerks use. Agreed to split the work — Mara handles the town hall break-in that night (deed only, no unnecessary risk), Darkhawk returns to Greta the next day for the breastplate fitting and apprentice walkthrough, and both start real repair work on Old Harlan's Forge to keep the cover solid.

Spent the afternoon on genuine forge repairs (cracked bellows, rotted tool pegs, a dead hinge) — real work, not just cover dressing. That evening, ran the town hall job Bramhollow-style: Mara alone inside, Darkhawk on nearby backup (no outside lookout this time), a 30-minute trigger, and a taught owl-call signal (two soft hoots = clear, one sharp cry = trouble). Darkhawk kept watch with advantage on his Perception check (rolled 12, taking the higher of two d20s) and caught the "clear" signal on schedule. Mara emerged clean with the ownership ledger entry copied by hand.

**Major discovery:** the Ravensmoor fairground building's deed is held by **Ravenshollow** — the exact same broker firm quietly buying up old mine workings in the Greyhorn foothills near Thundertree territory. Same name behind both a "processing" site in Ravensmoor and unexplained land purchases in Darkhawk's ancestral mountains — strong evidence Ravenshollow isn't a front the Circuit rents, but a company the Warden's network actually owns.

Wrote the finding up that night and paid a discreet local rider to carry it to **Ressa Dane at Fallow Cross** for onward relay via Pell Ashworth to Halvard and Kell in Kessick's Ford — judged too important to sit on until the courier-delivery lead plays out.

Closed the night at Old Harlan's Forge — first full, unwatched rest either of them has had in some time, and the relationship with Mara deepened further, openly sharing the night together.

**Ends:** fully rested at Old Harlan's Forge, Ravensmoor, morning of the next day. Breastplate fitting and apprentice walkthrough with Greta scheduled for today. Open threads: the courier boy's next delivery (within the week — Darkhawk's way toward the building itself), the Ravenshollow connection now in transit to Halvard/Kell, the courier boy's eventual safety, Duskgate, and the Greyhorn foothills/Thundertree.

## Session 8 — 2026-07-16 onward

**Mara Fenn given a full character sheet** as an official party member (Ranger 5, Gloom Stalker) — backfilled to match Darkhawk's level, leveling alongside him going forward.

Breastplate fitting completed at Ostmark & Daughter; Greta walked Darkhawk through enough smithing basics to sell the apprentice cover to anyone but another smith. Handed over the repair list; Greta covered bellows bladder and hinge stock free as "apprentice's kit," and arranged for charcoal supplier **Old Tammas** to start including Old Harlan's Forge on his regular route past the fairground district — a second, mundane reason to be seen in that direction.

Spent the day on genuine forge repairs. That evening, visited the Hound's Rest to thank innkeeper **Sela Hollis** and mention the new business; asked carefully about the stranger who'd taken interest in them their first night in town. Sela (Persuasion 16, advantage from banked goodwill) revealed he's an infrequent visitor, pays in exact coin, never runs a tab — and once paid with a coin bearing the same fist-and-chain mark Darkhawk's already carrying two of, though she spent it into circulation and it's untraceable now. Darkhawk offered smithy work at a friends' discount in thanks; Sela accepted (hinge, cracked pot, sign chain).

Walked the district with Tammas on his charcoal route (bringing Mara along as "wife out to see the town"); a joint Perception check to spot anything new came back weak (8) — nothing further observed that day, but the route itself is now a standing, unsuspicious reason to walk that stretch regularly.

Delivered the repaired items to Sela for **8 gp**. Took Mara to bowyer **Harmon Fletch** under cover of her "provisioning stall" plans; traded her old longbow plus 117 gp for an exceptional yew longbow (advantage on Stealth checks to stay hidden after firing), after a failed charm attempt (Persuasion 3) but a successful pivot to a concrete trade offer — a standing arrowhead supply from the forge in exchange for 10% off and hunting-trade referrals both ways.

Spent several days on steady, honest income to offset the gold spent on the bow: forge commissions (+15 gp) and Mara's actual trapping/foraging trade (+12 gp). On the third evening, Greta's runner arrived — the anonymous buyer's courier was early. Darkhawk made the hardware delivery himself under the apprentice cover, confirming the courier's face firsthand and, via a Perception check with advantage (18), spotting a second, unwatched side door in an alley and a trained watcher near a fruit stall. A second advantage Perception check at the door itself (15) confirmed a bare corridor, a second iron-banded door matching Greta's hardware, and the sound of a chain shifting — the fairground building is an active holding site, not empty storage.

Debriefed with Mara and decided to trail the next supply wagon to its origin/destination rather than break in again. Two nights later, Mara cast **Pass without Trace** and the pair tailed a departing wagon (group Stealth 23) along the district's outer edge to a hidden stone waystation roughly half a mile out of town. Closer observation (Stealth 18, one natural 1 nearly given away by a loose stone but covered by the spell) and eavesdropping (Perception 18) confirmed: the waystation is a relay point on a route running Ravensmoor → this waystation → at least two more unconfirmed relay stops → **the old mine road in the Greyhorn foothills**; the Circuit has tightened security specifically because of Darkhawk's own campaign ("that mess at the Yard," "the Ashpit ghost"); and the next run was still a few days off, timed to "the count from Ravensmoor's side."

Updated **Vantor's Map** (the regional Circuit map) to add the confirmed waystation, the traced mine road, the suspected true hub at the old mine, and corrected the Western Yard's marker to show it as cleared rather than still-sealed/unidentified.

Spent the waiting days on more honest cover work and a rotated hatch/door watch, with no further sign of the tavern stranger. On the appointed night, watched the side door open for a real transport: **four bound captives** loaded under guard (driver, two flanking guards, and the same trained rearguard from the delivery) and moved out toward the waystation. Decided against hitting the now-lightly-guarded building — the captives themselves were the ones leaving — and ambushed the wagon directly instead.

**The ambush:** struck from full surprise at an alley mouth. Mara opened with Dread Ambusher plus Extra Attack (three hits, one a natural 20, 27 damage) and dropped the rearguard instantly. Darkhawk raged and swung at the driver (missed despite advantage) as the surprise round closed; round two saw Mara finish the driver (16 damage) and Darkhawk cut down a flank guard in one exchange (19 damage) with both blades. The last guard tried to surrender; Darkhawk killed him anyway. All four guards dead, no alarm raised. Searched the bodies (Investigation 7, rushed): 26 gp, nothing else of note. Bodies hidden in a nearby shuttered storage yard — not permanent concealment, but enough for now.

Freed and reassured the four captives under the wagon's canvas. Split the party deliberately: **Mara drove the wagon and the captives to Fallow Cross** to hand them to Ressa Dane, expecting 2-3 days round trip and covering her absence as a provisioning supply run. **Darkhawk stayed alone in Ravensmoor** to keep the forge and apprentice cover looking ordinary, aware the Circuit will eventually notice the missing wagon crew.

**Leveled up (milestone — disrupting an active transport and freeing four captives outright):** Darkhawk to **Barbarian 4** (Path of the Totem Warrior — Bear) **/ Fighter 2**, taking the **Dual Wielder** feat as his Ability Score Improvement (+1 AC while dual-wielding). Rolled +6 HP, bringing him to 52 max. Also corrected a long-standing tracking error: Rage uses should have been 3/day since Barbarian 3, not 2/day. Mara leveled to **Ranger 6**, gaining a second Natural Explorer terrain (**Mountain**, alongside Forest) with the Greyhorn foothills in mind; rolled +10 HP, bringing her to 45 max.

Also retroactively granted Darkhawk his first **Inspiration** (not tracked at all until now, despite several qualifying moments across the campaign) — available to spend on advantage for one future roll.

**Ends:** Darkhawk alone at Old Harlan's Forge, Ravensmoor, holding cover while Mara is en route to Fallow Cross with four rescued captives. Open threads: the Circuit noticing the missing wagon crew, Mara's return, the courier boy's safety, the two unconfirmed relay stops and the old mine itself, Duskgate, and the Greyhorn foothills/Thundertree.

## Session 9 — 2026-07-16/17

Darkhawk held cover alone for three days while Mara was en route: honest forge work, drumming up business around Ravensmoor (Persuasion 18 — new commissions and a merchant's deposit), a losing night of dice at the Hound's Rest for cover's sake, and continued watching. Sela Hollis passed on rumor of unusual foot traffic near the fairground building and a man asking around town. That night, a natural 20 Perception check caught the tavern stranger himself pressing a guard at the building's side door over the missing wagon crew — confirmed he works for the Circuit and that the crew's disappearance has been noticed, with real urgency behind it.

Trailed the stranger (Stealth 14, cashing in a banked one-time advantage from training months back) to a previously unknown **unmarked house** two streets from the fairground building — clearly a second Circuit-linked location in Ravensmoor.

Mara returned from Fallow Cross the same day: the four rescued captives are safe, being cared for, and the wagon and horses were left there rather than risk being recognized back in Ravensmoor. Reunited and briefed each other fully.

Scouted the unmarked house in daylight (joint Perception 20): shuttered windows, an unused front door, all real traffic through a side door, and one unshuttered second-floor window. Watched for the stranger over two more nights (both quiet) before he reappeared on the third. Ambushed him alone along his route out, isolated from the house — Mara's Dread Ambusher opening (three hits with advantage, 28 damage) dropped him in a single turn, non-lethal by choice, before he or Darkhawk even acted. Moved him back to the forge under Pass without Trace (Stealth 31).

Searched him thoroughly: disarmed (shortsword, hand crossbow, garrote, lockpicks), **34 gp**, and a **sealed letter from "W."** confirming the Warden is personally alarmed by the missing crew, ordering a direct report (not through Bramhollow), and stating the count at the mine hub is being **moved up regardless of season** because of Darkhawk's own campaign.

**Interrogation:** Darkhawk told him plainly he intended to kill him once questioning was done. The stranger, given that honesty, negotiated: safety for a woman living at the house (debt-bonded, not Circuit by choice) in exchange for full cooperation. Darkhawk refused to harm an innocent regardless. The stranger gave up the house's real layout, confirmed he reports to the Warden via a **dead-drop well** behind the house (never sees who collects it), and confirmed the mine timeline acceleration. When offered a chance to turn king's evidence rather than be killed (Vantor precedent), he took it — citing that he's always been disposable to the Warden despite years of service. Also gave the fairground building's real internal layout: side door and corridor already known, two iron-banded holding cells, a handler's office with the count ledger, 2-3 staff on rotation, no real garrison, roughly four captives held as of his last information.

Transported him to Fallow Cross that night, cargo hidden under a rented cart's worth of legitimate smithy goods (cover check 11 — clean). Handed him to Ressa Dane, who's arranging for Magistrate Kell to come to Fallow Cross directly rather than risk moving him twice. Returned to Ravensmoor the same day to keep the cover from lapsing.

**Freed Wren**, the woman at the unmarked house — Mara made first contact (Persuasion 9, took real patience to earn trust), then Darkhawk approached deliberately unthreatening (kneeling, self-deprecating humor) and simply let her talk without pressing for information. She confirmed the dead drop's been in steady use for at least two years, multiple different men, never repeating. **Wren has joined the household at Old Harlan's Forge** as housekeeper — genuinely free, paid, with her own say — deepening the cover further. Mara searched the house and the (currently empty) dead drop afterward (Investigation 10) — nothing beyond what was already known.

**Planned the assault on the fairground building** using the captured intel: stealth approach at night to the known side door, Darkhawk leads loud once inside with Mara backing at range, secure the ledger and evidence before anything else, free whoever's held in the two cells, move them to Wren's now-vacant house afterward. Refined further into a cover story: after the guards are down and evidence secured, set a fire through where the fighting happened, then have Darkhawk "discover" it publicly and be seen carrying people out — the freed captives' honest account (guards resisted the rescue) doubles as the story that explains the guards' sword and arrow wounds without implicating "the Blondins" as the ones who broke in. Mara flagged plainly that this likely ends their undercover time in Ravensmoor regardless of how clean the story holds, given the building won't stay secret after tonight.

**Ends:** at Old Harlan's Forge, plan locked and ready to execute — the fairground building raid queued up for the next night.

## Session 10 — 2026-07-17/18

Mara cast **Pass without Trace** and the pair slipped in through the known side door (Stealth 26 and 25 — clean). A joint check (advantage, Darkhawk helping) confirmed the handler's office door was untrapped; inside, an unoccupied office held the count ledger on the desk and a bolted strongbox. A second check (advantage, nat 20) found the strongbox untrapped and turned up a hidden key rather than needing force — inside, **49 gp**, a pay roster, and a second handler's ledger, all taken.

Moved into the main hall (Stealth 19 and 23) and spotted the single roving guard on patrol plus firelight/voices from the staff room (2 more inside). Took cover behind crates near a support post. **Darkhawk charged and killed the roving guard in one exchange** (Reckless Attack, both blades hit, 17 damage — guard never got a sound out). Then baited the staff room by shouting for help; both guards burst out unarmed and straight into **Mara's readied triple shot** (Dread Ambusher + Extra Attack, all three hits, 24 total damage) — both dead before either could react.

Searched the three bodies (Investigation, advantage, nat 20): **17 gp** and a ring of cell keys. A follow-up Perception check (nat-adjacent, 20) on the pay roster caught a fourth name crossed out — reassigned to the mine road "effective tonight," confirming the Warden really is thinning every post to feed the mine's accelerated count.

Unlocked both cells: **four more captives**, including the previously unidentified **courier boy** (still silent, but visibly relieved to recognize Darkhawk). Freed them, laid out the extraction/cover plan, and sent Mara ahead with all four under Pass without Trace to shelter at Wren's now-vacant house, while Darkhawk stayed to stage the fire.

Slipped back to the forge alone (Stealth 19) to hide the ledger, second ledger, roster, and coin under a loose floorboard near the ash pit. On the return trip (Stealth 7) narrowly avoided being spotted and instead spotted a **fifth Circuit man** approaching the building's side door — likely the roster's replacement guard. Followed him in (Stealth 7, undetected) and killed him from behind the moment he discovered the bodies (Athletics 14 to keep him silent, Reckless Attack hit for 12 damage) — four dead total, no alarm ever raised.

Staged the fire in the main hall with crate wood and oil, then waited for Mara's return with a genuine town watchman in tow (fetched during her ~10-minute round trip). Burst out coughing and bloodied, screaming "fire," and sold the cover story on instinct and half a name ("Dar—") rather than a rehearsed line. The watchman organized a bucket brigade; Corlin Vey and Sela Hollis both arrived on the scene. **Brought the four captives back into the same chaos** to give their account to the sergeant while attention was thin — locking in the cover story before anyone had time to compare notes. Helped work the bucket line afterward to sell the "ordinary bystander" role to the end.

**Leveled up (milestone — the Ravensmoor fairground hub disrupted outright, four more captives freed, all on-site Circuit killed, cover story planted and corroborated):** Darkhawk to **Barbarian 5** (Path of the Totem Warrior — Bear) **/ Fighter 2**, gaining **Extra Attack** (three attacks per full turn dual-wielding) and **Fast Movement** (+10 ft speed, now 40 ft). Rolled +10 HP (1d12+2 CON), bringing him to 64 max.

**Ends:** dawn breaking over a gutted fairground building, the Ravensmoor undercover operation effectively concluded — evidence secured, captives free, cover story hardened into the town's own account of the night. Open threads: the two unconfirmed relay stops and the old mine itself, the courier boy's longer-term safety, Duskgate, and the Greyhorn foothills/Thundertree.

## Session 11 — 2026-07-18 to ~2026-07-21

**Ravensmoor wind-down:** A few quiet days let the town settle — "Dar" became local legend, Corlin Vey stayed nervous but grateful, the watch's inquiry closed itself. The four cell captives, including the still-silent courier boy, recovered at Wren's house. One evening a Perception check (21) caught a watcher in the alley behind the forge who vanished before Darkhawk could close the distance — first sign the Warden's "personal interest" might be more than a letter's turn of phrase. A courier from Fallow Cross then arrived with a sealed letter from Halvard.

**Halvard's letter:** confirmed Ravenshollow as a genuine six-year-old brokerage laundering land purchases skirting Thundertree's border, newest parcel closed 11 days prior — deepest into the foothills of any of them. Magistrate Kell was at Fallow Cross in person for the tavern stranger's formal statement and wanted to brief the party directly.

**Leaving Ravensmoor:** Darkhawk decided the Blondin cover was spent. Greta Ostmark took over Old Harlan's Forge's lease outright. Wren stayed in Ravensmoor for good (helping keep books for both forges); the older captive took honest work in town; one of the two women headed south to family (given **12 gp** for the road); the other stayed with Wren. The courier boy chose to follow the party rather than stay, and was given a real task — watching the yard at Fallow Cross for Ressa Dane — rather than just being left behind.

**Fallow Cross council:** reunited with Kell and a recovering Kaldur. Group cross-referenced the fairground's second ledger/roster against Halvard's Ravenshollow trace (Investigation, double natural 1 — the ledger turned out to be numeric shorthand nobody present could crack; earmarked for Corwin Pell at Kessick's Ford). Updated **Vantor's Map** with Ravensmoor marked resolved and Ravenshollow's plotted parcels, newest one circled as the likely target. Debated Duskgate as a diversion; Kell, Halvard, and Kaldur all argued against it (unconfirmed value, deliberately deferred for good reason) in favor of a systematic plan: take the waystation, then both relay stops in a synchronized pincer, garrison behind as they go, then Kaldur guides Darkhawk and Mara to Thundertree while the choke holds. Kell offered deputies, Halvard offered House Reeve retainers.

A quiet walk with Mara the night before departure surfaced a deeper thread: Darkhawk voiced a growing fear that the Warden is deliberately testing him the way the ring once did, that the pattern of only facing disposable "grunts" so far may be deliberate, and that Ashpit — his own point of origin — may be exactly where this is leading, converging with the Thundertree lead. Mara didn't dismiss it, and both openly said "I love you" for the first time. Darkhawk also named a fear of a future choice he already knows the answer to; Mara asked only to be there for it rather than have it decided for her in advance.

**The waystation:** advance scouts confirmed reduced staff (3, down from 4-5) and no wagon traffic — Ravensmoor's fall already disrupting the schedule. Darkhawk walked out alone in plain sight to lean into the "Ashpit Ghost" reputation (Intimidation 22) while Mara covered from the treeline and Kaldur cut off the one runner. The senior guard, **Rennick**, broke and fully surrendered along with the other two. Interrogated at length — Rennick asked for redemption and got it: turned along with his two men, now reporting to Fenris Cray (House Reeve retainer lead), holding the waystation as an allied garrison. Search of the post: **44 gp**, no written intelligence (operation stays compartmentalized even at this level).

**The dead drop:** Rennick guided Darkhawk and Mara two miles up the relay road to the dead-drop stone. A fresh, uncollected sealed letter was found (Investigation 22) — orders from the Warden halting all traffic until "the count concludes... within the fortnight," instructing posts to fall back rather than engage anyone unrecognized, and stating plainly he'd accept losing every post before losing his own silence. Lying in wait under Pass without Trace, the pair grabbed the dawn courier alive (Athletics 16), confirmed he only ever carries forward to the first relay stop and never returns anything, then handed him to deputy Torvin for escort back to the waystation. The drop was left looking undisturbed.

**Pincer plan executed:** Rennick led a second team (Corran plus 4 retainers/1 deputy) on a wide loop toward the smaller second relay stop while Darkhawk and Mara took the direct road to the larger first stop, staggered a day apart to land together. At the first stop, the pair looped a further hour around to attack from the mine-side path instead of the road side — Darkhawk raged and killed the gate watchman in a single exchange (Reckless Attack, triple hit, 34 damage), then a second guard who charged rather than surrendered fell to Mara (a crit finishing him). The last two surrendered outright under a deliberately terrifying show (Intimidation 20) and gave up full intel: the second stop's real headcount (3-4, lax lookout, no real defenses) and confirmation the mine sits roughly two days past the second stop, above the tree line. Search turned up **15 gp**. The two survivors were bound and locked in a storeroom.

**A close call:** immediately after the kill, Darkhawk failed a self-control check (Wisdom save, DC 13) and very nearly turned on the two surrendered guards before Mara's voice broke through the rage — the first loss of control like it since the fighting ring. A quiet, heavy conversation followed on the road afterward: Darkhawk named his fear that proximity to Ashpit is dragging the old rage back out of him, and that the theory about the Warden testing him feels more true the longer this goes. Mara didn't try to talk him out of the fear, only committed to watching for it with him.

Arrived at the second relay stop to find Rennick's team had already taken it clean — three dead, one alive and talking, Corran lightly wounded, no warning reaching further up the line. Both nodes secured the same day; the mine's supply line is now fully cut, blind to everything that's happened since Ravensmoor.

**Leveled up twice (milestone, catching up a missed level from the fairground raid plus this one — the mine's entire supply network disrupted, two relay stops secured, three more Circuit men turned rather than killed):**
- Darkhawk to **Barbarian 5** then **Barbarian 6** (Path of the Totem Warrior — Bear) **/ Fighter 2** — gained Extra Attack, Fast Movement (40 ft speed), Rage 4/day, and Aspect of the Beast (Bear). Rolled +10 then +11 HP, now **75 max**.
- Mara to **Ranger 7** then **Ranger 8** (Gloom Stalker) — gained Iron Mind (Wisdom save proficiency), a new spell known (**Silence**), an Ability Score Improvement (DEX 18→20), and Land's Stride. Rolled +6 then +7 HP, now **58 max**.

**Ends:** at the second relay stop, both nodes held and garrisoned, the road to Thundertree and the mine open ahead. Open threads: the coded second ledger (bound for Corwin Pell), Duskgate untouched, the courier boy settling in at Fallow Cross, and the still-unanswered question of what's waiting — or watching — at Ashpit.

## Session 12 — 2026-07-22 to ~2026-07-25

A long rest at the second relay stop, fully secured and garrisoned overnight — both fully restored (Darkhawk's rages back to 4/4, Mara's spell slots to 4/4 and 3/3). Backtracked three easy days through both relay stops and the waystation to collect **Kaldur Stonewake**, then set out together beyond the secured network into unmapped ground toward Thundertree, Kaldur leading and sharing pieces of Goliath culture and his own exile from Ninebells along the way.

Three days into the climb, a sharp passive read (21) caught the first sign of Thundertree's border watch — deliberately visible once they'd decided the party was worth a proper meeting rather than an arrow. Four sentries met them at a ridge shelf; Darkhawk stepped forward alone, face to face with the lead sentry, who recognized the carving on his lifelong trophy on sight and asked where he'd gotten it. Told plainly — *stolen from his parents as a child* — she went pale, and revealed a family at Thundertree still asking after a lost son every season. She personally escorted them the rest of the way in.

**Darkhawk's birth name and family, confirmed at last:** he is **Rurik**, son of **Sesha** and **Harek**, taken in a slaver raid on an outlying hunting camp roughly a Goliath five winters into his life — the same raid that killed two elders and a hunter who tried to stop it. He has a younger sister, **Thyra**, born after the raid, currently out on a hunting circuit and due back within days, who has only ever known him as family legend. The reunion was raw and unguarded on all sides — Darkhawk told the full story of his life, from the ring through Ravensmoor to the letter naming him specifically, with Mara filling in her own side of it; introduced Mara to his parents plainly as the one who saved him "not just my life but myself," and both parents welcomed her as family in turn.

Thundertree itself has independently noticed the Circuit's pressure at its borders — land purchases, strange traffic through passes that used to be empty — and Elder Vosk, who speaks for the clan, has reportedly been watching the mountain with real worry for some time. Harek left to request an audience with Vosk, ideally the same day, given the urgency Darkhawk laid out. Sesha promised Kaldur real hospitality and open acknowledgment of what he did to guide Darkhawk home.

**Ends:** resting by the hearth in Sesha and Harek's longhouse, name and family reclaimed, Mara at his side, the meeting with Elder Vosk — and the alliance the whole relay-stop campaign was built toward — still waiting for tomorrow. The fortnight deadline from the Warden's letter keeps ticking in the background, unaddressed but not forgotten.

## Session 13 — 2026-07-26

Woke rested after the first full, peaceful night's sleep in longer than either could remember. Breakfast with Sesha and Harek, then the audience with **Elder Vosk**, who confirmed Thundertree had already noticed Ravenshollow's land purchases and unusual mountain traffic independently. Darkhawk asked for Thundertree's help sealing the mountain paths against anyone fleeing the mine once he moved against it. Vosk pressed for real details — force size, timeline, expected numbers — and was told plainly: a 3-4 day prep window inside the Warden's fortnight deadline, the waystation and both relay stops already cutting supply, and no scouted numbers yet at the mine itself since Darkhawk came straight to Thundertree first.

**Terms agreed:** Vosk sends her own scouts to the mine within the hour (report expected within a day, two at most) and begins quietly positioning warriors along the likely escape paths without engaging. In return, Darkhawk and Mara owe a clear, sustained white-smoke signal from a ridge near the mine's tree line (Kaldur to help pick the exact spot) the moment the mine assault begins, so Thundertree's warriors know when to seal the paths — and Darkhawk's word that if the scouts report something worse than expected, the plan waits rather than proceeds regardless. Vosk retains final say on whether/when the paths close.

**Family time:** breakfast conversation, sparring tips and life advice from Harek (footing over speed, rage as "a tool, not a home," patience), and word that **Thyra** (Darkhawk's sister, out on a hunting circuit) is expected back within two to three days — potentially before the mine assault even begins.

**The Long Climb:** with only days before the mine and a season's usual preparation compressed to none, Darkhawk asked for and was granted an urgent blessing from Vosk to undertake Thundertree's coming-of-age rite — a solo climb to the **Shoulderstone**, above the tree line, no weapon or guide past the boundary stones. Handed his swords to Mara beforehand. The climb humbled him early — old ring instincts (speed over presence) nearly cost him a bad fall on a scree slope (Survival 9, Athletics recovery 12) — but slowing down and actually attending to the mountain paid off immediately after (Survival 18), including a quiet, wordless encounter with old bear sign that landed as real kinship rather than coincidence, fitting his Totem Warrior spirit. The final pitch to the Shoulderstone itself was the hardest stretch, no strength left to give (Athletics, natural 1) — resolved entirely through roleplay rather than a mechanical fix: choosing the bear's patient strength over rage or panic to finish the climb. At the summit, a wordless, wind-and-light acceptance from the mountain itself, followed by a full, raw release — grief, rage, and doubt all poured into a single roar that genuinely seemed to answer back across the range. Descended clean (24) and whole, formally accepted as Thundertree "not just by blood." Named the resolution plainly to the family afterward: *"I am Darkhawk and I am Rurik. I accept my past for shaping me, and respect the future to make me better than I am now."*

The evening that followed was unhurried and ordinary in the best way — a Thundertree dice game, old hunting stories traded between Harek and Kaldur, childhood stories from Sesha that Darkhawk has no memory of but takes as gifts rather than losses. Stepped outside alone before bed to actually look at the mountain properly for the first time, at peace with what's still ahead of it rather than afraid of it.

**Ends:** asleep at the longhouse, arm around Mara, the identity thread that's run since the very start of the campaign now fully resolved. Open threads unchanged otherwise: Vosk's scout report still pending, Thyra not yet met, the coded ledger still bound for Corwin Pell, and the fortnight clock still running on the mine.

## Session 14 — 2026-07-27 to ~2026-07-30

Early morning footwork practice with Harek turned into a friendly sparring match — Darkhawk won two of three falls applying the mountain's lesson about patient footing over speed, much to Harek's delighted, exaggerated dismay, and Sesha's fond, unimpressed commentary from the porch. First "Dad" landed quietly in the middle of it.

**Vosk's scout report:** the mine's garrison is real and dangerous — at least 20+ trained, disciplined soldiers (not the disposable men faced everywhere else), a fortified entrance flanked by two watch towers, and a separate holding area with a significant number of captives. Critically, one of Vosk's scouts recognized old fighting-pit structures cut into the same mountainside — strong confirmation that **Ashpit and the mine are the same site.** A single unnamed commander was spotted directing the garrison — better-armored than the rank and file, but almost certainly a lieutenant rather than the Warden himself, given the Warden's established pattern of never being seen even by trusted operatives. Three exploitable weaknesses identified: a lightly-watched **north face service cut** (bypasses the front entirely, narrow enough to limit defenders to one or two at a time), a **blind spot** between the two watch towers' sightlines, and a **thinner garrison during the midday shift change** (~20 minutes).

**The plan, worked out with Vosk:** a frontal feint of 25 Thundertree warriors led by Kaldur, spread wide and using banners/noise/constant movement to read as a larger force, timed to strike the main entrance at the exact same moment Darkhawk and Mara hit the north service cut alone — splitting the garrison's attention so neither door gets a clean, uncontested response. If the garrison sallies out after the feint line, Kaldur draws them into a natural defile two hundred yards out and surrounds pursuers, offering surrender first. Vosk commits to sending scouts to watch for a sustained white-smoke signal (lit the moment either strike begins) as the cue for Thundertree warriors already positioned to seal the mountain's escape paths — she retains final say on timing from her end. Target: the midday shift change, within the agreed 3-4 day window.

Marched down to the second relay stop to brief Fenris, Rennick, and Corran on the full plan; all agreed to hold the relay network as the last line behind everything else. Darkhawk and Mara then made the two-day approach alone (Survival 17, clean) to the mine's north face while Kaldur's column took the more visible route to the front, timed to arrive separately and strike together.

**The assault, opening exchange (combat paused mid-fight):** Darkhawk and Mara approached the service cut openly, swords bare, deliberately not hiding. The lone watchman raised a full alarm before Mara's opening shots (20 dmg) dropped him just as Darkhawk's rage-fueled follow-through (Reckless Attack) finished him. Three reinforcements arrived at a run; Mara dropped the lead one outright (24 dmg), Darkhawk's roar (Intimidation 18) visibly rattled the other two, and between a follow-up charge (17 dmg) and a finishing shot from Mara, a second reinforcement died. The third broke and fled toward the mine's interior; Darkhawk pursued to the edge of an open space beyond the tunnel bend but deliberately broke off rather than charge into unknown, unsupported ground — a real tactical restraint given the danger of the full garrison. The fleeing guard escaped and raised a wider alarm. A disciplined four-man response team, shields locked, advanced into the tunnel — nothing like the men faced so far, and Mara's opening volley against them missed clean for the first time this campaign (both shots deflected).

**The service-cut chokepoint (finished):** the four-guard shield team turned out to be real resistance — first exchange was a scoreless standoff, shields holding through everything thrown at them. Darkhawk broke the deadlock by kicking a guard's own shield back into the man behind him (Athletics 23 vs. 11), dropping both prone and opening a clean kill; ripped a second guard's shield away bare-handed the same way shortly after. All four eventually killed, bringing the service-cut total to **seven guards dead**. Searched the bodies with Mara's help: 23 gp, plus a rough charcoal sketch of the mine's interior and a knotted tally cord matching the Warden's own phrase, "the count."

**The holding area:** Pass without Trace plus a clean stealth approach let Darkhawk and Mara take both guards there without a fight ever really starting — one dropped in the surprise round, the second finished a beat later. Five captives freed; three (including two injured) chose to leave immediately via the now-clear service cut, and two — Joss and Idra — armed themselves with the dead guards' gear and asked to fight rather than run.

**The commander's post:** scouted undetected, revealing the unnamed mine commander and three guards. Rather than a straight fight, Darkhawk vaulted clean over the shield wall using the guards' own raised shields as a springboard (Athletics 15) to land behind them — splitting the fight so Mara had the exposed backs and Darkhawk had the guards now turned to face him. The commander went down to a devastating opening flurry (Rage + Reckless + Action Surge, 4 of 5 hits, 43 damage) followed by a natural-20 finishing shot from Mara. All three guards fell shortly after; the last, badly outnumbered, surrendered on Darkhawk's demand rather than fight to the end and was bound and held rather than killed. Searched the commander's body and the command table: 25 gp, a rank clasp, an unsent letter confirming a separate figure called **"the Warden's man"** stationed deeper in the mine, a full interior map (barracks, a processing area, and the Warden's man's chamber), troop rosters, and — buried in a manifest on the table — the real meaning of "the count": not the mine's own prisoners, but a scheduled wholesale handoff of people being moved *through* the mine, timed to close within the Warden's fortnight deadline. Interrogated the captured guard (G3), who confirmed the barracks (7-8 off-shift), the lightly-staffed processing area, and that the Warden's man was feared even by the garrison itself. G3 was sent back to the holding area's empty cells under guard by Joss and Idra rather than executed. Short rest afterward — Second Wind and Action Surge refreshed, 2 Hit Dice spent (11 HP healed).

**The barracks:** eight guards, six of them actually asleep, found via a clean scouted approach (Pass without Trace recast). A coordinated ambush — Darkhawk and Mara on the two awake guards, Joss and Idra finishing sleeping ones at melee range for automatic crits — killed three outright in the opening exchange and left the rest too wounded to matter; all eight guards confirmed dead by the end, none of them ever got to fight back. Searched the room and bodies: 38 gp and a shift schedule confirming the processing area's numbers and the same "count closes tonight" note.

**The processing area:** two unarmed handlers killed on approach (a third was never found). Chained captives — six of them — freed; five went out via the cleared route, and a sixth, Corren, stayed to fight, bringing the volunteer count to three. Searching the room with Mara turned up a coded shipment ledger matching the one already headed to Corwin Pell, and 12 gp in a cash box.

**The Warden's man:** revealed by Mara's Primeval Awareness to be an actual fiend, the source of the wrongness in the air and the light under the chamber door. Darkhawk and Mara hit it in a devastating surprise-round-plus-fresh-round combo — Rage, Reckless, Action Surge, and Mara's Hunter's Mark/Dread Ambusher combination did over 90 damage across two rounds before Mara's second attack (a natural 20) finished it; the body dissolved to ash on death, confirming it was never a human intermediary at all. The chamber search turned up a pact contract countersigned "on behalf of Ravenshollow" (direct proof the broker firm is the Warden's own instrument, not just a front), a black scrying mirror (the fiend's reporting method, untested), and a genuine **+1 dagger** that resonates near spoken lies — the campaign's first real magic item.

**The front entrance:** with the mine's interior fully quiet, the party moved back through it and came out behind the front defenders — exactly where Kaldur's 25-warrior feint was grinding against a thinner-than-expected garrison. The flank attack from an unexpected direction broke the defenders' formation outright; between that and Kaldur's push, the last 6-7 guards fell in short order, Darkhawk personally finishing the two he engaged (one via a last-rage reckless charge specifically to pull a guard's attention off Mara). Reunited with Kaldur, bloodied but standing. **Thundertree losses: three dead** (Ren Alderhawk and both of Yorik Stonebrand's sons) and 8-9 wounded but expected to recover. Darkhawk chose to carry the news to the fallen's families himself, as Rurik rather than Darkhawk.

**Milestone level-up (twice):** clearing the barracks/commander's post, and killing the Warden's man while breaking the entrance line.
- Darkhawk to **Barbarian 7** then **Barbarian 8** — gained Feral Instinct and an Ability Score Improvement (+2 STR, 19→21). Rolled +12 then +8 HP, now **95 max**.
- Mara to **Ranger 9** then **Ranger 10** — gained 3rd-level spell slots, learned **Conjure Animals**, and gained Hide in Plain Sight. Rolled +4 then +7 HP, now **69 max**.

**Ends:** the mine entirely cleared — every guard, the commander, and the Warden's man himself dead, one prisoner held, 22 captives freed across the session, three new volunteer allies, three Thundertree dead honored by name, and the campaign's single strongest lead yet on the Warden's identity sitting in a saddlebag: a signed pact naming Ravenshollow as his own instrument. Open threads: two coded ledgers still bound for Corwin Pell, the untried scrying mirror, whether the Warden's deadline still means anything with the mine gone, and Thyra still not yet met back at Thundertree.

## Session 15 — 2026-07-30/31

A long rest at the mine's entrance among Kaldur's warriors — resources already full from the level-up, but a real, quiet night regardless, the first since the assault. Come morning, Kaldur put Thundertree warriors and the freed volunteers (Joss, Idra, Corren) to work searching the mine top to bottom for anything missed in the fighting: **54 gp** scattered across personal effects, spare arms and armor left for Thundertree to use, an unsent guard's letter home, and enough supplies to resupply the trip down.

**The original Ashpit, found:** the scout who'd first recognized old fighting-pit stonework from outside led Kaldur to a deliberately sealed section behind a placed (not natural) rockfall. Darkhawk asked Mara to come with him; Kaldur held the entrance and let them go in alone. Inside: a genuine ancient arena — tiered seating, rusted cages, and walls scored floor to ceiling with names going back further than anyone could guess.

Searching for his own name turned up nothing — because he never had one there. His cage bore no name at all, only a tally of wins and a single carved numeral: **7.** The tally simply stops at his count; no one else was ever assigned the number afterward, and the section was sealed not long after — deliberately, not from age. Touching the tally triggered a full, visceral memory of the betrayal itself: refusing to finish a beaten opponent, the crowd's silence, being overwhelmed by hands and steel — and a face at the very edge of it that slipped away before it resolved, same as his childhood memories once did. Who betrayed him remains unknown.

A search of the old handler's alcove (Mara's Investigation, then Darkhawk's own instinctive pull to a loose floor stone, aided by Mara — advantage, 21) turned up a hidden cache: aged fist-and-chain coin (23 gp, a cruder/older minting than any seen since) and a water-damaged ledger fragment recording old match outcomes. Every surviving page is headed the same way, in different hands across the years: **"By the Warden's word."** Never a name — strong evidence the title is inherited, an office passed down rather than a person, and that this pit is where it began.

Darkhawk also showed Mara the old scar clean through his chest — a wound, by her read, no one survives untreated. Someone saved him after the pit believed him dead; who and how remains unknown. Standing in the pit one last time, he told it plainly it no longer has power over him, took everything worth taking, and walked out with Mara at his side, the section resealed behind them.

**Telling the families:** returning to Thundertree with Kaldur's column and its dead, Darkhawk (as Rurik) personally told both grieving families how their kin fell, with Kaldur giving the honest account of the entrance fight. **Yorik Stonebrand** (father of Bram and Torin, both killed holding the shield line together) gave Rurik a mourning-stone to carry into future battles. **Vessa Alderhawk**, Ren's widow, asked only that her husband be remembered as a friend and not just a name in a line, for the sake of their young daughter, **Kessa**.

Alone afterward to sit with the weight of command and loss for the first time in his life, Darkhawk found his own way back to his parents' longhouse rather than staying solitary with it — met there by Sesha, Harek, Mara, and Kaldur, and given a long rest surrounded by family.

**The dream:** a fitful sleep pushed past the memory-wall around his rescue for the first time — the blade going through, then (unlike ever before) *past* that point: large, fur-covered hands working urgently at the wound, a low not-quite-growl, chanted words in no language he could hold onto. Woke shaken; the details faded fast despite trying to hold them (Wisdom check, 10 — most of the specifics slipped away, only the sensory fragments stuck).

**Elder Vosk and the ritual:** sought out before dawn, Vosk offered old mountain-clan lore of a bear-spirit said to mark those who show mercy where the pit or the raid demands blood — restraint at the moment restraint should be impossible, matching Darkhawk's own refusal to finish a beaten man right before he was struck down. She guided a Spirit Seeker ritual (Speak with Animals, cast the old way) under the open sky. With Vosk's direct guidance (advantage), the ritual drew a real bear to the firelight's edge — no words exchanged, but an unmistakable impression: recognition, an old debt remembered rather than explained. It didn't linger. Whether it's the same presence that saved him at Ashpit is unconfirmed, but Darkhawk now believes the totem may have chosen him well before he consciously chose it back at Barbarian 3.

**The proposal:** walking with Mara as dawn broke, Darkhawk asked her to marry him — an immediate, unhesitating yes. He then told her the harder thing: he means to face the Warden alone when that day comes, not to protect her, but because it's a reckoning only he can answer, tied directly to the bear-spirit's message that he was saved for refusing to be the pit's killer, not for being its champion. Mara pushed back hard at first, until she connected it to the fear he'd voiced weeks earlier on the road ("a choice I already know the answer to") — she'd asked then only to be present when it came, and held him to exactly that: everything up to the Warden they do together, but she won't be shut out of the field when the fight itself is his alone. Terms accepted on both sides. Told Sesha and Harek together; both welcomed Mara as true daughter-in-law on the spot, wedding deferred until the Warden is dealt with.

**Ends:** Thundertree, morning after the mine's fall, engaged and resolved on the road ahead. Open threads unchanged from Session 14 (two coded ledgers, the untried scrying mirror, the Warden's deadline, Thyra not yet met), plus new ones: who saved Darkhawk at Ashpit, whether the bear-spirit and that rescuer are the same, and the near face at the edge of the betrayal memory that still won't resolve.

Before departing, Thyra was finally met — back a day early from her hunting circuit, overjoyed and full of questions, walked and answered at length on the ridge above the village. Farewells followed with the whole family, Vosk, and (at a distance) Yorik and Vessa, before heading back down to the mine to plan the next move with Kaldur.

**Planning session at the mine:** laid out every outstanding thread — the two coded ledgers, the Ravenshollow land-parcel lead, the untried scrying mirror, Duskgate, and the Warden's fortnight deadline. Mara argued against walking into Duskgate blind and pushed instead for scouting the newest Ravenshollow parcel first, given its proximity to Thundertree and how fresh the lead was; agreed. The ledgers were sent by trusted Thundertree runner down through the secured relay network to Fallow Cross, onward to Corwin Pell.

**Scouting the parcel:** a two-day approach with Kaldur and two Thundertree scouts found the parcel tucked into a fold in the foothills — a raw-timbered "storehouse" still being framed and a finished, shuttered "strongroom" built with the same cage-grade ironwork Greta Ostmark once unknowingly supplied to Ravensmoor, currently empty. Mara's Pass without Trace let Darkhawk and Mara close the distance undetected (advantage rolls, 19 and 24) for a clear look: four unarmed laborers, two armed watchers, no sign yet of captives or a functioning operation.

**Talking the guards down:** Darkhawk walked in openly, swords sheathed, and demanded surrender outright (Intimidation 17) — the crossbow guard, **Pellin Marsh**, broke almost immediately; his partner, **Aldous Rook**, held out briefly before standing down as well once the math of the situation became clear. Both were questioned by Kaldur and agreed to keep working their normal rotation to preserve the stakeout. The four laborers, questioned separately, confirmed they're ordinary hired hands who know little beyond the job — but revealed a site foreman named **Merrick** visits every 8-9 days, and that "the owner" himself was due to inspect the site in person once the storehouse frame finished, four to five days out.

**The stakeout:** the party held a hidden camp for five days (Survival 14, one close call with a passing rider avoided cleanly) while the guards kept up appearances. On the fifth morning, the owner arrived ahead of schedule.

**The confrontation:** the party moved in openly. The owner — plainly dressed, unarmed, flanked by two quiet personal guards who stood down on his own signal — recognized Darkhawk immediately by both names, "Rurik of Thundertree" and "Darkhawk Blondin," and engaged in direct conversation rather than fighting or fleeing. Under sustained, calm pressure, he confirmed the central truths of the whole campaign: the title "the Warden" is inherited, sustained by a genuine infernal bargain first struck at **Duskgate** and renewed there each time a warden dies or steps aside; the actual currency has always been **souls**, with people, spectacle, and profit serving only as the vessel; the devil on the other end of the bargain is known only as **Malphestor, the Reckoner** — itself almost certainly a title rather than a true name. Darkhawk, in turn, told him plainly that ending the idea rather than the man is the only way this actually ends — an insight the owner admitted he hadn't expected and clearly hadn't planned for.

Rather than kill him, Darkhawk had Kaldur and the scouts restrain him alongside Merrick and his two guards. A full search turned up his real name — **Edmund Vale** — via a signet ring (fist-and-chain, worn smooth from more than one owner) and a personal document case, including an unsent letter to "my successor" preparing for exactly this day. Merrick's tent yielded a preliminary manifest: 12-15 captives expected once the strongroom went operational, timed to begin right after the mine's own numbers would have closed — confirming this site was built as the mine's direct replacement. 62 gp taken off Vale and his guards.

Escorted the prisoners back to the Bear Cave (see below) and called the full party together — Kaldur, the two scouts, Joss, Idra, Corren — to lay out the truth: souls, not people, are the real currency, and Duskgate is the only place the bargain can actually be ended. Rather than rush Duskgate blind, the group committed to a consolidation phase: Kaldur to work Vosk and the wider mountain clans for reinforcements and hold the base; Darkhawk and Mara to personally deliver Edmund Vale and the evidence to Magistrate Kell at Kessick's Ford; a rider sent ahead to Greta Ostmark in Ravensmoor, with Perrin Dask to be consulted once at Kessick's Ford; the mine's spare arms and armor distributed to Joss, Idra, Corren, and other allies in the meantime. Everyone at the fire — given an open, genuine choice to walk away — chose to stay for the long haul.

Darkhawk renamed the mine on the spot: no longer Ashpit, now **the Bear Cave** — Thundertree's own name for the site going forward.

**Leveled up again (milestone — Edmund Vale captured alive, Duskgate confirmed as the true target, the Ashpit/identity arc closed):** Darkhawk to **Fighter 3**, choosing **Battle Master** (Trip Attack, Disarming Attack, Riposte), rolling +9 HP, now **104 max**. Mara to **Ranger 11** (Gloom Stalker), gaining Stalker's Flurry, a third 3rd-level spell slot, and learning **Protection from Energy**, rolling +7 HP, now **76 max**.

Before departing, sent **Joss** ahead to Ravensmoor to brief Greta Ostmark on the situation in person rather than by letter alone — he volunteered readily, given how close he came to being one of the strongroom's first "vessels."

**To Kessick's Ford:** escorted Vale personally (bound, hooded, guarded by two Thundertree warriors) down the secured relay chain — both relay stops, the waystation, Fallow Cross — arriving to a town still unaware how close the campaign's central mystery had come to resolving. Went straight to Magistrate Kell, laid out Vale and the full evidence case, and had Halvard Reeve and Corwin Pell brought in immediately after.

**The council:** explained the full truth — souls as the real currency, Malphestor the Reckoner, Duskgate as the bargain's true anchor. None of the three had any real knowledge of infernal contracts, but Kell named a contact: **Sister Ysolde Marrow**, a cleric at the regional seat (the same city Ravenshollow is chartered from) versed in judgment and infernal debts. A careful, detail-free message was sent ahead to her.
