On a hunch, Darkhawk suggested the two still-uncracked coded ledgers might be written in Infernal rather than a numeric cipher — Corwin immediately recognized this as the likely answer nobody had considered, and volunteered to escort both ledgers to Sister Ysolde personally rather than risk them to a courier alone.

**Real commitments secured:** Kell authorized a dozen deputized fighters to march for the Bear Cave within two days, paid from the town's own purse; Halvard committed a standing House Reeve supply line (coin, weapons, armor) starting within the week, funded largely off what was taken from Vale and the Circuit itself; Perrin Dask agreed to mass-produce sky-iron-treated (and silvered, where possible) weapons for the incoming garrison, first shipment moving alongside Halvard's convoy.

**Vale's last act:** offered one final, genuine chance to speak before being taken back into custody, Vale gave up the single most important piece of information yet: **the Warden's bargain doesn't renew automatically on death — it requires a living successor to travel to Duskgate in person and accept it.** He had quietly groomed **Merrick**, the foothill parcel's foreman, as exactly that successor, without ever telling him. As long as both men stay held and neither reaches Duskgate, the bargain has nowhere to renew.

**Back at the Bear Cave:** briefed Kaldur on Vale's revelation about Merrick, then personally walked the defenses with him, closing the three exploitable weaknesses from the original assault — the north face service cut permanently garrisoned, the watch towers rebuilt to close their sightline gap, the midday shift change staggered so the garrison's never thin all at once. A thorough sweep almost missed a faint disturbed patch near the tree line — dismissed at first as possibly an animal trail.

**The watcher:** investigated with Mara (advantage, 21) and confirmed it was a person, not an animal — the same profile as the never-caught watcher from behind Old Harlan's Forge in Ravensmoor. A tracking attempt initially went badly (natural 1) before Darkhawk's help turned it around (advantage, reroll 11): the trail held to a bare rock stretch and died, direction unclear beyond "east." Rather than let it go, pursued that evening — a fresh trail picked up fast (advantage, 19), a still-warm cold camp found, and a distant cloaked figure sighted moving steadily east. Mara cast **Pass without Trace** and the pair closed the distance in near-perfect silence (natural 20) — close enough for the figure to realize, stunned, that they'd finally been caught.

**Sael, revealed:** the watcher turned out to be **Sael**, a solitary bear-kin druid (Circle of the Moon) who confirmed, once Darkhawk offered to listen rather than demand, that she was the one who pulled him from Ashpit years ago — witnessing his refusal to finish a beaten opponent, feeling an old primal instinct answer before she'd decided anything, using her bear Wild Shape to drag him from the wreckage through an old drainage tunnel, and healing the near-fatal wound with druidic craft. She's watched from a distance ever since (Ravensmoor's alley watcher and the Bear Cave's tree-line watcher were both her), reasoning it was to protect what the spirit marked without her own shadow over his path — until a literal devil's bargain at Duskgate made continued silence feel irresponsible. She confirmed the bear that answered Darkhawk's Spirit Seeker ritual at Thundertree wasn't her — a genuinely separate, older bear-spirit choosing to answer him directly, a rare and significant thing among bear-kin tradition.

Darkhawk thanked her plainly rather than demanding more, and — with Mara's blessing, on the condition Sael fights where the party can see her while trust builds — welcomed her onto the team outright. A full character sheet was written up for her (Druid 9, Circle of the Moon).

**To the regional seat:** with the Bear Cave in Kaldur's hands, Darkhawk, Mara, and Sael traveled to the regional seat — the city Ravenshollow is chartered from — meeting Corwin and **Sister Ysolde Marrow**, a Grave Domain cleric of Thossia, already deep in the two coded ledgers. After a full recap of the campaign to date, Ysolde confirmed the ledgers are written in Infernal, not cipher, and had already begun translating a partial copy of the bargain's own renewal rite.

**The bargain's true terms, revealed:** souls are banked across a warden's tenure and offered at Duskgate at renewal, alongside a living successor's in-person acceptance; in exchange the warden receives wealth, network control, and a standing ward against ever being truly seen or scried — which explains why nobody who ever dealt with "the Warden" could describe him with confidence, and why Edmund Vale lost his own protection the instant he was captured in the open. Pressed further, Ysolde found the renewal clause's contingency: if no successor arrives within a **fortnight** of a warden's fall, Malphestor is entitled to act directly, likely compelling Merrick or another vulnerable target itself. Holding Vale and Merrick buys time, not permanent safety.

Asked about the black scrying mirror, Ysolde judged it almost certainly Malphestor's own communication line, too dangerous to test blind, and offered to examine it herself under prepared wards. Asked for protection against fiends, she offered a full day's consecration ritual on the party's weapons (warding Darkhawk's blades and dagger, Mara's bow and shortsword, Sael's staff and sickle against fiends specifically) plus Protection from Evil and Good to save for the fight that matters most, and committed to traveling with the party through to Duskgate itself — not as a fighter, but for wards, blessings, and expertise nobody else in the party has. A full character sheet was written up for her (Cleric 10, Grave Domain).

**Ends:** the regional seat, the day's consecration ritual complete, weapons warded, Sister Ysolde now a full party member alongside Sael. Vale remains in custody at the Bear Cave alongside Merrick, the site's known weaknesses closed, reinforcements and better-armed weapons inbound. Duskgate remains the confirmed final target, with real breathing room to prepare and a clearer picture than ever of exactly what has to be broken there and why. Open threads: the untried scrying mirror (Ysolde to examine it at the Bear Cave), the true nature of the bear-spirit that answered Darkhawk directly, whether the accumulated soul-tribute is still physically present at Duskgate, and the fortnight clock on Malphestor's contingency.

**Back at the Bear Cave:** Kell's dozen deputies arrived ahead of schedule, drilling on mountain terrain under Thundertree supervision. On Darkhawk's order, Kaldur added a dedicated sky-watch (Vosk's allied hunters) on top of the ground rotation, since devils and fiends aren't bound to roads.

**The scrying mirror, examined:** Ysolde warded a circle and analyzed it properly (Religion/Arcana, 25) — confirmed it's a genuine two-way scrying link keyed specifically to Malphestor's domain, dangerous to activate carelessly (would announce the party's presence instantly), but capable of a limited, one-time covert viewing if prepared properly in advance. On Darkhawk's call, the party chose to hold off using it until the Bear Cave was fully battle-ready rather than risk drawing attention mid-preparation.

**Full readiness:** over the following days, Halvard's supply wagon and Perrin's sky-iron-treated weapons arrived, the deputies finished drilling, and warriors from two clans allied through Vosk began arriving alongside Thundertree's own — the Bear Cave transformed from a holding force into a proper fortified stronghold.

**Briefing Vosk:** gave her the full accounting — Vale, the bargain's true terms, Malphestor, the fortnight contingency, Merrick, Sael's true history, Ysolde, the mirror. Vosk committed further rather than less: **veteran warriors (not green scouts) to reinforce the waystation and both relay stops**, per Darkhawk's own request that anything approaching the Bear Cave "bleed to get there" — pairing them with the existing turned/allied garrisons (Rennick, Fenris Cray) rather than replacing them outright.

**A quieter moment:** asked Vosk directly for leadership advice, admitting doubt in his own instinct-driven decisions. She told him plainly that leaders who stop questioning themselves are exactly how titles like "the Warden" rot from within, that his instinct has already proven itself keeping the people around him alive, and that asking the question at all says more about his fitness to lead than any answer could.

**Family, and Sael's own closure:** introduced Sael to Sesha, Harek, and Thyra as the one who saved him at Ashpit. The family's gratitude was immediate and overwhelming — Harek offering the forearm clasp reserved for kin, Sesha declaring her family "however that has to work with your own people's ways." Darkhawk told Sael plainly she could forgive herself now that everyone else already had — the first real release of guilt she's let herself have since the night she pulled him from the pit.

**Ends:** Thundertree, a full and peaceful night with family, the Bear Cave fully fortified and garrisoned behind them, the waystation and relay stops about to be reinforced with veterans. Duskgate remains the confirmed final target, with the mirror's viewing still held in reserve. Open threads unchanged: the true nature of the bear-spirit, whether the soul-tribute still sits at Duskgate, and the fortnight clock.

**The mirror, used:** back at the fully garrisoned Bear Cave, Ysolde attempted the covert viewing first — a chaotic glimpse of a vast, ember-lit chamber deep at Duskgate, empty chains, and drifting soul-lights that turned toward the connection and forced her to cut it early (Religion/Arcana, 10 — noticed, not fully hidden). Rather than treat that as a failure, Darkhawk chose to reopen the link deliberately and alone, naming himself directly to whatever was watching, over the group's honestly weighed but ultimately deferred-to-him objections (Mara's caution about provoking speed, Ysolde's read that a devil's ego could cut either way, Kaldur's blunt cost-accounting, Sael's point that secrecy was likely already spent).

**Malphestor, met directly:** the devil answered in person — patient, unhurried, framing everything in ledger and debt terms. It confirmed recognizing "Number Seven" from Ashpit's own old accounts, acknowledged Darkhawk as the first debtor in its memory to close his own account "in his own hand," and countered "justice" by asserting the reckoning runs both ways — that the Circuit's own historical debts predate its involvement. It invited Darkhawk to Duskgate to "collect his justice in person," and confirmed the fortnight clock still runs.

**The probing-attack read:** Darkhawk deduced Malphestor's invitation is likely bait to draw the Bear Cave's full strength away from Vale and Merrick — the bargain's only real vulnerability. The party agreed: **only the four of them (Darkhawk, Mara, Sael, Ysolde) will go to Duskgate**, while Kaldur holds the fully garrisoned Bear Cave. Final orders given to Kaldur: expect a probing attack testing the base's numbers, and deliberately undersell the garrison's true strength to any observer — normal-looking visible rotations, real numbers held back out of sight.

**Ends:** the Bear Cave, the four-person strike team about to set out for Duskgate alone, Kaldur and the full garrison holding behind them with deceptive numbers ready. Malphestor now knows Darkhawk by name and history. The campaign's final act begins.

**The road to Duskgate:** five careful days past Ravensmoor into unmapped country, the "dead ground" wrongness thickening the closer the party got, Sael scouting ahead in bird form. Duskgate itself resolved into view as less a fortress than a scar built into the mountainside — no walls, no banners, just presence.

**Scouting the site:** Darkhawk's instinct to fight was overruled by his own read of the ground — the group planned instead. Sael shifted into a fox (well clear of Ysolde's ward-sensing, on Darkhawk's own catch that fresh casting near the boundary might trip a magic-keyed alarm) and scouted the layout: an outer market (10-12 guards, holding pens, ordinary Circuit business) and an older, unguarded inner section radiating dread — the market's own guards avoiding it on instinct, leaving a 40-yard unwatched gap in the perimeter. Ysolde sensed the wards directly (25) — old, layered, keyed to intent and magic rather than raw presence, meaning Sael's mundane scouting shape passed clean.

**The judgment door:** Mara and Sael's physical trap-check came up clean but wrong-footed (8) — Ysolde's magical read (advantage, 17) found the truth: a ward that judges intent, safe for a willing successor, an alarm for anyone else. An attempt to unravel it rather than force it went bad (advantage, 10) and tripped the alarm outright. Darkhawk called the pivot immediately: force the door, bar it from inside, go in hot.

**The hellhound guard (full initiative combat, first proper use of the new formal-initiative and companion-tactical-control agreements):** three hellhounds answered the alarm. Mara opened with **Conjure Animals** — eight wolves erupting in front of the pack — then landed a Dread Ambusher bonus shot; the lead hound recharged Fire Breath into its own reinforcements, costing itself the exchange. Sael dropped **Spike Growth** around the whole hound position; when Mara ordered the wolves to feign retreat, both surviving hounds gave chase straight through the thorns, gutting themselves on their own aggression. Ysolde's **Command ("Flee")** routed the last hound at 6 HP; Darkhawk's last javelin finished it mid-flight. **Zero damage taken by the party across the entire fight.**

**The antechamber and the great doors:** Mara's conjured wolves froze well short of a set of towering iron-black doors, refusing to go further. Ysolde read old, aware wards radiating from beyond them — not static like the outer boundary, but *awake*. Before opening them, Ysolde cast **Protection from Evil and Good** on Darkhawk (saved specifically for this), Mara stacked **Protection from Energy (fire)** on top of it (confirmed to stack cleanly — separate casters, separate concentration), and the group fanned out before Darkhawk forced the doors.

**Malphestor, faced directly:** beyond the doors, the chamber from the mirror — chains, drifting soul-lights, a massive altar — with Malphestor itself waiting, unhurried, already expecting him. A long, honest dialogue followed rather than a fight: Malphestor confirmed the reckoning "runs both ways," that killing it alone changes nothing (the office survives its holder), and — pressed hard by Darkhawk's own philosophical challenge about whether vengeance would "tip the scales too far" — revealed the real truth: only destroying the anchor itself ends the bargain, and doing so requires a soul willingly exposed to it, cost unknown, framed as honest uncertainty rather than a lie ("a devil that has to be honest with you is a devil that's found a truth dangerous enough not to need a lie" — Ysolde's read, confirmed via Religion/Arcana 23). Malphestor also confirmed its own motive plainly when asked outright: cutting its losses on a failing bargain, spending honesty now while it still buys something, rather than lying uselessly on the way down.

**The vow and the trial:** Darkhawk named this as the choice he'd foreshadowed weeks earlier on the road; Mara, keeping her long-standing promise to be present rather than talked out of it, agreed to two final requests — not to follow him into the trial itself, and to tell Sesha and Harek in person that their son chose this willingly to protect people who couldn't protect themselves. A quiet prayer to the bear-spirit was answered with a felt, sustained warmth (Sael confirmed feeling it too) just before Darkhawk placed his hand on the altar.

The trial itself nearly unmade him — a Wisdom saving throw (advantage from the bear-spirit's blessing, DC 20) came up short (12) against an ordinary soul's odds, but the bear-spirit intervened directly and decisively rather than passively, claiming him a second time ("this one's mine") exactly as it did at Ashpit. **The anchor broke. Every soul bound to the altar was freed outright.**

**Cost and gift:** Darkhawk emerged with 1 level of exhaustion (clears on a long rest), a permanent claw-shaped scar over his heart, and a standing bond with the bear-spirit — advantage on saving throws against being charmed, frightened, or possessed, permanently.

**Malphestor's ending:** stripped of purpose, it corrected itself — using Darkhawk's real names for the first time — and admitted uncertainty whether redemption is possible for something like it. Darkhawk's answer ("if anger can fall, maybe devils can rise") visibly moved it; it chose, for the first time in its existence, to walk away on its own rather than wait passively for judgment. Its fate afterward is unknown — it does not know itself whether its own superiors will arrive in moments or much later. It was not killed, and confirmed it has no authority over or interest in the market's mundane guards and captives, but freely gave up that intel anyway, along with the location of a strongroom holding the bargain's accumulated wealth.

**Leveled up a third time this session (milestone — breaking the anchor, ending the Warden's bargain permanently, and offering Malphestor genuine hope rather than simple vengeance):**
- Darkhawk to **Barbarian 9** (Brutal Critical). Rolled +9 HP, now **113 max**.
- Mara to **Ranger 12** (Gloom Stalker), gaining an Ability Score Improvement (+2 WIS, 14→16). Rolled +4 HP, now **80 max**.
- Sael to **Druid 10**, keeping pace on the same milestone schedule. Rolled +4 HP, now **66 max**.
- Sister Ysolde to **Cleric 11**, likewise. Rolled +10 HP, now **79 max**.

**Ends:** Duskgate's inner chamber, the anchor shattered, the trapped souls freed, Malphestor alive but purposeless and gone into the shadows, the whole party leveled up together. One task remains before Duskgate is fully finished: the outer market's living captives and the strongroom's wealth, both still waiting behind ten or twelve ordinary guards. Open threads: Malphestor's ultimate fate, who actually betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit (never resolved, and Malphestor didn't know either), and what becomes of the Circuit's scattered remnants now that its true center is gone.

**The market raid:** Darkhawk opened with a raging Reckless Attack charge (Extra Attack + off-hand, one a natural-20 Brutal Critical) that killed the first guard outright before the fight properly started. Full formal initiative across three guard clusters: Sael's **Spike Growth** pinned Cluster A in place; Mara cleaned up its two crossbowmen; Ysolde's **Flame Strike** wiped two of Cluster B outright and routed the third; the last guard standing from Cluster A landed one real hit before folding. Cluster C, cornered at the holding pens, took two captives hostage rather than fight — Darkhawk's point-blank ultimatum ("free them and walk, or hold them and die," Intimidation 20 with advantage) broke all three cleanly, hostages released unharmed. Guards were let go rather than killed or held, on Darkhawk's word alone ("fuck off... now").

**The pens, fully cleared:** nine captives total freed and armed from the guards' own gear for anyone who wanted a weapon for the walk out. One, an older man, flagged the strongroom's existence unprompted, matching Malphestor's own account exactly.

**The search and the strongroom:** the seven fallen guards yielded 57 gp, a duty roster confirming Duskgate's operation was already scheduled to lapse ("the season's close" that was never going to arrive), and an unsent letter home from the dead flanker — set aside respectfully rather than looted. The strongroom (opened with a key found in the handler's office) held **1,240 gp**, gemstones, mixed jewelry, a pair of exceptional silver-inlaid dueling daggers, a fine chainmail shirt stripped off a "graduate" before sale, and a gold pendant engraved with unknown initials — its owner unidentified, worth trying to trace rather than simply keep. All coin and generic valuables earmarked for redistribution to affected families, per Darkhawk's own standing word.

**The road home:** Sael scouted the route clear (Perception 11 — the fled guard spotted well down the trail, too panicked to have organized any response), and the full group — party, nine freed captives, and everything recovered — made it back to the Bear Cave without incident. Kaldur met the column partway, and the captives were fed, warmed, and properly settled by the garrison before anyone else sat down for the night.

**Ends, for real this time:** the Bear Cave, quiet and secure, Duskgate fully and completely resolved — the bargain broken, the market cleared, nine more captives freed, the strongroom's wealth recovered for redistribution, and the whole party home safe. Open threads carried forward: Malphestor's fate, who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, the pendant's owner, and what remains of the Circuit now that its true center is gone.

**The morning after — naming the fear out loud:** during the long rest, Malphestor's own words kept surfacing — that whatever it answers to would have felt the anchor break instantly. Darkhawk voiced the fear to Mara first (that doing good may have unleashed a war), then called a full council (Mara, Kaldur, Sael, Ysolde) to say it plainly to everyone rather than carry it alone. Pushed hard by Darkhawk himself, Ysolde revised her first read: a devil-hierarchy that just lost decades of banked souls likely seeks *recovery or retaliation* before quiet reassignment, and Darkhawk — the only person in memory to have actually done this — should assume he's the example it's most interested in making. Kaldur's response: prepare specifically for that, not vaguely.

The weight of it tipped Darkhawk toward trying to push everyone away rather than risk them — the authority-discomfort thread naming itself at last, born from his own guilt over the Thundertree dead rather than any external authority. The full council refused to let it stand, each with a distinct answer (Mara: love isn't spent like coin; Kaldur: isolation doesn't honor the dead; Sael: don't hand her guilt back; Ysolde: isolation doesn't make you safer). Darkhawk asked Ysolde directly why she follows him after only days — she answered that watching him weigh mercy against vengeance at Duskgate was the most genuine act of judgment she's witnessed in a career spent studying it. The council closed warm rather than heavy, ending on Darkhawk asking everyone to promise to "smack him back into line" when he errs — accepted, gladly, by all four.

**The restitution fund opens:** a proper reception area built at the Bear Cave's entrance, staffed personally by Darkhawk. Seven verified claimants paid over the following days — a Kessick's Ford boot-maker's widow and son (50 gp, his fate confirmed via the ledger four years after his disappearance), a Western Yard farmer's nephew (40 gp), an older couple's daughter from the Dead Ground's old "unaccounted for" list (50 gp, no happy ending but an honest one), and four more (147 gp total) — **287 gp distributed, 953 gp remaining** in the walled-off Family Restitution Fund. One false claim was refused outright rather than paid. Standing policy: whatever's left once claims stop arriving splits evenly across every claimant.

**Training the garrison:** while waiting on more claimants, the whole party spent real time training the Bear Cave's growing force directly — Darkhawk on combat fundamentals and Battle Master tactics, Mara on archery/stealth/tracking, Sael on wilderness craft, Ysolde on battlefield first aid and theology. Kaldur confirmed it's the best-prepared the base has ever been.

**A settlement, and a council:** Darkhawk proposed turning the Bear Cave from a garrison into a genuine self-sufficient town, explicitly asking Vosk's blessing first rather than assuming the right, since it sits on Thundertree ground. Vosk granted it on real terms (a Thundertree voice, some of her own people settling there, the place staying what it's proven itself to be) — then Darkhawk expanded the vision further still: a full regional council with a seat for every allied tribe and every town down to Kessick's Ford, framed explicitly as building for "our children's future." Vosk is carrying the proposal to the other clans; Darkhawk still needs to carry it to Kessick's Ford and the river towns himself.

**The wedding:** with the bargain dead and nothing left hunting either of them, Darkhawk re-proposed to Mara exactly as she'd asked — "once it's actually over." She said yes again, immediately, in front of Vosk, Kaldur, Sael, Ysolde, and Darkhawk's whole family. Vosk offered to host the wedding at Thundertree. No date set yet.

**Ends the session:** Thundertree, evening, the proposal's warmth still settling over everyone gathered. The Warden's whole conspiracy is resolved. What remains is entirely constructive: a fund still paying out real justice, a garrison turned into something that might outlast this generation, a council being born, and a wedding still to plan. Open threads: whatever sits above Malphestor and whether it comes looking, Malphestor's own fate, who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, the pendant's unknown owner, and the shape of the settlement and regional council still to be built.

## Session 16 — 2026-08-06 to ~2026-08-24

**A quiet morning at the Bear Cave:** the settlement's first real morning of building rather than bracing — survey talk, drill-yard noise, two more Thundertree families walking the ground with Kaldur. A runner arrived with a letter from Halvard: Ravenshollow's regional office (the same city Ysolde's order calls home) had gone quiet in the past week — no raid, just clerks vanished and paper burned after dark — and Kessick's Ford's council wanted to hear the Duskgate account directly rather than through rumor.

**Settlement planning with Kaldur:** he laid out what Urskelde (still "the Bear Cave" at this point) needed to become a real settlement — walls, a second water source, an export good, and a proper name for any charter. The one thing with an actual clock on it: a land survey, needed before Halvard's settler incentives could mean anything.

**Sael's survey:** she and Kaldur's surveyor, Bellik, walked the full boundary in a day. Findings: a clean second spring good for a well, a sound timber stand on stable ground, boundary lines wide enough for the settlement's real ambitions — and a cluster of old goliath-worked cairns on the northern edge, old enough to predate Thundertree's own memory, flagged as needing Vosk's word before any building goes near them.

**Garrison security:** Kaldur raised the real fear underneath the settlement's growth — that whatever sits above Malphestor lost decades of banked souls in an afternoon, and the two men who could ever renew the bargain (Vale, Merrick) are held right here. Ysolde agreed to ward their cells directly (a day's work) rather than attempt a full perimeter ward alone, and — at Darkhawk's request — sent a rider to her order at the regional seat requesting five or six warding clerics, with a full account of Duskgate and Darkhawk's own fear of retaliation attached in his own words. She also asked a trusted contact in the same city to quietly check on the Ravenshollow office before it finished burning its own trail.

**The restitution line:** an honest claim paid (35 gp), a false one refused, and the engraved pendant from Duskgate's strongroom finally identified — an elderly woman recognized it as her late sister's, worn since by her niece Mira. Returned without payment asked or offered. **322 gp paid to date, 918 gp remaining in the fund.**

**The Ravenshollow office, infiltrated:** traveling to the regional seat with Ysolde, the party found the office shuttered, char still faint in the courtyard behind it. Mara cast Pass without Trace; a clean stealth approach (rolls of 20, 25, 31, 21) got everyone to the side door unseen. She checked it for traps (Investigation 23 — clean) and picked the simple pin lock (Dexterity 24). Splitting up, Sael and Ysolde's search of the front room (12) found only a professionally emptied strongbox and ash raked flat on purpose — someone closed this office to a plan, not a panic. Darkhawk and Mara's search of the back office (21) turned up what the cleanup missed: a half-burned ledger page listing property in three towns (two known to Halvard, one unidentified) and an unsent letter, signed with an unfamiliar personal mark, closing with "if asked, I was never here" — proof someone besides Vale held real authority in that office and fled the moment he was captured.

**Kessick's Ford, briefed in full:** Halvard, Magistrate Kell, Corwin, and Tomas heard the entire account — Vale confirmed as the Warden himself, the ledgers as the bargain's own contract, the anchor's breaking, Malphestor left alive. Kell pledged the office's full formal written backing for the regional council on the spot; Corwin took on writing the true account for the record. Halvard agreed to run down the Ravenshollow office lead through his own contacts (3-4 weeks estimated).

**The Bear Cave gets a name — and an economy:** at the same table, Darkhawk laid out plans to reopen the old mine's vein, recruit smiths, and turn Urskelde into something that could actually sustain itself. Halvard, Corwin, and Kell weighed in on labor, a mining charter, and trade goods; Perrin Dask — visited at his own forge — agreed to travel out and assess the vein personally, offering to bring in Greta Ostmark if it proves worthwhile. Reaching for his own half-remembered Giant, Darkhawk named the settlement himself: **Urskelde**, "the bear's shelter." Halvard sent riders to carry the name to both Kaldur and Vosk that same night.

**The secret wedding:** pulling Halvard aside privately, Darkhawk asked him to send word ahead to Vosk and to his own family — arranging a full Thundertree wedding to surprise Mara, using "the slow road" (scouting the ridge line and mine terrain, officially for hunting grounds and Perrin's benefit) to buy the necessary time. Sael and Ysolde were let in on it once Mara was out of earshot; Ysolde offered to weave a blessing of Thossia into the rite itself, and Darkhawk asked for both traditions together, "the binding of two people and two tribes." Eleven days of deliberately unhurried travel followed — Mara hunting well (Survival 18), Sael scouting the ridge as genuinely excellent hunting ground (Survival 25), the old vein getting an honest first look — before Thundertree came into view already dressed for something, Halvard's whole Kessick's Ford party waiting among the clan.

**Mara worked it out before anyone said a word**, turning back to find Darkhawk hanging deliberately at the rear of the group. Married the same day: a full Thundertree rite led by Vosk (Sesha speaking the old words, hands bound in red-dyed cord), and Sister Ysolde's blessing of Thossia woven in after. Harek presented the wedding gift Darkhawk had asked his family to help make — his own lifelong trophy, split and reworked by Thundertree's own carver into a matching pendant for Mara, a bear's claw twined with a fletched arrow, cut from the same original piece. The bear-spirit's presence was felt again, quietly, as Ysolde finished her blessing — noted by Sael, unremarked by Vosk beyond a private smile. The feast ran long into the night; for the first time in the whole campaign, Darkhawk let himself simply enjoy it, with nothing left to fear in the moment.

**Ends the session:** Thundertree, deep night, married, at peace. The pendant's owner found and the ledgers' mystery resolved (Vale was always the Warden, no one deeper), but new threads opened in their place: the Ravenshollow office's fleeing signatory and its three-town property list, Urskelde's economic future (incentives, the vein, the cairns still to be raised with Vosk), and the still-unanswered question of what's above Malphestor. Open threads carried forward: who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, Malphestor's fate, and whatever felt the anchor break.

## Session 17 — 2026-08-25 to ~2026-09-02

**Three quiet days at Thundertree:** real rest after the wedding — Mara teaching Thyra archery, Sael finding real peace in the tree line, Ysolde in delighted theological argument with a clan storyteller. On the second day, Darkhawk found Vosk alone and raised both outstanding items. **On the cairns:** she committed to leading a rite herself before any building goes near them, and set the rule permanently — nothing built within a bowshot, ever. **On the mining charter and settler incentives:** she'd already reviewed Halvard's draft and called it fair, then set three conditions of her own — a real share of the vein's wealth back to Thundertree, first choice of Urskelde plots for Thundertree families, and the charter naming Thundertree's claim to the whole Greyhorn stretch alongside the settlement's. Darkhawk reflected aloud on what he'd learned — that the past can't be altered and the future can't be controlled, only the present acted on — and Vosk affirmed it as hard-earned wisdom, not naivety. He bowed formally and accepted all three conditions on Thundertree's behalf.

**Return to Urskelde, married:** the settlement had visibly grown in the time away — survey stakes down, a timber frame rising near the second spring, the garrison turning out to celebrate the marriage in person. Darkhawk told Kaldur plainly that he'd always be a brother to him, not just an ally; Kaldur, visibly moved, accepted it the same way.

**Settlement business caught up:** five clerics from Ysolde's order had arrived and finished warding Vale and Merrick's cells directly. Perrin and Greta (she'd come after all) confirmed the main vein as genuinely valuable iron, and flagged a smaller seam that might be sky-iron-adjacent, worth a careful two-week excavation to confirm. Darkhawk committed to the dig, with the explicit condition that Darkhawk, Mara, Sael, and Ysolde get first pick of anything it yields before trade or the garrison does.

**The council chamber, designed:** Darkhawk asked for it built in time for the first council — a perfect round table, no seat more important than another — and Kaldur proposed a site on a rise near the second spring with an open view down the valley, left open to the sky by design rather than roofed. Sael, brought in properly, suggested building the ring around a natural circle of old weathered boulders rather than a surveyed line, with living trees planted at the boundary instead of a built wall. Asked separately if she wanted a druid's grove of her own nearby, she was visibly moved — the first place she's ever had that was simply hers — and asked for it left wild, marked only by a stone or two. Both were staked out on the spot; Greta agreed to craft the table itself once the site's ready.

**The restitution line, resumed:** two clean claims paid (40 gp, 45 gp). A third — an older man claiming a daughter lost near Bramhollow, absent from any recovered ledger — came back unreadable on every check tried (Darkhawk's Insight 2, Ysolde's 11, only Mara's Perception 17 turning up a genuine-seeming detail). Darkhawk chose to believe him on judgment rather than paperwork and paid him anyway (45 gp), explicit that the ledger's silence isn't proof of anything. **452 gp paid to 11 verified claimants; 788 gp remaining.**

**The cairns, honored:** Vosk arrived in person three days later and performed the rite herself — laying boughs at each of the six mounds, speaking an old formula ("watched, and remembered"). Ysolde's own read of the site (Religion/History 20) identified the pattern as a *watch-cairn* circle — not an ordinary burial ground, but the dead charged with watching over something even after death, facing the deep Greyhorn rather than any settlement. Pressed for what they might have been watching, Vosk admitted to only a thin, half-believed fragment of old Thundertree lore: something is held shut deep in the Greyhorn Range, and mountain-born folk are charged with keeping it that way rather than investigating it. She gave her blessing for Darkhawk to eventually go find out — guide and lore to be arranged when he's ready — on the condition that Urskelde is stable enough to stand without him first.

**Vosk's visit closes with a tour:** Kaldur walked her through everything built and staked so far; she left calling it "something real," rare in her own long experience of promises like this actually being kept.

**Ends the session:** Urskelde, evening, business caught up and a new goal named plainly for the first time — finding out what the cairns were really guarding. Open threads carried forward: who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, Malphestor's fate, what's above it, the Ravenshollow office's fleeing signatory, and now the Greyhorn Range's old, unconfirmed "wound."

## Session 18 — 2026-09-03 to ~2026-10-01

**Sael begins expedition prep:** sent ahead to scout cautiously (never more than a day past Thundertree's own mapped hunting range) and gather cold-weather gear, rations, and rope. Her scout found the true edge of mapped territory — a lone weathered marker stone, thinning wildlife despite good hunting ground, and total birdsong silence past a certain point. Her read: the land doesn't feel hostile, it feels *held*, like something's being kept in place. Vosk gave one further fragment of verse: *"What sleeps beneath stone does not dream kindly. Wake it gently, or not at all."*

**Urskelde made ready to stand alone:** weeks of hands-on work — Darkhawk personally trained deputies Idra and Torvin to run watch rotations and hold judgment calls without him. Corwin's finished charter (Vosk's three conditions folded in) arrived and was sealed. Perrin and Greta confirmed the seam is genuinely sky-iron; first pieces went to reforging the party's gear, starting with Darkhawk's chainmail shirt (now counts as magical armor, same as his blades) and a new pair of sky-iron bracers (**+1 AC**, bringing him to **AC 17**). The council chamber's foundation and Sael's grove were both staked into the ground. Halvard's Ravenshollow lead closed out: the mystery signature belonged to Vale's ordinary bookkeeper, Ostrin Vahl, now vanished with no trail — a loose end being tied off, not a hidden conspiracy.

**The expedition launches:** marching order set — Sael and Mara leading, Ysolde center, Darkhawk rear, deliberately unhurried pace. Four easy days through familiar foothills, then past Sael's marker stone into genuinely unmapped ground. On high alert, Sael read old, generations-deep animal avoidance patterns in the terrain; Ysolde's Eyes of the Grave found no undead but sensed a very faint, ancient impression at the edge of her senses. Mara's Primeval Awareness, at Mountain range (6 miles), confirmed a single, unmoving elemental presence — "settled," not roaming.

**The Standing Ring, found:** three days further in, a sheltered hollow holding an ancient circle of standing stones around a counterweighted central slab — craftsmanship matching the Urskelde cairns, but generations older, maintained rather than built once. Splitting the investigation (Mara and Sael on the ring itself, Darkhawk assisting Ysolde) turned up a clear picture: Mara found four of the stones carved with channels running to the center, meant to be walked rather than the slab forced open; Sael confirmed the stones were raised and re-tended across generations by the same craft tradition as the cairns; Ysolde's Religion/Arcana read confirmed it as a still-holding binding, not a failing one — forcing it risks disaster, but nothing needs rescuing today.

**The bear-spirit answers directly:** Darkhawk reached out in quiet ritual, same as at Thundertree, and received a clearer impression than ever before — walking, not breaking; the number four; and a wordless certainty: *not broken open, carried through.* All four took positions at the marked stones — Sael toward the mountain's depth, Mara back the way they came, Ysolde toward sunrise, Darkhawk at the slab itself — ready to walk the channels inward together.

**Ends the session:** the Standing Ring, the party positioned and about to attempt the ritual. Outcome not yet resolved — picks up here next time.

**The ritual walk, resolved:** all four moved inward together. The binding's waking pressure hit unevenly — Darkhawk hardest (a WIS save of 3, staggered by fragmented visions: stone hands, ancient Giant speech, the cold of being buried rather than merely cold), Mara close behind, Sael and Ysolde holding steady enough to actually anchor the working (Sael's wordless hum matching the stone's resonance, Ysolde's holy symbol glowing in answer). The center slab's counterweight shuddered and shifted, revealing a hidden descending stair rather than a pit.

**Kethrun, met:** at the bottom, a pale stone sentinel — humanoid, weathered, ancient — rose from where it had sat waiting, greeting Darkhawk as "kin-touched" for carrying the bear's mark plainly enough to feel through stone. Darkhawk closed his eyes and reached out through the bear-spirit bond directly (18) and received a mediated impression: *kin, not the same — stone where I am fur, waiting where I answer* — confirming the sentinel was placed to hold "a wound the mountain itself couldn't close," set by ancient hands who never returned to relieve it.

Introduced himself fully and apologized for the abandonment; the sentinel — **Kethrun** — was visibly moved, having heard no apology in longer than Thundertree's oldest stories reach. Gave his name and origin: placed by goliath-kin older than Thundertree's own name for itself, tasked with holding the wound and waiting for his makers' return, which never came.

**The full account given:** Darkhawk told Kethrun everything — Ashpit, the Circuit's fall, Duskgate, Malphestor spared rather than killed, and Urskelde's settlement/regional-council vision, specifically naming the cairns. Kethrun recognized the cairns immediately as the same ancient ward-tradition's "lesser" technique — confirming whoever built Urskelde's boundary stones was unknowingly continuing his own makers' work.

**The wound, revealed:** ancient — older than devils and bargains — where something extraplanar once tried to force a foothold into these mountains, fought back and sealed (not destroyed; some wounds can only be held) by long-dead goliath-kin and primal spirits kin to the bear-spirit, working together. Kethrun has felt something testing the seal faintly since the night Duskgate's anchor broke: very likely whatever sits above Malphestor, searching for new ground now that its old foothold failed. Closing this fully won't end that threat, but buys real time.

Darkhawk named the moment as feeling deliberately meant — the four of them, together, hearing this specifically — without being able to explain why. Kethrun didn't ask him to; his makers believed some things simply arrive as right when the right hands and hearts are in the right place, and said he'd waited an age for exactly this shape of company.

**The commitment:** Darkhawk formally took Kethrun and the wound under Urskelde's protection — asked for, not assumed, mirroring the same respect shown to Vosk over the settlement. Kethrun accepted: he'll sense anything testing the seal and relay warning through the cairns if they're kept tended, in exchange for Urskelde sending a mason who understands old ward-work and a cleric to renew the seal's blessing regularly, rather than leaving him to hold it entirely alone. Darkhawk intends to bring this to a full council before committing resources, and Kethrun agreed the decision deserves care rather than dread-driven haste.

**Ends the session, for real this time:** Kethrun's chamber beneath the Standing Ring, an ancient ally found, a direct confirmation that whatever's above Malphestor is actively searching for new ground, and a new standing duty accepted on Urskelde's behalf. Open threads carried into the next session: who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, the pendant's owner (since resolved — see character sheet), what's actually above Malphestor and how close it's gotten to finding the seal, and the mason/cleric assignment still to be decided by council.

**The party splits, deliberately:** Sael and Ysolde set out back to Urskelde to gather help and bring word to Vosk (Ysolde wanted time to prepare a proper, unhurried blessing rather than a rushed one); Darkhawk and Mara stayed at Kethrun's chamber.

**A history freely given:** Darkhawk asked Kethrun for the history he was never allowed as a stolen child. Kethrun told it plainly — an ancient, unified goliath-kin people who worked as true allies alongside primal spirits (bear-kin among them), a deliberate tear forced into these mountains by something patient and ambitious, driven back over years rather than one battle, and sealed rather than destroyed because none of his makers believed true destruction was possible. Thundertree and every modern clan are what came after that war's cost scattered the old alliance — Vosk's own cairn-verse is the last worn-down echo of a once-precise warning.

Pressed further, Kethrun confirmed the old primal spirits aren't uniformly gone — some have genuinely withdrawn after centuries of mortal carelessness, but not all, and the bear-spirit's own repeated, deliberate intervention (twice, at Ashpit and at Duskgate) is itself proof some of them are still watching, waiting for someone who "asks instead of assumes."

Asked whether other doorways exist, Kethrun confirmed at least three other sealed sites from his own age (current condition unknown — he's had no way to sense his kin-sentinels the entire time he's waited here), and raised a harder possibility: whatever's hunting Urskelde's seal doesn't strictly need an *existing* wound — a patient enough power could try to tear a wholly new one, given enough time and inattention.

**A turning point:** hearing the scope of it plainly laid out, Darkhawk's dread resolved into calm acceptance rather than deepening — confrontation being certain, he realized, isn't a weight, it's clarity. Named his guilt over the Thundertree dead honestly one more time, but reframed it: they didn't die for nothing. Mara and Kethrun both recognized the shift as real.

**The Bear's Answer:** in that same clarity, Darkhawk fully opened himself to the bear-spirit and offered it a mutual vow — partnership freely given, not mere gratitude for being saved twice. It accepted, deepening the standing bond into something Kethrun called a genuine alliance, the kind his own makers once had and the world has since forgotten how to make. **New ability gained:** once per long rest, as a bonus action while raging, Darkhawk can call on the bear-spirit directly for 1 minute — his melee attacks deal an extra 1d6 damage, and any creature starting its turn within 10 ft who can see him must save (WIS DC 14) or be frightened.

**Ends, properly this time:** Kethrun's chamber, Darkhawk and Mara waiting on Sael and Ysolde's return with Vosk and reinforcements, a real alliance formed with both an ancient sentinel and the bear-spirit itself, and Darkhawk standing in something closer to peace than fear for the first time since Duskgate's aftermath.

**Reinforcements arrive:** Sael and Ysolde returned with Elder Vosk herself, two Thundertree warriors, and Kaldur, who insisted on coming personally. Darkhawk gave the full account — Kethrun's history, the wound, the other sealed sites, the surrounding points of interest (a healing hot spring, a richer sky-iron vein, warded hunting grounds). Vosk committed Thundertree properly to helping maintain the seal going forward; Kaldur volunteered mason **Torvald** from among the deputies' own families. Vosk and Kaldur then returned to their own duties, leaving Torvald to work alongside Ysolde.

**The seal reinforced:** a careful two-day joint ritual — Ysolde's blessing matched to Kethrun's own remembered phrasing, Torvald's masonry repairing centuries of physical wear, Darkhawk's bear-spirit bond anchoring the whole working — left the Standing Ring's seal the strongest it's felt in a century. Torvald chose to stay on with Kethrun to keep learning the old ward-work properly.

**The ancient village, found:** with Kethrun and Torvald settled, the party set out for the goliath-kin village two days northeast. A quiet, undamaged ruin carved directly into the mountainside — no sign of battle, flight, or ordinary death. Mara's Primeval Awareness and Ysolde's Eyes of the Grave both confirmed four faint, non-hostile undead presences scattered through the halls — spirits frozen mid-moment, not killed, echoing Kethrun's own story of abandonment.

**The ritual hall:** Sael tracked down a central hall distinct from the surrounding homes. Its murals told the fuller history — the same ancient tear, the joint goliath-kin/primal-spirit war, and a deliberate mass-freezing of the whole village as protection rather than harm, meant to be temporary. A hidden dais held four pedestals (mirroring the Standing Ring exactly) and four empty vessels marked bear, storm, stone, and still water. Sael flew back to Kethrun alone to confirm the mechanism: each vessel needs a genuine offering of self from someone the corresponding spirit would recognize as true — sincerity tested the same way the anchor tested Darkhawk, with the same real risk for a false or obligated offering.

**Three new bonds, freely chosen:** Darkhawk stepped away deliberately to let Mara, Sael, and Ysolde decide together, without his presence shaping the choice. All three chose to try. Reaching out through his own bond, Darkhawk asked the bear-spirit to "call out" to its old kin on their behalf — it agreed, clarifying it couldn't compel an answer, only remind older voices that someone was asking again.

Each offered a true, personal reckoning rather than a performance: **Mara** offered her hard-won choice to face fear instead of avoid it, and bonded to a spirit of still water (new ability: **The Deep Water's Stillness** — 1/long rest reaction, auto-miss an incoming attack by going perfectly still). **Sael** offered the end of her self-imposed isolation, and bonded to a spirit of stone (new ability: **The Mountain's Patience** — 1/long rest reaction, reduce incoming damage by 1d12 + CON). **Ysolde** offered her shift from thirty years of careful scholarship into real action, and bonded to a spirit of storm (new ability: **The Storm's Judgment** — 1/long rest bonus action, impose disadvantage on a struck creature's next save).

All three succeeded. The vessels filled — dark still water, packed grey stone-dust, and a crack of clean storm-charge — and the whole hall (and reportedly the wider village) responded at once, a sound like the entire mountain finally exhaling.

**Ends the session, mid-release:** the ancient village's ritual hall, all four vessels filled, something clearly beginning to happen to the frozen villagers throughout the site. Outcome not yet resolved — picks up here next time. Open threads: whether/how the villagers are actually released, what becomes of Kethrun's other sealed sites, who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, and what's actually above Malphestor and how close it's gotten to finding a new foothold.

**The village wakes:** the release completed fully — every frozen figure across the whole community returned to life, disoriented but alive, centuries having passed in what felt to them like a single held breath. Met **Threnn**, the village's leader, and told him the truth plainly ("too long") rather than softening it. He absorbed the shock of lost centuries with real grief and real resolve, asking only whether his village still had a future to answer for.

Told him honestly: yes. Explained Urskelde, the settlement, the regional council built for children not yet born. Threnn recognized it immediately as a new attempt at the same alliance his own people once lost, and offered his people's service — old ward-craft, stonework, primal knowledge — in exchange for a real place in what's being built, not treated as a curiosity from a lost age.

Spent four days helping the village prepare to travel before addressing anything deeper, at Darkhawk's insistence — practical care first. Walking the two days back toward the Standing Ring, Threnn told the fuller story: his people were farmers and smiths, not soldiers; something broke loose from the main fighting at the wound and came for the village directly; the elders made the choice to freeze the whole community as protection rather than face annihilation, meaning it to last days rather than centuries — and the elders themselves never returned to release them, fate unknown. Darkhawk offered a comforting, unconfirmed theory: perhaps the elders became something like Urskelde's own cairns, still watching in a different form. Threnn found real comfort in the possibility, whether or not it's ever proven.

**Kethrun and Threnn, reunited:** the whole column of roughly three dozen goliath-kin descended into Kethrun's chamber. The sentinel was left nearly speechless — real kin of his own makers, not just descendants, standing in front of him after centuries of total isolation. Darkhawk gave them both space and time together, promising Kethrun plainly: "your watch will never be alone again."

**Milestone level-up (the campaign's first full four-person level-up, earned for finding and restoring an entire lost goliath-kin people and ending Kethrun's isolation for good):**
- Darkhawk to **Fighter 4**, taking **Resilient (Wisdom)** as his ASI. Proficiency bonus now **+5** (character level 13). Rolled +4 HP, now **117 max**.
- Mara to **Ranger 13**, gaining a 4th-level spell slot. Proficiency bonus now **+5** (character level 13). Rolled +6 HP, now **86 max**.
- Sael to **Druid 11**, gaining a 6th-level spell slot. Rolled +7 HP, now **73 max**.
- Sister Ysolde to **Cleric 12**, taking **War Caster** as her ASI. Rolled +7 HP, now **86 max**.

**Ends the session, for real:** the party heading back toward Urskelde to prepare for Threnn's entire community's arrival, Kethrun and Threnn left to their long-overdue reunion, and the whole party freshly leveled. Open threads carried forward: who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, what's actually above Malphestor and how close it's gotten to finding a new foothold, the fate of Kethrun's other sealed sites, and the true fate of the village's lost elders.

## Session 19 — 2026-07-23

**Home to Urskelde:** three easy days back from the Standing Ring, no incident, Threnn's column of roughly three dozen goliath-kin following a few days further behind at their own pace. Kaldur met the party at the gate himself, already briefed secondhand and visibly thrilled at the scale of what they'd found.

**Word to Vosk, and the council plan:** Darkhawk asked Kaldur to send word so Vosk could be present for Threnn's people's arrival. Told plainly there was grave news from Kethrun and Threnn to share, Kaldur set aside the earlier idea of a polished, finished council chamber in favor of a two-tier plan: a wide summons going out immediately to every goliath clan and every town or village that wants a seat (the real regional council, arriving over the following weeks), and a smaller, fast first council — Vosk, Threnn, the party's own household, and Kessick's Ford's leadership by rider — to convene within days of Threnn's people reaching Urskelde. Kaldur committed to sending Ninebells a summons too, unprompted, despite his own exile from them. Rider left for Thundertree the same day. Darkhawk chose to tell Kaldur the news himself, privately, that night, rather than leave him to walk in blind.

**The council chamber, re-scoped:** foundation, boundary stones, and Sael's boundary trees were already in from before the expedition; the stonework itself had stalled for lack of anyone at Urskelde who actually knows old goliath-kin craft. With Threnn's people offering to help build it themselves, Kaldur expects the remaining work to go from months to about a week once they arrive and are willing.

**Perrin and Greta, briefed:** Darkhawk told them about the richer sky-iron vein Kethrun pointed out near the Standing Ring — worked by the same ancient tradition, not merely rediscovered secondhand the way Urskelde's own vein was. Both were already deep in reforging the party's gear (Ysolde's mace nearly finished; Sael's quarterstaff done, her sickle proving difficult since sky-iron resists a curved edge). The real news landed harder once Darkhawk explained that Threnn's own people are the literal source tradition, not just distant descendants — Perrin asked for time with whoever among them still holds the old smithing craft once they arrive; Greta asked for the same, and to fold it into the table work if any of them have a hand for that too.

**The council table, designed:** with the summons now open to every willing clan and town rather than a fixed guest list, Mara estimated roughly thirty as a reasonable starting count given what's already known (Thundertree, Ninebells, the two clans already tied to Vosk from Duskgate, Kessick's Ford, possibly Fallow Cross, Threnn's people themselves). Ysolde pushed back gently on treating that as a hard ceiling — a fixed number risks becoming exactly the kind of quiet limit Darkhawk has spent the whole campaign refusing to set. Settled: a core ring of thirty seats, built by Greta to extend cleanly if more answer the summons than expected.

**Joss and Corren, found doing good work unasked:** two of the captives freed at Duskgate's market have made real places for themselves at Urskelde — Joss running the stores, Corren leading what hunters the settlement has. Joss laid out the math plainly: current stores can't stretch to feed Threnn's arriving community for long. Darkhawk's answer was the warded hunting glade Kethrun had pointed out near the Standing Ring, rich enough to feed Urskelde for years — with one condition stated plainly: hunted with respect, never stripped clean. Corren asked to learn the glade's proper rites from Threnn's people directly rather than guess at them, and Darkhawk agreed to wait for that guidance before anyone hunts there.

**Vale and Merrick, told the truth:** Darkhawk had Ysolde confirm there was no magical reason Vale and Merrick couldn't meet face to face — the succession danger was always tied to Duskgate itself, not proximity to each other. Vale told Merrick himself, plainly, that he'd spent years quietly grooming him as the bargain's successor without ever asking or telling him. Merrick's fury nearly became violence — stopped only when Darkhawk challenged him directly (Intimidation 15 vs DC 14, a near thing) that striking Vale in blind rage would just be Vale's shaping finishing its work, not a choice of Merrick's own. In the moment that followed, Darkhawk's own rage rose dangerously close to the surface answering Merrick's question about how he masters it (WIS save 12 vs DC 15, failed — the rage broke through visibly before he willed it back down by choice rather than force), which landed harder as a real demonstration than any words could have. Mara spoke honestly to what she's actually witnessed of Darkhawk's rage — both its real cost and his repeated choice to master it rather than let it own him.

**A path forward for both captives, not full freedom:** Merrick will train under guard with Threnn's people once they arrive (to understand the same discipline he just watched firsthand) and spend time with Ysolde. Vale asked to be put to real, difficult work rather than comfortable idleness — assigned to logistics under Joss specifically, knowing Joss came through the very market his bargain ran. Darkhawk was plain about it: redemption isn't supposed to be easy, and justice worth the name doesn't avoid the hard, uncomfortable parts.

**The news, given to Kaldur privately:** exactly as promised, Darkhawk told him everything that night rather than leave him to walk in blind — Kethrun's confirmation that "the one above" is coming, not if but when (though likely its champions or soldiers first, not the entity itself); worst case, months rather than years; and the still-unknown fate of Kethrun's other sealed sites, any of which failing unseen could matter as much as the Standing Ring itself. Kaldur refused to be shielded from carrying it, same as their standing agreement, and reframed the wide summons plainly: not just a good idea anymore, but the region's actual defense, everyone unified before whatever's coming arrives. Also told him about Vale and Merrick's new arrangement; Kaldur will work out proper guard rotations with Idra.

**Ysolde rides for Thundertree:** sent personally, at Darkhawk's request, to invite Sesha, Harek, and Thyra to come see what's been built.

**A quiet close with Sael:** found tending her grove, caught up on the day's news, and asked honestly how she was after the Ring. She named the offering plainly — not really about the spirit at all, but finally admitting her isolation had been chosen, not just circumstance — and that tonight, being sought out and asked rather than left to her own quiet, was the first real proof the change wasn't a mistake. Also raised, gently, that her new bond with a spirit of stone might eventually help investigate whatever forced the ancient village's elders to freeze their people — not tonight, but worth remembering. Darkhawk offered standing company, in words or silence, whenever she needs it.

**Ends the day:** Urskelde settled and quiet, every truth that mattered told to the people who needed to hear it, nobody carrying tonight's weight alone. Open threads carried forward: what "the one above" actually is and what shape its champions might take, the condition of Kethrun's other sealed sites, what forced the ancient village's elders to freeze their entire people and whether it's still out there, who betrayed Darkhawk at Ashpit, and the full regional summons now underway.

## Session 20 — 2026-07-24

**The Ashpit betrayal, finally pursued properly:** with Ysolde away at Thundertree, Darkhawk went to Vale and Sael instead. Vale, now working logistics under Joss rather than sitting confined, was visibly more willing to talk — he wasn't yet Warden when Ashpit closed, but his predecessor once called it "the closure that couldn't be explained away": a fighter who should have died didn't, the crowd turned on the house instead of demanding blood, and whoever engineered the betrayal vanished from the Circuit's own records that same week, never resurfacing anywhere trackable.

Sael, gently thanked for having waited until Darkhawk was actually ready to ask, finally shared what she's never told anyone: she reached Ashpit in bear-shape that night, pulled there by what she now understands was the bear-spirit itself, and arrived just after whoever did it had already gone. No face — same as Darkhawk's own fragmented memory — but a bear's senses held onto an impression a human's wouldn't have: a scent like a storm about to break, a limp favoring the left leg, and someone tall, lean, and controlled — moving like they'd disappeared cleanly before, not like ordinary hired muscle.

Real progress, not a resolution — the Ashpit ledger fragment still hasn't been gone through in detail, and Ysolde's help recovering more of Darkhawk's own memory is still days off.

**The whole shape of it, reframed:** talking it through with Mara, then Vale again, the pieces reassembled into something far more specific than "a betrayal." Mara caught the detail Darkhawk had misremembered — the crowd's mercy meant his opponent *lived*, not died — and reframed the real question as who that opponent was. Vale's predecessor had also mentioned the match itself was specifically commissioned, not an ordinary pairing, by someone with real outside influence. Darkhawk put the last piece together himself: it was a hit, not a fight — his opponent was meant to die quietly, made to look like an ordinary pit casualty, and Darkhawk was chosen specifically *because* he wasn't known for mercy, a guaranteed and deniable kill. The crowd's unplanned mercy ruined it, leaving two loose ends the commissioner never intended: the target, alive, and Darkhawk himself, a witness who'd seen the man's face up close and survived. Ashpit's closure and Darkhawk's own betrayal weren't punishment for a lost fight — they were cleanup for a failed assassination. Vale, visibly shaken by how cleanly his own fragments finally fit together, drew his own parallel to being made into someone else's instrument, and noted plainly that Darkhawk refusing to finish the kill, even unknowingly serving as someone's blade, still counts for something.

Open now: who the intended target actually was and whether he's still alive somewhere, unaware whose mercy saved him; who commissioned the hit and why; and the betrayer's identity, still only a physical impression from Sael and a paper trail that goes cold the same week.

**Communion at Sael's grove:** with Sael staying close and Mara nearby, Darkhawk reached for the bear-spirit directly for the first time since the vow at the Standing Ring — not a ritual borrowed from Vosk this time, just the bond answering on its own. He asked it plainly what impression it kept of the man he fought and of whoever left him for dead. It gave him two: the opponent, remembered almost tenderly, as someone as broken by the pit as Darkhawk was, no malice in him at all; and the betrayer, watched from further off and less clearly, but placed above the pit floor in a screened box, moving with a stillness that had nothing to do with a crowd's bloodlust, carrying a scent of oil and old resin, like incense burned too long in a closed room. Darkhawk laid a hand over his scar and thanked it; the bond answered back, wordless, before receding. Sael confirmed the box itself was real — she remembered it from the treeline that night, dismissed at the time as just money, and regrets not looking closer. Mara read the position for what it likely was: not a gambler's seat, a handler's or owner's.

Taken together with Sael's own account from that night, it reads as two snapshots of the same person at different moments — controlled and perfumed while seated watching the match, then adrenaline-hot, storm-scented, and limping by the time Sael caught his trail up close after the killing was meant to be finished. Controlled build, trained enough to vanish cleanly either way.

**The ledger, gone through properly:** back at camp, Mara led (Investigation 22, Darkhawk helping directly — should have granted advantage from the start) through the Ashpit ledger fragment page by page. Buried past the usual "By the Warden's word" boilerplate, cage seven's entry — Darkhawk's own match — carried a margin notation in a different hand and ink, added after the fact, mostly lost to water damage but distinct from the fist-and-chain sigil seen everywhere else. The same partial mark turned up in the margins of two earlier matches once they knew to look — not a one-off, a pattern, someone keeping a private record of specific fights across weeks or longer, separate from the Warden's own bookkeeping.

**Back down into the old sealed section:** Darkhawk and Mara returned to cage seven itself to check whether the screened box the spirit described was still standing. It was — collapsed and rotted, untouched since the night it closed. Searching it together (Perception 23, Mara's help making the difference), they recovered a small tarnished brass censer still coated in old resin, a scrap of fine dark-blue wool caught on a splinter, and — half-melted into the warped floorboards — a cracked blob of old sealing wax carrying a partial signet, not the fist-and-chain sigil, close enough in shape to the ledger's own margin mark that Mara would bet real coin it's the same hand.

Darkhawk tried standing in the box's own vantage point to force a memory loose (Wisdom check, natural 1) — it backfired hard, vertigo and a slammed mental door instead of anything surfacing, his body's fear response answering before his mind could. Mara talked him through it afterward: this kind of memory doesn't open under deliberate pressure, only on its own terms or with Ysolde's steadier, guided hand.

**Reported to Kaldur:** full account given, including the physical evidence. Kaldur didn't recognize the seal himself, but named Halvard Reeve as the right person to identify it quietly — House Reeve's business runs through exactly this kind of heraldry — and offered to route it to him without looping in the wider council yet. Darkhawk agreed; Kaldur took the wrapped seal fragment to send on.

**Ends here, deliberately unresolved:** Ysolde's still days off at Thundertree, and the memory work will keep until she's back to look properly. Open now: the intended target's identity and fate; who commissioned the hit and why; the betrayer's identity — a composite now (oil and resin, calm control in the box; storm-scent, a left-leg limp, trained flight afterward), physical evidence in hand (censer, fabric, partial seal) but still no name; and whether Halvard can put a name to the seal's shape.

**Ends the session:** the Ashpit thread deliberately set down rather than forced further tonight — evidence gathered, seal fragment already riding for Halvard by morning, memory work waiting on Ysolde. Kaldur caught the party up on Urskelde's own business: the summons already drawing confirmed riders from smaller clans, the council chamber's stonework stalled on Threnn's people (two or three days out), Vale and Merrick quiet and settled under guard, and the compensation line thinning out to almost nothing. A quiet close for Darkhawk and Mara, nothing left unspoken between them tonight.

## Session 21 — 2026-07-24

**Threnn's people arrive:** two days after Kaldur's fresh estimate, the watch sighted the column, and the whole community of roughly three dozen goliath-kin reached Urskelde's gates by midday. Threnn himself, greeting Darkhawk by his birth name, admitted he'd half-expected the promised place to turn out not real — and stood visibly moved to find it was. Settling in took the better part of two days: Joss handled housing with practiced ease; Perrin and Greta found instant kinship with Threnn's own smiths at the forge; Corren spent both days walking the glade's boundary with Threnn's hunters; Sael quietly took in a pair of Threnn's elders who began helping tend her grove without being asked; and one of Threnn's own trainers sought out Merrick unprompted, leaving him visibly steadier afterward.

**Vosk arrives:** riders from Thundertree reached Urskelde the following evening. Darkhawk greeted her with a full bow, which she brushed off in favor of taking his face in her hands the way she did the night his identity was confirmed. Seeing Urskelde whole — walls, chamber, cairns, Threnn's people already settling in — she called it "not just held ground, but something worth calling by a real name."

Darkhawk gave her the same grave account already given Kaldur: Kethrun, the ancient wound, the seal being tested since Duskgate, a worst-case timeline of months, and at least three other sealed sites of unknown condition. Vosk recognized her own cairn-verse for what it had actually been warning against all along — "what sleeps beneath stone does not dream kindly" — and pushed for the first council to convene within days rather than wait on full formality.

**House Corvane:** Tomas, Halvard, and Magistrate Kell arrived from Kessick's Ford three days after word reached them. Halvard brought the seal fragment's identification in person: the reconstructed wing-and-blade signet belongs to **House Corvane**, old Ravenshollow nobility, faded from public affairs for at least a generation. An estate reportedly still stands on Ravenshollow's northern edge, nominally occupied by a skeleton household — worth walking past in person when there's time. No living name attached yet, and Halvard was careful not to overclaim a link between Corvane and the Warden's bargain beyond both tracing back to the same city.

**The first council, held:** convened that evening in the half-finished council chamber — Vosk, Threnn and two of his elders, Kaldur, Tomas, Halvard, Kell, Mara, and Sael, with Urskelde's own guards and workers explicitly invited to stand in and be heard. Darkhawk opened by thanking Urskelde's ordinary workers by name-of-role first, before any grave business, and framed tonight as the fast first tier of the two-tier council plan, with the wider regional council still to follow.

He laid out the full account to the whole room at once — Kethrun, the wound, the timeline, the other sealed sites, Threnn's people as living proof of the cost — then raised the Ashpit/Corvane thread alongside it, naming his own suspicion the two might be connected. Halvard and Kell both counseled caution: a shared city isn't yet proof of a shared hand, worth pulling on but not yet worth naming as fact. Threnn agreed it was at least reason to watch the name closely.

**Questions from the floor**, guards and workers included, as promised:
- **Sera (gate watch)** asked what to do if the threat's agents arrive before the summons is answered. Darkhawk's answer: build, fortify, live — pointed the room at what they'd already built as reason not to let fear take that from them.
- **Borin (Threnn's elder)** asked about a real plan to check the other sealed sites. Darkhawk committed the council's combined reach (all goliath tribes being summoned) to locating and securing them, and Borin suggested asking Kethrun directly rather than assuming three is the whole count.
- **Joss (stores)** asked whether tonight changes day-to-day planning. Darkhawk turned it back on him — fight or flee, if the enemy were at the gate tonight — and Joss chose fight, and to keep building as planned.
- **Magistrate Kell** asked for a concrete timeline. Darkhawk set it firmly: **one week from tonight, the full regional council convenes** — matching what was already sent out in the original summons.
- **Bren (mason)** asked how Threnn's people's customs get honored without conflict. Darkhawk answered with a mason's own metaphor — build on a sound foundation rather than tearing down and starting over — which visibly landed with him professionally.
- **Mira (freed captive, 16)** asked whether people like her would be expected to fight or told to flee. Darkhawk asked what *she* wanted; she said fight, having spent years having things happen to her rather than choosing. He answered that the choice is hers alone to make either way — to fight for her home or help in other ways — and it visibly moved both her and several other freed captives nearby.
- **Wren (garrison quartermaster, 20 years)** asked who leads if Darkhawk doesn't come back. Rather than answer for the room, Darkhawk turned the deeper question back on it — asking whether he'd ever actually been named leader, or simply acted as one — and opened the floor for anyone to put their own name forward. Nobody did. Kaldur volunteered himself honestly as the practical continuity (not a ruler, "the one who knows where the grain stores are"), and Threnn and Vosk both framed the silence itself as the real answer: trust freely given and freely renewable, not a title. Wren accepted it plainly.

**Closing the council:** Darkhawk asked Vosk, Threnn, and Kaldur to coordinate with the other tribes and consult Kethrun directly to locate the remaining sealed sites, and closed by telling every hunter, miner, guard, and mason in the room they were the most important people in Urskelde and that he needed them, not just their labor. It landed hard across the room — Sera, Bren, Joss, and Wren all visibly moved.

**Walking the settlement:** rather than end the night there, Darkhawk walked every corner alone, deliberately giving space to anyone too nervous to speak up in the full chamber. An old net-mender thanked him quietly for her grandson's new place among the trainees. A young deputy asked honestly what happens to Vale long-term; Darkhawk gave him an honest "not decided yet" rather than a confident lie. At the grove's edge, Merrick admitted, unprompted, that some part of him still isn't sure he wouldn't want to kill Vale given the chance, and that nobody had asked him that directly since the truth came out. Darkhawk told him honestly he hadn't asked because he trusted Merrick to say so himself when it mattered — which seemed to land better than any reassurance would have.

**Ends the session:** Urskelde now hosting Threnn's entire community, Vosk and Kessick's Ford's leadership both present and briefed, a full regional council formally set for one week from tonight, a task delegated to locate the other sealed sites via Kethrun and the wider tribes, and a settlement that heard its leader mean it when he said he needed every one of them. Open threads carried forward: the Ashpit betrayer's identity and House Corvane's possible connection, the intended target's fate, the condition of Kethrun's other sealed sites, what's above Malphestor and how close it's gotten, and what the full regional council will decide in a week's time.

## Session 22 — 2026-07-24

**Ysolde returns, family in tow:** four days after Vosk's own arrival, Ysolde rode back into Urskelde leading Sesha, Harek, and Thyra — not hard to convince, in the end; Sesha hadn't asked whether it was safe, only when they could leave. Ysolde herself looked worn thin from the road (dozed off in the saddle twice, by her own admission) and was sent straight off to eat and rest, but not before quietly flagging that she'd want to examine the Ashpit evidence properly, and try again at the memory work, once Darkhawk was ready — not tonight, but soon.

**Reunion at the gate:** Sesha closed the distance on foot before anyone else dismounted. Rurik, half-joking that a hug wasn't offered, got tackled properly by all three at once — Sesha not letting go, Harek's steady hand on his shoulder, Thyra half-laughing that she'd gotten "a mountain for a brother."

**The Old Ashpit, shown first, deliberately:** Rurik chose to take his family to where it all began before anywhere else, telling them plainly not to be angry or sad — it's part of him. Standing on the old pit floor, he answered each of them in turn: told Thyra the truth of his years there (allowed out roughly eight hours a day between training and fighting, about thirteen years total, starting as a child around age fifteen by his own rough count backward); told Sesha, when she asked, that the family never stopped searching for him even after the elders called him lost. He laid the whole reframed Ashpit betrayal out for Harek — the commissioned hit, the crowd's mercy ruining it, House Corvane's seal recovered from the box — and was plain about his intent: "my justice," not the pit's or any lord's, once Corvane's involvement is confirmed. Harek accepted that answer on the condition it never curdles into the same thing Rurik's spent his life fighting free of. Rurik also credited the bear-spirit itself for the crowd's mercy that night — it likely didn't need to sway many, just one or two, for the rest of a crowd's bloodlust to follow the lead.

The hardest moment was Sesha's own question, asked plainly: whether there was memory work with Ysolde that Rurik hadn't told her about. He confirmed it honestly — there is a memory still locked away, and he wants it unlocked. Sesha, relieved rather than hurt at being told directly, asked only to be present when it happens. Not tonight, but soon.
