**Threnn's Quarter, and a debt refused:** Rurik brought the family to eat among Threnn's people, using the meal to explain the ancient village's whole story on the way. Threnn himself, and the elder Bettha, met Sesha, Harek, and Thyra with open delight, tracing old Giant naming conventions and shared customs (the winter vigil, still kept at Thundertree in diminished form) back to a common root older than either people remembered clearly. When Threnn named it a debt his people owed for being found at all, Rurik refused the framing outright — "no debt between family" — and offered his forearm. Threnn met the clasp in the old grip, visibly moved, and settled instead on "kin, plainly said." The evening turned easy after that — food, stories, Thyra badly losing a goliath drinking game.

**The forge, and a father finding his hands again:** Rurik peeled Harek away to meet Perrin and Greta, who welcomed him instantly over sky-iron craft. Harek's careful, weighing composure fell away almost immediately once actual stonework and metalwork were in front of him — genuine praise for Greta's reforged fittings, real curiosity about the ore itself, and an open invitation from Perrin to keep coming back. Rurik collected Sesha and Thyra from Threnn's fire to walk back for him afterward; Sesha, watching him absorbed at the anvil, said quietly it was the first time all day he'd looked like himself.

**A quiet doorway moment:** waiting outside the forge, Sesha named her own ongoing fear plainly — that loving someone who runs toward the fire never really stops being frightening — and called it a fair trade anyway for the chance to watch her family be fully themselves in a place her son built. Rurik didn't dismiss the fear; he let it stand alongside the good of the day.

**Joss, and a joke that landed soft:** introduced as the reason Urskelde eats, Joss met the family warmly, naming plainly what Rurik gave him after Duskgate's market — a reason to get up in the morning. Rurik's fox-in-the-henhouse joke about Harek got genuine laughs all around and a loaf pressed into Harek's hands anyway.

**The council chamber, at night:** Rurik framed it to his family as the settlement's brain, after the forge (heart) and the stores (stomach). Harek gave the foundation work his real, earned approval — sound enough, in his words, to hold "three lifetimes of arguing" — a message meant for Threnn directly. Asked by Sesha whether Rurik would really hold the region together, he answered without hedging: not try, *will* — "if I have to grab hold of it and drag it up to my face and force it to listen to me." Sesha's answer, half warning and half pride, drew the line between holding something together and choking it; Harek backed the sentiment without reservation. Rurik named Mara as the steady hand and conscience behind that resolve.

**Sael's grove, and a bear for Thyra:** Sael welcomed the family in, and — with Rurik's blessing left entirely to Thyra's choice — shifted into her bear-shape for her. Thyra's composure lasted about two seconds before an undignified yelp, recovered instantly into deliberate bravado, and ended in genuine delighted awe with both hands in the fur. Rurik's booming laugh at her expense only made her more determined to prove herself, which she did.

**Home, and Mara:** the family arrived at Rurik and Mara's own house last, by design. Mara met Sesha with an embrace and the word "daughter," met Harek and Thyra with the same easy warmth, and the evening closed around shared food and wine Mara distills herself. Watching the room — Sesha and Mara talking easily, Harek eating his second dinner, Thyra recounting the bear encounter to anyone who'd listen — Rurik let himself feel, without deflecting it this time, that he'd never truly believed this moment would come. A few tears fell, quietly, and Mara caught his eye across the room without making anything of it.

**Staying for the council, and a plan for tomorrow:** asked if they'd stay for the full regional council three days out, Sesha answered before anyone else could — they weren't leaving before the part that mattered. Harek and Thyra agreed just as readily. The next day was mapped out before the night ended: Rurik will start teaching Thyra the basics of fighting; Sesha will spend the day with Mara; Harek will work alongside Threnn's own masons.

**Ends the session:** Darkhawk's family under one roof with Mara for the first time, committed to staying through the full regional council, and Ysolde back within reach for the still-pending memory work. Open threads carried forward unchanged: the Ashpit betrayer's identity and House Corvane's possible connection (now with Ysolde available to help recover the memory itself), the intended target's fate, the condition of Kethrun's other sealed sites, and what the full regional council will decide in three days' time.

## Session 23 — 2026-07-24

**Dawn training with Thyra:** Rurik woke her before sunrise, deliberately picking the hardest hour to teach her that failure is just information. Her first stance was flat-footed and easy to sweep — he showed her why by sweeping it, twice, the second time baiting a feint at her front foot to punish her overcorrected back foot instead. She recovered fast, absorbing the actual lesson underneath both falls: a feint isn't about the target, it's about making someone defend the wrong thing. Handed a practice dagger next, she worked through grip, a hip-led stab (badly overthought on the first real attempt, clean and fast once she stopped consciously conducting it), and finally a live strike against Rurik's own chest — controlled, accurate, stopped exactly at the point of contact. He answered it by demonstrating what happens next: catching her wrist mid-strike, pulling her off-balance, and dropping her a fourth time, driving home that a landed blow means nothing without a plan for what comes after — "you plan two steps ahead."

**Handed off to Kaldur and Idra:** Rurik brought Thyra to the garrison afterward rather than keep teaching her solo. Kaldur agreed to run her through proper formation basics himself, warned she'd come back "sore, humbled, and better for it," and handed her to Idra's drill line without ceremony. Rurik left her to finish the full session rather than pull her early, watching briefly from the wall as she visibly struggled and visibly held her line better than she had an hour before.

**The forge, and a father reunited with his hands:** Rurik brought Harek to meet Perrin and Greta, who welcomed him instantly over sky-iron craft. Harek's guarded composure dropped almost immediately once real stonework and metalwork were in front of him; Perrin and Greta both praised his eye, and the visit ended with an open invitation to keep coming back.

**The council chamber, and Harek's professional verdict:** brought later to see the half-built chamber, Harek gave Threnn's foundation work genuine, earned approval — sound enough to "hold three lifetimes of arguing" — a message meant to travel back to Threnn directly, which it later did over lunch alongside some good-natured mutual ribbing about who was teaching whom.

**Family lunch, full table:** Sesha, Mara, Harek, and Threnn (invited on the spot) gathered at Rurik and Mara's home; Thyra arrived last, wrecked and grinning from Idra's drills, to good-natured teasing from the whole table about the soreness still to come.

**Council prep with Kaldur:** numbers confirmed — Kessick's Ford in full, Kell's contingent, two of Vosk's Duskgate-allied clans, a Fallow Cross representative, several smaller mountain families reaching out unprompted after word of Threnn's people spread. Ninebells hasn't answered; Kaldur sent the summons anyway and left the silence to mean what it means. Turnout now likely exceeds the original thirty-seat estimate — Greta's already building extension panels.

**A deliberate invitation to House Corvane:** rather than fold Corvane quietly into the wide summons, Rurik chose to send a direct, sealed invitation naming Urskelde plainly — reasoning that "Rurik" means nothing to whoever ran the hit on cage seven's fighter, who almost certainly believes that loose end died with the rest of Ashpit. Kaldur pushed back only to understand the reasoning, then helped shape it: a message as much as a tactic, inviting the past back onto ground it once tried to bury, on Darkhawk's own terms this time.

**Merrick and Vale, checked on:** Merrick visibly steadier under one of Threnn's trainers, no longer flinching at Vale's name. Vale remains consistent, hardworking, and untested against the wards — trusted with the numbers, not yet with comfort, by his own choice as much as anyone else's.

**A full security plan set:** weapons checked at the gate for all delegates (old, respectable custom, not an insult), household and garrison remaining armed throughout, Darkhawk's own blades kept close but sheathed, Mara/Ysolde/Sael seated at his side rather than scattered through the room. Doubled watch, a hidden reserve under Idra, riders on the approach roads a day out, Kethrun and Torvald's warning line kept open, Vale and Merrick's wards re-checked the morning of, and — Darkhawk's own addition — double rations for the garrison, on the principle that a well-fed guard is an alert one.

**The Ashpit memory, finally recovered:** with Kaldur's business handled, Rurik asked Ysolde to gather at Sael's grove that evening — Mara, Sesha, and Sael all present. Ysolde was plain about the difference from Duskgate: not forcing the door, just holding steady while he knocked instead of kicked. Seated in a chalk circle as dusk fell, Rurik broke through where the solo attempt at Session 20 had failed outright. The memory returned whole: the crowd's refusal, the mercy chosen, and — for the first time — a face glimpsed in the same instant, looking up toward the screened box as the screen itself slipped. Lean, sharp-featured, pale, dark hair cropped close, unnervingly still inside a screaming crowd. A silver pin at the collar, worked as a wing folded over a blade — an exact match to House Corvane's own signet. A half-heard address near the box surfaced too, something short and familiar rather than a full name or title.

Ysolde recorded the full description on the spot, word for word, while it was fresh. The group's fear at hearing Corvane had already been personally invited eased once Rurik pointed out the obvious: the man in the box has no reason to know cage seven's fighter is the same man welcoming him to Urskelde. Sael offered to confirm by scent alone if he actually walks through the gate; Mara and Ysolde folded the discovery straight into the council's security planning.

**A working theory, unconfirmed:** turning the pieces over aloud, Rurik connected Corvane's own name to raven and corvid imagery, then reached further — the Raven Queen, death and fate bound together. Ysolde took the thought seriously rather than dismissing it, laying out what she actually knows of that faith (ravens as messengers, patience, an absence of mercy for anyone trying to cheat what's owed) and agreeing it could explain how calculated and deliberate the whole affair was — a killing arranged to look like fate rather than murder. Nothing confirmed; she'll look into it quietly before the council.

**Ends the night:** description sent to Halvard, House Corvane's invitation already riding, and Rurik rising from the circle with the bear-spirit close enough to the surface that everyone present could see it — steady fire behind his eyes rather than rage, a promise rather than a threat. Two days now stand between here and the full regional council: Thyra mid-way through real training, the family settled and staying, and for the first time since Ashpit closed, an actual face to the man who left him for dead.

## Session 24 — 2026-07-25

**The morning of the council:** two days passed quickly — Thyra's soreness came and went exactly as promised, delegates began arriving early, and no word came back from either Halvard or House Corvane before the day itself. Rurik asked Mara to stay at his side the whole day and keep watch; she agreed without hesitation. The household — Mara, Ysolde, Sael — gathered together deliberately, walking the settlement as a visible, unified group before heading to the gate to watch arrivals, then choosing the council chamber itself as the proper place for greetings rather than the gate.

**The chamber, full:** over forty delegates filled Greta's extended table — Kessick's Ford, Thundertree, Threnn's people, several of Vosk's Duskgate-allied clans, Fallow Cross, and a number of smaller mountain families who'd joined the summons unprompted after word of the ancient village spread. Rurik's household took their planned seats — Mara, Ysolde, and Sael close at his side, his own blades sheathed on a stand behind his chair, weapons checked at the gate for every other delegate. Rurik let the room's own chatter run until it settled into total silence entirely on its own before rising to speak.

**The history lesson:** Rurik opened by naming plainly why people had come — curiosity, skepticism, or simple interest in the man at the center of the invitation — and disarmed the room early with humor (a promised "quiz" callback that ran through the whole speech) before walking them, deliberately, through Ashpit's history: its mysterious closure thirteen years ago, the Circuit that ran beneath ordinary commerce across the region, Ravenshollow's own winter fare as one of its unremarked fronts, and a training pit built specifically for survivors rather than mere fighters. He called every survivor and defender of the Ashpit's fall to stand and be recognized — some two dozen people rose across the room, household to garrison to freed captives to grieving Thundertree families, Yorik Stonebrand among them for his sons. Rurik named the dead plainly and let a real, unhurried silence hold before continuing: "we honour them by carrying on the fight."

**The reveal:** Rurik told the room the pit sat directly beneath the very chamber they were seated in, then asked plainly who in the room had helped end it — before turning the thread back on himself. He confirmed he was cage seven's fighter, the boy who refused a kill the crowd itself rejected and was stabbed through the chest for it, saved after the fact by Sael in bear-shape. He let the bear-spirit visibly manifest in front of the whole council — the same fire behind the eyes shown privately at Sael's grove two nights before, now witnessed by forty strangers, Sael rising in answering kinship beside him.

**Lord Edrin Corvane confronted:** seated among the Ravenshollow-adjacent delegates throughout, Corvane's practiced stillness had already begun cracking under Rurik's earlier pointed pauses. Addressed directly by the private name "Raven," his composure broke entirely. Given the floor rather than condemned outright — Rurik's own standing promise that everyone gets a voice — Corvane confessed fully: he was the one in the screened box, the hit was inherited family duty rather than personal malice, and House Corvane has kept faith with **the Raven Queen** for generations, where a death made to look like fate rather than murder counts as service rather than crime. He named his own father as the one who held the obligation before him, and admitted he never learned the name of Rurik's original opponent nor whether the man survived.

**Redemption offered, not vengeance:** Rurik drew the same line he drew for Malphestor — "I offered a devil redemption, we are not done yet" — refusing to let thirteen years end in blood after everything the room had just witnessed. Given the free choice to leave or stay as a guest, Corvane chose to stay, offering his house's own records on the Raven Queen's old faith for examination. The room's mood shifted from dread to something closer to hard-won respect by the end of the exchange.

**The wider threat, laid out whole:** with the personal reckoning settled, Rurik tied every thread together for the room — Malphestor, the broken anchor, the Raven Queen, and "the one above" Malphestor, now starved of every banked soul it lost in an instant and actively hunting for a new foothold. He asked the room plainly what they wanted to do about it, then sat and waited on their answer rather than dictate one. Kell pledged Kessick's Ford's full support — riders, supplies, and formal magistrate authority. Threnn pledged his people's strength toward finding and defending Kethrun's remaining sealed sites. Vosk pledged Thundertree's hunters and watch fires. Smaller mountain families and Fallow Cross's representative followed in turn. A genuine regional alliance stood by the time the formal proceedings closed — the actual purpose the summons was sent for, finally realized.

**Aftermath:** the council dissolved into easier conversation once Rurik closed with the long-promised "quiz" joke, breaking the last of the tension into real laughter. He slipped out for air with Mara shortly after, the weight of the day catching up all at once — dropping to his knees outside the chamber and letting out everything he'd held back in the room itself, Mara holding him through it without trying to quiet it. Sesha, Harek, and Thyra joined them outside once word reached them; Harek, asked plainly whether Rurik had done the right thing, answered without hesitation that he had — the harder kind of wall to build, but the kind that holds a whole region up rather than just keeping one man out. Rurik closed the night with Thyra specifically, tying her morning's lesson to what she'd just watched him do: sometimes fighting isn't enough — knowing when not to fight matters more.

**A milestone earned:** the whole party leveled up together for resolving the Ashpit betrayal and forging the regional alliance — Rurik to Barbarian 10 (Totemic Attunement), Mara to Ranger 14 (Vanish), Sael to Druid 12 (ASI, WIS to 20), Ysolde to Cleric 13 (a 7th-level spell slot, proficiency to +5).

**A private reckoning with Corvane:** once the household had gone home, Rurik had Kaldur and Corvane's attendant clear the room and sat down alone with the man who'd once had him killed — not to threaten him further, but to actually talk. Corvane, given the space, filled in what the council hadn't had time for: he never touched a blade himself that night, only watched and verified from the box, the actual killing arranged through a separate hire — a professional, working name possibly **"Greyfeather,"** trained enough to vanish from every record the same week and never resurface. The order to target Rurik specifically had come down oddly precise, naming his cage number before the match was ever scheduled, raising a new, unanswered question about whether something more than his fighting record had marked him. House Corvane's attempt to re-locate Rurik's spared opponent afterward collapsed in the same chaos that closed Ashpit for good — the trail's been cold for thirteen years. Corvane also confirmed the real cost of that failure: his own house's fall from public standing wasn't ordinary scandal, but disgrace inside the Raven Queen's own faith for an unsettled debt, inherited directly onto him.

Pressed on the Raven Queen herself, Corvane offered his honest, limited read: her faith values order and settled debts, not the hunger and desperation driving whatever sits above Malphestor — if anything, she'd likely see that thing as an affront to her own domain rather than a kindred force. Rurik, working through his own long-running philosophy of justice as balance between good and evil rather than one-sided dominance, refused Corvane's offer to accept any punishment up to death, naming it plainly as revenge rather than justice. He set real terms instead: Corvane's father's notes handed over in full, a personal letter to the temple requesting anything known about the wider threat, and House Corvane's remaining strength placed at Urskelde's service. Corvane accepted without hesitation, visibly moved rather than merely relieved. The two parted on Rurik's own terms — not friendship, but a partnership, sealed with a handshake neither man entered lightly.

**The next morning:** Rurik told his family everything the household had already heard — Greyfeather, the unsettlingly precise targeting order, Corvane's read on the Raven Queen, the terms set — and Sesha named the "specifically targeted" detail as no coincidence she liked the shape of. Asked how long the family meant to stay, Harek admitted he hadn't looked past the council (Threnn's smiths still have plenty to teach him), Sesha wanted to travel back with Vosk rather than alone, and Thyra flatly refused to leave until Idra pronounced her "not hopeless."

**A new role for Thyra:** Rurik asked her seriously — complete her training at Urskelde, then return to Thundertree to train the next generation the same way she's being trained now, with Rurik speaking to Vosk to arrange it properly. Thyra hesitated, doubting she was ready to teach anyone after only days of learning herself, until Harek pointed out that remembering what not-knowing feels like matters more than already being the best. She accepted, wanting to actually be good at it first rather than fake competence in front of children who'd see through her. Rurik framed the ask plainly: Urskelde is what he's building, but Thundertree is still his home, and he wants it as ready as Urskelde is for whatever's coming.

**A quieter morning session:** with the full council dispersed, Rurik held a smaller, formal Q&A in the chamber for delegates with overnight second thoughts — Halvard, Tomas, and mountain clan elder Bryn Hollowmere among them. Discussed privately with Halvard: rather than announce House Corvane's alliance publicly, Rurik proposed using Corvane's house and its shadow connections as a quiet intelligence network, and named **Halvard Reeve as Urskelde's spymaster** on the spot — tasked with recruiting a couple of trusted, unremarkable people at Urskelde to relay findings, working both his own legitimate Ravenshollow contacts and Corvane's less legitimate ones. Halvard accepted, already turning "Greyfeather" into his first assignment. Bryn asked plainly about the Raven Queen; Rurik answered honestly that he expected no conflict given her domain's apparent opposition to what's above Malphestor, but that gods are unpredictable and the household won't stop watching regardless. Tomas raised Greyfeather as a shared concern rather than Rurik's alone, which Rurik agreed with plainly — any information on him is good information for everyone.

**Ends the session:** House Corvane's betrayal resolved after thirteen years, not with a killing but with a confession and an open door; a real regional alliance formed against the threat above Malphestor; the whole party leveled up together; a new intelligence network quietly stood up under Halvard; Thyra given a real future purpose at Thundertree; and Darkhawk's family holding him together in the dark once the hardest day of the campaign so far was finally, fully over. Open threads carried forward: the identity and fate of Rurik's original opponent, the working name "Greyfeather" and why Rurik was named specifically rather than chosen generically, how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen actually runs, locating and securing Kethrun's remaining sealed sites with the alliance's full backing, and what's above Malphestor still searching for a way in.

## Session 25 — 2026-07-26

**A walk, a confession, a theory:** unable to sleep easily after the council and the private reckoning with Corvane, Rurik asked Mara to walk with him and voice a thought that had been building since the confrontation — that every enemy the party's faced has felt like a probing test rather than a true attempt to end them: the corrupted wardens, the lone scout at Hollowmere, even the market cut at Duskgate, none of it the overwhelming strike an entity capable of tearing sealed wounds should be able to land. Mara took the theory seriously rather than talking him down from it, working through it alongside him — that whatever's testing them either hasn't been ready to commit, or is still deciding whether the party's worth the real cost of stopping outright. They chose to sleep out under the stars near Sael's grove rather than head back inside.

**A dream, and "the count":** reaching for the bear-spirit's connection in sleep, Rurik received a fragmented but unmistakable impression — a vast, patient sense of scale, and a single recurring idea: *counting*, the same word that's turned up before in Vale's dead-drop letter and the tally cord taken off a mine guard, except older and colder than either. The bear-spirit's presence answered the counting with a wordless, ancient refusal — not a promise of safety, a promise that whatever's counting doesn't get to simply collect what it's owed uncontested. Rurik woke at dawn certain of one thing: *something has been counting him since before he can remember, and the bear-spirit has stood between him and that count the whole time.*

**The theory built out, with Mara, Sael, and Ysolde:** woken and gathered at the grove, the four worked the idea through from every angle. Ysolde connected "the count" to her order's own theology of debts and judgment — a ledger is kept because someone intends to collect. Sael offered a hunter's read: counting readiness, not just debt. Rurik pushed it further than either — that the raid that took him as a child wasn't random (a raid that killed two elders and a hunter, and took exactly one child), that Ashpit's true purpose was cultivation rather than cruelty (thirteen years spent ensuring that when the moment came, he'd have every reason to finish a kill), and that his refusal to do exactly that may have been the single most consequential choice of the entire war so far — the one outcome the whole design was built to prevent. He connected it further still to Malphestor's own broken ledger: the devil's visible surprise at the scrying mirror wasn't just shock, it was a predictive model failing, because Rurik had stopped being predictable the moment he started thinking instead of reacting. Final and largest leap, reached together: the bear-spirit isn't just protecting him indefinitely — it's holding "the count" at zero, a true, active balance point, and every bond the party's formed since (Mara's, Sael's, Ysolde's own primal offerings) may be part of the same old alliance Kethrun described being quietly rebuilt, piece by piece, with Rurik being prepared not just to survive the count, but to eventually help hold it himself.

**Kept deliberately quiet:** Rurik chose not to let the theory outrun what could actually be verified — asked Ysolde to keep the Raven Queen/Corvane research (physical evidence) prioritized over pure theory, and asked all three to hold "the count" theory between the four of them rather than spread it through the household until there's something solid enough to act on. The plan to eventually bring it to the wider inner circle — Vosk, Threnn, Kaldur, and Kethrun directly — at the Standing Ring, where the bond runs strongest, was set but deliberately not rushed.

**A day of preparations before the road:** with the family staying on and the household briefed, Rurik spent a full day making the rounds before departing for the Standing Ring — Vosk (who named the region's "held breath" plainly and urged pressing Kethrun directly rather than waiting on scouts), Ysolde (whose theological read on the Raven Queen's subtlety led to an unsettling theory: Rurik's own thirteen-year-blocked memory surfacing right before the council may itself have been her hand, a debt called in on schedule), Sael (whose read on the land — thinned wildlife, a restless bear-spirit — matched Vosk's and Ysolde's from a different angle entirely), and Thyra, formally charged to finish her training under both Idra (formation) and Kaldur (judgment and leadership) and to protect Sesha and Harek while Rurik's away, framed deliberately as a fighter and leader's task rather than a sister's favor.

**Kaldur named commander, Threnn as his council:** at Rurik's request, Threnn agreed to stand beside Kaldur as political adviser during the absence — the trust already real, made official — with Halvard's spy network folded in as another set of eyes. Announced publicly at the gate the next morning to the assembled settlement, alongside Rurik's own reminder that everyone's voice matters whether he's present or not.

**Gear and household squared away:** Perrin and Greta fitted Rurik with a sky-iron open-faced helm (once per long rest, turns a critical hit against him into a normal hit) and reforged Mara's longbow and shortsword in sky-iron to match the rest of the party's weapons, adding a warded waterskin/ration pouch capable of feeding the group from barren ground once per long rest. Joss restocked travel provisions and two potions of healing salvaged from Duskgate's market, took resolved evidence off Rurik's hands for safekeeping, and was given a standing mandate to grow Urskelde's stores on his own judgment — starting with recruiting an alchemist to end the settlement's total dependence on borrowed healing. A comically disastrous attempt by Rurik to serve dinner and reorganize the house solo capped the night before departure.

**Departure and the Standing Ring:** Mara, Sael, and Ysolde set out with Rurik the next morning. En route, a survey of Urskelde's cairns turned up a deliberately planted dampening shard muffling one cairn's ward — removing it backlashed hard on Ysolde (costing her *Sending* for the day) before the ward snapped back to full strength. A bird-like, part-grafted scout struck Rurik once from ambush and withdrew rather than press the fight — confirmed by Sael's tracking and Ysolde's Grave sense as something "built rather than born," not fully alive, reporting back to something else via a crude scrying circle. A follow-up ambush attempt failed (poor stealth and a stiff cover story), though Sael got close enough for a clearer look, confirming the read.

**Kethrun pressed for real answers:** at Rurik's insistence, Kethrun named all three of his other sealed sites for the first time — the Still Throat (a frozen glacial pass north), a nameless valley southeast near Threnn's restored village, and the Drowned Hollow west, sealed in haste and therefore weakest. He also confirmed the sentinels' old sensing-thread between sites broke down gradually over centuries of neglect, not all at once, and could plausibly be rewoven site by site rather than rebuilt from scratch. The party chose the Drowned Hollow first, judging a hastily-sealed site the most urgent regardless of distance.

**Corbin's holding, warded:** en route west, the party met Corbin, a mountain trapper whose family (wife Merra, two sons, his mother) had lost goats and whose dog wouldn't settle — signs matching the same scout's hunting pattern. Ysolde warded the homestead against undead and "built" creatures before moving on; Corbin gave the clearest directions anyone had to "Widow's Still," the local name for the Drowned Hollow.

**The Drowned Hollow, found and the guardian met:** the site matched every warning — a dead treeline stopping abruptly, water that never ripples, old goliath-kin stonework half-submerged at the shore. A careful survey (after the cairn lesson) found no scout sign but confirmed the seal ran erratic and weak. Rurik entered the water alone, bloodline and intent judged the deciding factor, and was seized by **Ilsevet**, an ancient goliath-kin who sealed the wound alone in haste when the rest of her people couldn't reach it in time, and has held it by herself, uncounted centuries, ever since — believing herself entirely forgotten until Rurik named Kethrun. She shared the seam's layout directly: a thin, fragile center to avoid and solid anchor points safe to build from.

**Draining and reinforcing the Hollow:** with Ysolde's *Sending*, Urskelde mobilized fast — Threnn personally leading a crew of masons and diggers, Perrin joining for the stonework. While waiting, the party built a proper forward camp, cleared the old outflow channel's choke point by hand, marked the safe dig zones, and stocked provisions well ahead of the crew's arrival — cutting real time off the project. Threnn met Ilsevet himself, kin recognizing kin across centuries. Rurik's own strength (moving stones that should have needed six people and pulleys) impressed Fira, the lead mason, enough to fold him directly into the crew. A two-day reinforcement ritual — Ysolde at the center, Threnn's ritual-keepers grounding it in true goliath-kin tradition, the bear-spirit anchoring it the way it anchored the Standing Ring's own repair — closed the seam properly for the first time since it was made. Odra, one of Threnn's masons, volunteered to stay on permanently and learn the seam's needs directly from Ilsevet, mirroring Torvald's arrangement with Kethrun.

**A milestone earned:** the whole party leveled up together for finding and relieving Ilsevet and securing the second sealed site with sustainable, shared tending rather than a one-time fix — Rurik to Barbarian 11/Fighter 4 (Relentless Rage), Mara to Ranger 15 (Shadowy Dodge), Sael to Druid 13 (a 7th-level spell slot, proficiency to +5), Ysolde to Cleric 14 (an improved Destroy Undead).

**Ends the session:** the Drowned Hollow closed and properly held for the first time since it was hastily sealed; Ilsevet relieved of centuries of solitary vigil and given real tending going forward; the whole party leveled up together; and two of Kethrun's four known sealed sites now accounted for. Open threads carried forward: the Still Throat and the nameless southeastern valley, still unvisited; the bird-like scout and whatever it reports to, confirmed to be "building" more of itself and testing multiple points at once; "Greyfeather" and the question of why Rurik was targeted specifically; how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen runs; and what's above Malphestor, still searching for a foothold.

## Session 26 — 2026-07-27

**A clean two days on the road:** with Bettha reached by *Sending* for anything she remembered, the party learned the valley's old name — **Sorrow's Hush**, a hollow between twin peaks "shaped like cupped hands" — along with a real warning passed down from her grandmother: something there answers if called by name. Two days of travel followed, unusually smooth after the tension of the road west; deliberate checks each evening confirmed no sign of the scout creature anywhere along the route, the cleanest stretch since leaving Urskelde.

**Sorrow's Hush, entered carefully:** the valley broke from every pattern the party had learned to expect — dense old-growth forest, genuinely alive with birdsong and insects rather than the Drowned Hollow's dead stillness. A careful survey (shard lesson well learned) found no scout sign, confirmed the "aliveness" was real rather than a mask, and turned up old goliath-kin warning-carvings, a worn path leading toward a ring of five moss-grown pillars around a single massive ancient tree, and a weathered marker at the path's edge. Ysolde translated the marker's old Giant script in silence first: a name, **Vessyn** — almost certainly the very name Bettha's warning meant.

**The name spoken, and the guardian met:** rather than approach with the same caution as the marker deserved, Rurik shouted the name aloud. The valley's sound cut out instantly, and the great central tree tore itself half-loose from centuries of stillness — **Vessyn**, an ancient tree-guardian, manifesting fast and clearly displeased at being summoned rather than approached. Rurik, badly frightened by its sheer presence (a failed save even with Bear-Claimed's advantage), refused to run or draw a weapon regardless, standing his ground through visible fear rather than false calm.

**A different kind of site:** Vessyn revealed it guards no extraplanar wound at all — it binds back fear itself, a temptation rather than a tear in the world, set here by the old alliance to hold against the ordinary, universal failure of letting fear choose for the body instead of the will. Impressed that Rurik named his own daily struggle against exactly that honestly rather than claiming false fearlessness, it eased its aggression and asked to hear why the party had come.

**Reweaving the thread, with a twist:** after Rurik explained Kethrun, Ilsevet, and the wider threat, Vessyn agreed to attempt reconnecting to the sentinel network — but worried its own nature (fear-based rather than wound-based) might make the working land differently. Rurik proposed himself as a direct conduit for whatever strain resulted, given his own proven resistance to fear (Bear-Claimed), and chose to enter a deliberate, controlled rage specifically to hold the line between the two emotions rather than let either win. Vessyn, visibly moved by a mortal remembering an old technique it hadn't expected to see again, agreed. The working succeeded cleanly — Ysolde's ritual reaching the thread, Rurik's chosen rage absorbing the strain without breaking, and the connection landing true: Vessyn, Kethrun, and Ilsevet all aware of one another for the first time in centuries.

**A gift given in kind:** Vessyn marked Rurik afterward — once per long rest, he can now grant advantage to an ally's saving throw against being frightened. No physical repair was needed at Sorrow's Hush; unlike the other two sites, this one was never damaged, only isolated.

**A milestone earned:** the whole party leveled up together for finding and reconnecting Sorrow's Hush's guardian to the sentinel network, resolving the third of Kethrun's four known sealed sites — Rurik to Barbarian 12/Fighter 4 (+2 CON, Vessyn's Gift), Mara to Ranger 16 (a second 4th-level spell slot), Sael to Druid 14, Ysolde to Cleric 15 (an 8th-level spell slot).

**Ends the session:** Sorrow's Hush secured without a single blow struck, its guardian's isolation ended and its true nature — a binding on fear itself — understood for the first time in centuries; the sentinel thread now connects three of Kethrun's four known sites; and the whole party leveled up again. Open threads carried forward: the Still Throat, the last unvisited sealed site; the bird-like scout and whatever it reports to; "Greyfeather" and why Rurik was targeted specifically; how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen runs; and what's above Malphestor, still searching for a foothold.

## Session 27 — 2026-07-28

**Back to the Standing Ring first:** before pushing north, the party swung through Kethrun's chamber to check in and outfit properly. Torvald confirmed the Ring had held quiet and steady, though Kethrun himself had gone unusually still for a stretch recently — "listening," by his own account. Pressed on it, Kethrun confirmed a real, grim read: reaching toward the Still Throat through the newly restored thread felt nothing like touching Ilsevet's solitude or Vessyn's isolation. It felt *occupied*. Rurik's own unnamed unease about the site was validated outright rather than dismissed. Torvald outfitted Mara, Sael, and Ysolde with proper glacier gear (furs, ice-picks, crevasse rope) from Threnn's people's stores; Rurik, Goliath and Mountain Born, needed none of it.

**The approach:** two days of hard, honest travel north, the terrain worsening exactly as promised. A scouting flight by Sael (wild-shaped into a snowy owl) confirmed the worst before the party ever got close: the sealed site sat in a wide ice bowl, air visibly cold-warped around it, and at least four pale, deliberately-moving shapes already present — not testing the seal, holding it. A cautious, *Pass without Trace*-aided approach got the party close enough to confirm four undead, patrol-pattern, exact type unknown.

**The Still Throat, fought.** Rurik walked out alone to bait two of the four back into the pass's chokepoint, the same tactic tried (and missed) at the Drowned Hollow — this time it worked cleanly. A hard, five-round fight followed: Mara's ranged damage and Dread Ambusher opening volley, Rurik raging and reckless throughout, Sael alternating between owl-scouting, Moonbeam, and melee strikes, Ysolde's Guiding Bolts and a mid-fight Cure Wounds keeping Rurik standing. The sky-iron helm downgraded one crit early; two more later landed unmitigated but were absorbed by Bear Totem's rage resistance. All four **Frostbound Wardens** destroyed — Rurik ending the fight at 103/161 HP before Ysolde's follow-up healing and a potion brought him back up.

**A darker truth, examined.** Ysolde's post-fight investigation of the wreckage (a natural 20) revealed the Wardens weren't random undead — fragments of goliath-kin bone structure and old ceremonial binding-work, matching Vessyn's own grove craft, confirmed they *were* the Still Throat's original guardians, corrupted and turned rather than simply defeated. A recovered black-iron shard, matching the Urskelde cairn shard's craft, was wrapped and stowed rather than touched directly — lesson learned.

**Borvik, met and lost.** At the exposed, cracked seal — far worse-damaged than either prior site — Rurik made contact with what remained: not a whole presence like Ilsevet or Vessyn, but scattered fragments, barely holding a name (**Borvik**) together. A first stabilization attempt (Ysolde anchoring, Rurik channeling via rage) partially succeeded, letting Borvik describe what took the site: not one thing, but many, "wearing one purpose like a borrowed coat," and — critically — that the wound here **opens down, not up**, unlike Kethrun's own skyward tear. Pushing further to try connecting Borvik to the full sentinel network in one motion overreached; the attempt failed (natural 1), the connection scattering entirely and hitting Rurik with a 17-damage psychic backlash. Borvik's last coherent words absolved Rurik of blame before fading for good: *not your failing, mine — I was already breaking before you found me.*

**Grief, named honestly:** Rurik's anger and guilt over Borvik surfaced plainly rather than being buried — "I failed him, I failed the guardians" — and the household talked him through it the same way they did after the Corvane confrontation, drawing an explicit parallel to the Thundertree entrance dead. No head doctor needed, by his own later joke, just family.

**The matching threat confirmed:** the black-iron shard recovered from the Wardens matches the Urskelde cairn shard exactly — one enemy, reaching through multiple doors (skyward at the Standing Ring, downward here) rather than several separate threats.

**Reaching the thread alone, a first.** Grieving and wanting answers, Rurik attempted something untried — reaching Kethrun directly through the bear-spirit's bond, unassisted by Ysolde's working. It held. Kethrun confirmed what this means: repeated service as a conduit has made Rurik something like a living tending-hand for the network himself, not just a visitor who helps others reconnect. Rurik raised, then — after hearing Kethrun's and Ilsevet's honest, grave counsel about the danger of reaching alone toward an active, corrupting wound — chose *not* to attempt opening the path down himself, holding it as a known option rather than a unilateral action, and told his companions everything rather than keeping it private.

**The site stopgap-secured:** with no guardian left at all to anchor a seal, the party worked a group ward together (Ysolde leading, Rurik reinforcing via the thread, Mara and Sael physically anchoring) — not permanent, but real, keyed against the same corruption that took the Wardens. Expected to hold days to weeks. The open question of a permanent replacement guardian — freely chosen, not another sacrifice like Borvik or Ilsevet — was raised and left for Kethrun, Threnn, and Vosk to weigh properly.

**Home.** A clean, uneventful return journey, family and household waiting at Urskelde's gate. Rurik explained the sentinel-network development to Threnn and Kaldur; Kaldur delivered a full status report (garrison solid, Halvard's network running with no Greyfeather lead yet, Joss's alchemist search underway, Perrin/Greta's sky-iron work ongoing). **Rurik called a full regional council for one week out** — the guardian-replacement question, the confirmed single enemy, and everything else too large for four people to decide alone.

**Ends the session:** all four of Kethrun's known sealed sites now accounted for — three secured and thread-connected, the fourth stopgap-warded after its guardian's tragic loss; the enemy behind every site confirmed as one and the same; Rurik's own bond to the sentinel network deepened into something new and lasting; and a full council called to make the coming decisions together rather than alone. Open threads carried forward: finding a willing, freely-chosen replacement guardian for the Still Throat; the bird-like scout(s) and their maker; "Greyfeather"; how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen runs; and what's above Malphestor, now confirmed to be reaching through more than one door at once.

## Session 28 — 2026-07-29 to 2026-08-05

**Thyra's trial, arranged deliberately.** The morning after returning, Rurik asked Idra to clear the garrison's drills and quietly gather an audience — Kaldur, the household, and a good portion of Urskelde — without telling Thyra, so she'd feel real pressure rather than a staged lesson. Before the match, he told her plainly she wouldn't be facing her brother but "Darkhawk," and gave her the choice to walk away; she chose to cross the line anyway. A real, full-pace sparring match followed: Thyra lost the first several exchanges badly, then landed one genuine, earned touch reading a feint Rurik himself had taught her. He closed the demonstration by raging fully in front of her and the crowd — controlled completely, disarming her and stopping a blade's width from her throat — to let her feel, not just hear, the gap between skilled and truly dangerous. He knelt before her afterward and gave her the moment; she pulled him back to his feet instead, unwilling to let him kneel for what she'd earned standing.

**The lesson, named.** Afterward, at home, Thyra worked through what she'd actually learned: that real strength isn't the absence of danger but danger held on the thinnest possible leash. Rurik named it plainly as balance — each emotion needing a counterweight — and, asked to name his own, answered "love," crediting Mara directly as the one who taught him the rage's opposite. Mara named him as hers in turn. The household closed the moment out with each person naming their own balancing emotion (Mara: patience; Sesha: hope; Harek: satisfaction; Thyra: curiosity), before Rurik broke the mood on purpose with a callback to the stew disaster, refusing to let the night stay too heavy.

**A full regional council, held — governed differently this time.** Rather than open with his own account, Rurik invited every section head of Urskelde to report first, good and bad both, no filtering: Wren flagged thin arms supply for the growing garrison and a labor gap on the new eastern fields (Petra, farming); Corren flagged unfamiliar tracks near the northern treeline; Joss and Idra reported routine progress. Wren and Petra's problem was solved on the spot — a ten-person rotating field detail, five active daily, drawn from garrison volunteers first.

**Perrin and Greta's pitch, brought into the open.** At Rurik's earlier invitation to voice their "too ambitious to ask" ideas, the two smiths proposed a formal combined workshop with Threnn's people — sky-iron craft extended beyond gear into structural ward-work, potentially recovering techniques closer to Kethrun's original makers'. The council raised no real objection; Kessick's Ford's Ardyn asked for clarity on cost (none expected of them yet) and Halvard asked for discretion (agreed). Reporting lines were set: defense findings to Kaldur, structural work to Threnn, supply matters to Joss, anything larger to Rurik directly.

**Kaldur's readiness report, and a standing drill policy.** Garrison strong, cairns restored, alliance support confirmed ready. Asked by young recruit **Corim Ashvale** whether the enemy could corrupt guardians here the way it corrupted the Still Throat's, Kaldur answered honestly rather than reassuring emptily — isolation seems to be the vulnerability, and none of Urskelde's own sites remain isolated. Rurik, praising the question, gave Corim a real assignment: draft a proposal for training the garrison to spot subtle "wrongness" in people, not just places, due back at the next council. Separately, Rurik proposed testing the alliance's actual response readiness rather than assuming it — random, unpredictable drills, both internal and across Kessick's Ford, with results reported back each council. Adopted as standing policy.

**Threnn's report, and a needed correction.** Stonework finished, the Drowned Hollow holding strong, and real progress from his elders (particularly Bettha) on the old guardian tradition. When Threnn described his people's needs as something Urskelde might "provide," Rurik corrected him directly and publicly — family bears burdens together, not as charity — and Threnn accepted the correction openly in front of the room, declaring his people not guests of Urskelde but Urskelde.

**Kethrun consulted live, in council.** Rurik reached the sentinel thread a third time — markedly easier than either prior attempt — to ask Kethrun directly whether a replacement guardian had to be goliath-kin. Kethrun confirmed, and the whole room heard it through Rurik: the old alliance bound whoever a spirit chose to answer, freely, regardless of people. This reopens the Still Throat's guardian search far wider than Threnn's own lore alone suggested.

**Rurik's own near-mistake, told to the council by Mara.** Rurik disclosed that he'd privately considered staying alone at the Still Throat as a temporary guardian before his companions talked him out of it. Rather than explain it himself, he asked Mara to tell the room — she did, plainly, framing it as proof he could be told no and actually listen. The council settled on a patient, unhurried search for a freely-chosen guardian (any people, not just goliath-kin) rather than a rushed volunteer, backed by a **rotating paired watch** drawn from both Threnn's people and Thundertree in the meantime. **Thyra was given command of the rotation** — not required to attend every shift, expected to set the standard others follow — with Threnn's elders (Bettha specifically) added to her ongoing education at Rurik's request, making her development a three-way investment between Thundertree, Threnn's people, and Urskelde.

**Halvard's full report.** Pursuing Rurik's theory that Greyfeather might be an organization rather than one assassin, Halvard found real supporting evidence — three unexplained deaths in Ravenshollow over two years, all disguised as fate, no consistent physical description across witnesses. Ysolde and Threnn's examination of the Still Throat's recovered shard confirmed a match to both the Urskelde cairn shard and a centuries-old Ravenshollow cult's spirit-binding craft — one maker or tradition behind the shards, the corrupted guardians, and the scout creature alike. This also finally explains the impossibly precise targeting order from Ashpit: an organization with reach into the same network that built the scout could have supplied Rurik's cage number in advance. Corren confirmed the northern tracks match the known scout type exactly, renewed and active. Halvard requested — and received — authority to formally merge the Greyfeather and scout-maker investigations into one effort, with Kessick's Ford's intelligence folded in at Ardyn's offer.

**Closing words:** Rurik closed the council with the family theme he'd been building toward all session — that a family doesn't always agree, always has someone who won't stop talking (a pointed, affectionate jab at Thyra), and gets through hard things by staying in them together rather than avoiding them. Landed warmly; several in the room visibly moved.

**The hunt, planned.** Afterward, at Sael's grove, Rurik, Mara, Sael, and Ysolde worked out the next move: Ysolde will take several days to study the recovered shard and her order's notes on constructed-spirit binding before anyone goes near another one in the field; Sael will scout the northern treeline at dusk (the scout's confirmed active window) in owl-shape, unseen; Mara and Rurik will run randomized, unpredictable patrols with the garrison as both real reconnaissance and public reassurance. Rurik's own suggestion — deliberately getting captured to be tracked — was unanimously and immediately vetoed.

**Ends the session:** Thyra tested and proven in front of the whole settlement, given real command of the Still Throat's rotating watch; a full council that reshaped how Urskelde governs itself — radical transparency, a standing drill policy, a combined sky-iron workshop, and shared accountability named and accepted rather than assumed; the guardian search widened beyond goliath-kin by Kethrun's own words; and Greyfeather and the scout's maker formally confirmed as one investigation, with a patient, prepared hunt now underway rather than another rushed pursuit. Open threads carried forward: finding the scout's source and maker; a freely-chosen guardian for the Still Throat; "Greyfeather" the organization; how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen runs; and what's above Malphestor.

## Session 29 — 2026-08-06

**The morning patrol, and a real find.** Rurik let Mara run the loop her own way — deliberately irregular, unpredictable — and near the northern tree line she found a fresh, wrong print: birdlike but too deep, too evenly spaced, pacing a fixed line rather than ranging freely. Read together as watching, not hunting — angled toward the eastern fields, the cairns, and the drill yard. Rather than push further and risk spooking it before Sael's dusk watch, they marked the spot precisely and withdrew naturally, finishing the patrol loop as if nothing had changed.

**Lunch with Ysolde, and three theories deepened.** Reporting the print in person doubled as a check-in — Ysolde, absorbed in the shard research and having forgotten to eat again, was fed and tidied up under Mara's fond insistence. Her early findings: the shard's craft binds *intent* into something non-living, giving it purpose without a soul — different in kind from the Raven Queen's own debt-and-judgment tradition, likely a different hand entirely despite sharing a region. Rurik pushed the implications further across the meal: that this craft would offend far more than one faith at once (nature, creation, soul-sanctity traditions alike, not just death-and-order ones), and that whatever's behind it might not fear divine attention at all — might be powerful enough to measure against gods themselves, the way Kethrun described the original war. Ysolde, unflinching, agreed it was plausible rather than talking it down. Rurik named his own growing suspicion plainly — that he was marked before he was born — then reframed it himself: not *chosen*, which implies no choice in the matter, but **trusted**, which doesn't. Both women affirmed the distinction as the truer one, given everything he's actually chosen since Ashpit. Closed on a lighter note: Rurik's genuine laugh that "Mara has more control over me than the Gods," which nobody in the room disputed.

**Waiting for dusk, and the bond tested properly.** Staked out near the marked tree line with Mara, Rurik deliberately tested what the sentinel-network bond could do beyond simple communication. A first attempt (unassisted) returned only the network's broad, familiar shape — three sites calm, the Still Throat still tender. A second attempt, with the bear-spirit's presence actively lending itself to the reach rather than just watching, went further: a faint, directional sense of "wrongness" matching the shard's taint, northwest — roughly the print's own location. Relayed to Sael via a runner and Ysolde's *Sending* before she went out, giving her a real head start instead of a blind search.

**The signal, caught by instinct rather than strain.** Mara taught Rurik her own stillness technique while they waited — attention held inward on breath and ground rather than straining outward at the tree line, letting anything wrong stand out against a background that's stopped reaching for it. On the first real attempt, it worked outright: Rurik caught Sael's alarm call (three short, one long) before Mara did. Both sprinted for the tree line at full speed, Longstrider already active on both of them.

**Two constructs, one captured alive.** They found Sael cornered by two bird-like creatures — the larger matching Corbin's reports, a second smaller one hanging back, clearly coordinating rather than just present. Rurik's first attempt to grab the larger one clean failed against its unnatural speed; Sael's *Entangle* rooted it in place instead, and Rurik bound it properly with rope while restrained — Urskelde's first live capture of one of these things, rather than another chase through cold trails.

**A message sent through the puppet, and answered.** With Mara and Sael escorting the bound creature back, Rurik stayed alone with the smaller one and noticed a scrying signature active behind its eyes — something listening in real time. He spoke directly through it: *"It's not the settlement that interests you, it's me. I know this for certain."* The connection flinched violently and cut out entirely, the creature left disoriented and undirected. Read as instinct-driven rather than intelligent (confirmed further by a careful attempt at communication — no language, but real, searching attention), Rurik offered it a gesture of release rather than a killing blow; it wandered off into the trees on its own, no longer under anyone's direction.

**Lockdown and debrief.** Back at the gate, Rurik ordered the captured creature hooded immediately, held under triple guard, no conversation near it — protecting Urskelde from exactly the kind of scrying exposure the smaller one had just demonstrated. Briefed Mara and Sael properly: the creatures are puppets, basic survival instinct only, no real intelligence behind the eyes — and that he'd spoken directly to whatever *does* have intelligence on the other end, naming the "count" theory to it outright. Both women registered the risk of tipping their hand early, but agreed the confirmed reaction (a genuine flinch at being named) was real evidence the theory has weight, not just internal speculation.

**Ysolde briefed, and a safeguard set.** The three went straight to Ysolde with the full account and the live specimen. She confirmed the value immediately — direct comparison against the shard, and the first real proof anything behind this is actually *reacting* to being understood. Rurik asked that Sael accompany her for every study session, no exceptions, given the risk of another scrying attempt or worse; both agreed without argument. Closed with familiar warmth — Rurik extracting a promise from Ysolde to actually rest tonight rather than return straight to her notes.

**Ends the session:** a live construct captured and secured under full lockdown; direct, confirmed contact made with whatever's behind the scout creatures and the shards, and a genuine reaction drawn from it; the sentinel-network bond proven capable of more than simple communication when the bear-spirit actively lends itself to the reach; and "the count" theory strengthened by real evidence for the first time rather than resting on inference alone. Open threads carried forward: what Ysolde's study of the captured creature reveals; whether naming the theory to the enemy directly was a risk or a genuine advantage; the smaller creature's fate now that it's unguided; "Greyfeather" and the merged investigation; how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen runs; and what's above Malphestor.

## Session 30 — 2026-08-07

**The core, extracted.** The morning after the capture, Rurik joined Ysolde and Sael to examine the construct properly. Ysolde identified a small embedded core as the construct's true anchor — remove it cleanly and the whole thing should simply stop, no malice required. With Ysolde guiding his hands directly through the delicate work, Rurik freed the core intact rather than shattering it in his first, unguided attempt.

**An untried working, four bonds at once.** Rather than have only Rurik reach for the core's resonance, he proposed something none of them had tried — Mara, Sael, and Ysolde each placing a hand on him and opening their own primal bonds alongside his, slowly and deliberately ("a tap, not a floodgate") rather than forcing it. The combined working succeeded past anyone's expectation: the four bonds converged like separate streams finding a shared riverbed, the bear-spirit anchoring the center. Through it, the core resolved into a real impression — a distant, cold, hollow place, carved rather than built, old stone repurposed by patient, organized hands — and underneath it all, the same vast, watching scale from Rurik's earlier dream. Something on the other end felt the working land and responded, but — as Rurik was careful to point out afterward — it recognized the *nature* of four converging primal bonds, not any of their identities. The anonymity from Session 29's confrontation held.

**The decision made: summon the inner circle, for real this time.** With actual evidence in hand rather than pure theory, Rurik called it — time to bring "the count" to Vosk, Threnn, Kaldur, and Kethrun directly, at the Standing Ring where the bond runs strongest, as planned days ago. The summons was kept deliberately vague (enough to signal real weight without alarm), aimed for three days out, with Ysolde flagging that Vosk's actual location (Urskelde or already back at Thundertree) would determine whether that timeline holds. Halvard was looped in too, given the core's "mountain country, high, cold, hollow" impression falls squarely inside his merged investigation — he'll run a parallel, quiet search of nearby mountain sites while the party's at the Ring.

**Kaldur asked to come himself.** Rather than leave him holding Urskelde again, Rurik asked Kaldur to join the journey directly — "there's more than I can say here that you all need to know." Kaldur didn't hesitate long, proposing **Thyra** take real acting command in his and Threnn's absence, backed by Idra (garrison), Bettha (Threnn's people), and the section heads already used to reporting upward. Rurik deferred the final call to Kaldur outright, bowing and addressing him as "Commander" — a gesture that visibly moved him. Kaldur confirmed the plan: he'll brief Thyra plainly, the same honesty he gave her at her public trial, and set the reporting lines properly before the party departs.

**Ends the session:** a live construct successfully broken down for its core, a first-ever four-bond combined working yielding a real, actionable location impression while preserving the party's anonymity, and the inner circle summons finally set in motion — Vosk, Threnn, Kaldur, and Kethrun to be told everything at the Standing Ring, roughly three days out. Kaldur is coming in person, leaving Thyra in real acting command of Urskelde for the first time. Open threads carried forward: pinning down the "mountain country" location more precisely (Halvard's parallel search); Vosk's exact location and its effect on timing; what the full inner circle will decide once they hear "the count" theory in full; "Greyfeather"; how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen runs; and what's above Malphestor.

## Session 31 — 2026-08-10

**Arrival, and the wait for Vosk.** Three uneventful days brought the party to the Standing Ring; Threnn arrived the next morning with a small party of his own people, leaving only Vosk unaccounted for — riding in from Thundertree with no fixed arrival time given. Rurik spent part of the wait helping Torvald with routine stonework rather than pacing, telling him plainly that quiet, unglamorous vigilance matters as much as anything the party does out in the world — a moment Torvald clearly hadn't expected to hear said so directly, and one he wouldn't let go unfinished even once Vosk's riders were spotted on approach.

**The full inner circle assembled.** Vosk arrived and was brought straight down to Kethrun's chamber to join Threnn, Kaldur, Torvald, and the household — the first time this many people have stood together in that space at once. Before laying out the theory itself, Rurik asked Vosk to think back on the raid that took him as a child with fresh eyes. She realized, saying it aloud for the first time, that the raiders took nothing but him — no goods, no other captives, not even the horses — and struck on the one night the whole camp was gathered close for a rite, the worst possible night for Thundertree and the exact right night for whoever planned it. Not luck. Design.

**"The count," told in full.** Rurik laid out everything before the assembled circle: the bear-spirit's dream-borne warning, the theory that Ashpit was deliberate cultivation rather than cruelty, Malphestor's broken ledger as proof of genuine unpredictability rather than luck, and the reframing from "chosen" to **trusted**. Kethrun's reaction confirmed it beyond anything the household could have proven alone — "the count" is a real, old term from his makers' own era, a reckoning the ancient enemy keeps only on the rare few who might become something it can't afford to ignore. He named the mechanism plainly: a child killed outright is a crime against balance, but a child turned into his own corruption, made to spend it by his own hand, is a debt collected cleanly — and Rurik's refusal in that cage was the failure that kept the count from ever closing.

**The evidence added in turn.** Ysolde presented her own theory — that the Raven Queen's faith, opposed by nature to what's above Malphestor, may have deliberately timed the release of Rurik's blocked memory as a quiet, undeclared intervention rather than a coincidence. Sael and Ysolde together walked the circle through the captured construct, the extracted core, and the four-bond working's location impression: a distant, cold, hollow place of old, repurposed stone in mountain country — and confirmation that whatever responded to the working sensed the *nature* of four converging bonds, not any identity, preserving the party's anonymity. Rurik closed with the boldest piece: speaking directly through the smaller creature's active scrying link two nights prior, naming himself as the true target to whatever was listening, and watching the connection flinch and cut out — the first real evidence the theory had ever produced, not just inference.

**The offer, made and answered.** Rurik named the final step himself: asking the bear-spirit to stop merely holding "the count" apart from him and instead bond with him as a true, equal partner — sharing the reckoning rather than being shielded from it. Given the full circle's blessing and Kethrun's grave guidance (his own makers achieved exactly this partnership, though not all of them survived the asking), Rurik disarmed and unarmored himself completely at the chamber's center, standing rather than kneeling, and called the bear-spirit directly. The working staggered him hard — a wave of the count's full weight nearly buckling his knees — until he met it not by resisting or lashing out, but by entering a deliberately **balanced rage**, the same technique he'd used reweaving Vessyn, holding fury and restraint in the same hand at once. The bear-spirit recognized that balance and folded into it rather than testing further. The bond completed: no longer protection held apart from him, but a true partnership, marked by new lines etched into his forearm alongside the old claw scar. For one unbroken moment, the bear-spirit spoke directly through him — naming Kethrun "stone-kin," confirming Rurik's choice (not luck) as the reason the count never closed, and thanking Mara, Sael, and Ysolde for holding the door open long enough for him to walk through it himself.

**Kept to the inner circle alone.** Spent afterward, Rurik asked that tonight's working — the full bond, not just the theory — stay strictly within this circle, not even reaching Sesha and Harek, given the risk of it reaching the enemy's own ears. Vosk, Threnn, Kaldur, and Kethrun all agreed without argument.

**Ends the session:** "the count" theory confirmed as real, ancient truth rather than speculation, direct from Kethrun's own memory of his makers; the full inner circle briefed and bound to secrecy together for the first time; and Rurik fully, mutually bonded to the bear-spirit — sharing the ancient reckoning as a true partner rather than being shielded from it alone. Open threads carried forward: what the deepened bond changes going forward; the mountain-country lead and Halvard's parallel search; why Rurik was marked so precisely before the raid that took him; how deep House Corvane's service to the Raven Queen runs; and what's above Malphestor, now facing something it can no longer fully reckon.

## Session 32 — 2026-08-10 to 2026-08-18

**The morning after, answered honestly.** Rather than let the bond's weight sit unspoken, Rurik opened the floor to the whole circle and answered each question in turn. To Kethrun: he means to keep the balance deliberately, mercy and ruthlessness both chosen rather than defaulted to — which Kethrun named as exactly the shape his makers never accounted for, and likely why the bear-spirit answered him as an equal. To Vosk, who asked whether she could have prevented the raid: a river meets a boulder and simply finds another way around — design doesn't lose to vigilance, and nothing she could have watched for would have stopped hands patient enough to plan thirteen years ahead. She let thirteen years of guilt go in that answer. To Kaldur: confirmation that Rurik now sits genuinely outside the enemy's scrying reach, which is why physical scouts had to watch him instead of simply finding him — Kaldur kept the doubled watch regardless, unwilling to relax on an assumption. To Mara: a deflection with jokes wrapped around it, which she accepted for now and reserved the right to ask again. Threnn voiced the question he'd been holding all along — whether his resettled people were now more exposed for having come home — and Rurik told him plainly they'd become more important, not less: kin sharing the same fight, not bystanders near it. Kethrun added the wider frame unprompted: four old spirits answering within one lifetime is a declaration the old world will notice, for good and ill both. Sael and Ysolde both got real answers too — Sael reassured that her rescue, her years of watching, and the capture at Session 29 stand as their own bond, not a lesser one beside the bear-spirit's; Ysolde pushed to trust her own "goddess working subtly" theory rather than keep hedging it as unprovable. Torvald closed the round out, unprompted, thanking Rurik for saying vigilance mattered and extracting a promise not to vanish on him.

**A night for family, not strategy.** The circle broke into an unplanned feast — Threnn's people's own brew, Kaldur telling stories on himself, Vosk singing an old Thundertree hunting song half the room already knew, Ysolde losing all scholarly restraint by the second cup, Sael laughing outright for the first time anyone's seen. It ran long and loud, by choice, until it finally burned down to embers and just Rurik and Mara by the last of the fire.

**Command returned, cleanly.** Back at Urskelde after an uneventful three-day return with Vosk, Threnn, and Kaldur in tow, Thyra delivered a full report — watch doubled and held without incident, Corim's training proposal finished and ready, Perrin/Greta's workshop ahead of schedule, the new drill policy tested twice, Vale and Merrick unchanged, family well. Kaldur formally relieved her of acting command in front of the party, praising her plainly and promising to say as much at the next council. Rurik asked for one more thing first: a hug from her brother, not her commander.

**Halvard's lead: Greywatch Keep.** Halvard narrowed the core reading's "mountain country, cold, hollow, repurposed old stone" impression to a single strong candidate — an abandoned goliath-kin border fortress two weeks north and west, officially empty for generations but quietly avoided by caravans, trappers, and at least one metalworker buying up scrap for an unnamed northern client. Rurik chose caution over haste: no scouts sent in yet, information-gathering only, and a full council called for two weeks out to weigh real evidence before committing to anything.

**A goddess's attention, deliberately sought.** Rather than wait on Ysolde's research alone, Rurik asked Lord Corvane to take the party to House Corvane's private chantry in Ravenshollow — the quiet, unglamorous seat of his family's generations-long service to the Raven Queen — and asked to go in alone first. He named the theft plainly to her: that what sits above Malphestor doesn't kill, it steals a mortal's own free choice and forges it into payment on a ledger that was never rightfully its own. The Raven Queen confirmed it in full — calling it counterfeit of her own honest practice, something she cannot act against uninvited, but has now, for the first time, genuinely been asked to notice. She marked him with a lasting gift: the ability to recognize, without doubt, when a choice being asked of him or anyone standing with him is being weighed toward that false ledger rather than freely made. She then received the rest of the party briefly — honoring Corvane's house for keeping its ledgers honestly even at cost, recognizing Ysolde as a fellow keeper of a related practice under Thossia, and thanking Mara and Sael for holding the door open long enough for Rurik to walk through it himself.

**Corvane, freed and freely chosen.** Afterward, Rurik told Corvane his debt was fulfilled and asked, without any obligation attached, whether he'd stand with the party by free will. Corvane said yes — not as inherited duty or ongoing penance, but as a real, chosen alliance, the first time in his life he's ever framed his House's purpose as his own rather than his family's. Rurik answered with a goliath forearm-grip rather than a handshake: "friends shake hands, family do this." Corvane, visibly moved, accepted it as kinship.

**The ledger.** That night, Ysolde, Sael, and Corvane worked through the chantry's old archive and found a warning entry generations old, describing a near-identical "counting done in stone" — a child marked young, raised toward corruption, who resisted the same way Rurik did, and the entity behind it withdrawing to regroup rather than accept failure. The location named for its retreat: "the watch upon grey stone, north and cold, where the old border-keepers once stood and stand no longer" — Halvard's Greywatch Keep, confirmed by independent record rather than guesswork alone. Corvane released the volume to the party outright. Ysolde, Sael, and Corvane agreed to keep working the ledger on the road home, aiming to have a real evidentiary briefing ready before the council rather than raw scholarship.

**Ends the session:** the inner circle's questions answered honestly and completely; Thyra's acting command closed out with real honor; Halvard's Greywatch Keep lead identified and treated with due caution; a genuine audience won with the Raven Queen, who confirmed "the count" as theft from her own domain and marked Rurik with a permanent sense for corrupted-choice manipulation; and Lord Corvane fully, freely aligned with the party as kin rather than a debt half-paid. Open threads carried forward: Greywatch Keep itself, still unconfirmed and unscouted; the full council, two weeks out, waiting on Halvard's intelligence and the Corvane ledger's full briefing; what's above Malphestor, now confirmed to have failed and regrouped from a near-identical attempt once before; and how the Raven Queen's quiet attention might shape what comes next.

## Session 33 — 2026-08-18 to 2026-09-01

**Home ground, business first.** Back at Urskelde, Corim Ashvale walked Rurik through his finished corruption-detection proposal in private — monthly cross-chain check-ins, behavioral markers worth a second look rather than an accusation, and a standing (if still-rough) arrangement with Ysolde and Sael for the rare case someone needs real checking. Rurik approved it for formal council presentation on the spot.

**The cairns, made visible.** Perrin and Greta showed off a working model rather than describing it: sky-iron worked directly into a cairn's stonework blinds it to scrying the same way it does a blade, and Threnn confirmed reworking all six would make Urskelde as hard to find as Rurik himself. Rurik committed the two weeks before the council to the project — northern cairn first, full-size, with an honest progress report due back in one week.

**Told plainly.** On Mara's counsel — tell them, but gently, and on his own terms — Rurik sat down with Sesha and Harek and gave them the whole truth: the raid was deliberate, "the count" is real, the bear-spirit's bond is now full and mutual, and a goddess has personally taken notice. Harek's grief sharpened into resolve; Sesha wept and held her ground plainly — proud regardless of the shape the truth took. Asked only to keep being his parents, nothing more, which they gladly agreed was the easiest thing anyone had asked of them in years.

**The Family Restitution Fund, closed clean.** Sesha and Harek came along to Kessick's Ford to see it firsthand — all eleven verified claimants paid out evenly (roughly 71 gold each) alongside the last of the strongroom's gemstones and jewelry, Corwin Pell closing the ledger for good. Standing in the square afterward, Rurik looked to the sky and whispered "debt settled, not stolen" — answered not with a second audience, but a single raven circling once over the square, and a quiet, wordless confirmation through **The Honest Ledger** that the debt had been honestly kept.

**Two weeks pass.** The northern cairn's rework finishes on schedule, proven against every scrying attempt thrown at it — five more to go, four to five weeks at pace, sky-iron supply now the binding constraint. Corim's proposal comes back tightened, no longer resting on one person's goodwill. Halvard's intelligence deepens further: more caravans, more local folk-memory of "the old stones" warned against. Ysolde finishes a full formal briefing from House Corvane's archive.

**The full council convenes.** Kaldur opens with Urskelde's state: doubled watch holding clean, the new drill policy proven across two runs, garrison at full strength. **Corim's proposal is formally adopted**, effective the next day, abnormalities routed to both Kaldur (defense) and Halvard (intelligence) by design. **Perrin, Greta, and Threnn** report the cairn project's real bottleneck: sky-iron material, not skill or will.

**Three guardians, reached at once.** For the first time, Rurik pushed the deepened bond wide enough to hold **Kethrun, Ilsevet, and Vessyn** in conversation simultaneously across half the region — a visible strain, but one the bond held. Asked about sky-iron sources, Kethrun named a known fall-site in exactly the "mountain country" already under suspicion — the same ground as **Greywatch Keep**.
