Ilsevet confirmed it from old goliath-kin maps, marked "claimed" rather than cursed; Vessyn confirmed it from the wild's own memory of the wound a fallen star leaves behind. The room watched Rurik hold all three at once, and understood for the first time in front of everyone exactly what "fully bonded" now means.

**Joss reports supply is sound**, restitution work no longer competing for his ledger's attention, and a promising alchemist candidate found through Kessick's Ford — close to ending the reliance on borrowed healing potions.

**Halvard delivers the full case.** Every independent thread — trade rerouting, a trapper's silence, a metalworker's unnamed northern client, generations-old folk warning, and now three guardians' own testimony — converges on Greywatch Keep. "Not a candidate anymore," he told the council. "The site."

**Ysolde lays the ledger over it.** The old warning entry: a nameless child, marked young, resisted the same way Rurik did, and the entity forced into withdrawal rather than defeat. But the newer evidence — caravans rerouting starting eight months ago, the scrap-metal buyer six weeks back — means Greywatch Keep isn't simply old history. Something reoccupied it recently. This may be a second attempt already in motion, not settled history.

**Open questions, answered plainly.** Kaldur (tactics): scout first, no direct assault. Threnn (legacy): wants to know what's actually there before deciding whether to want it restored. Sael (a possible living victim): if the pattern holds, a survivor might be kept as a *reminder* rather than killed — Rurik confirmed from his own scars that this tracks, and Sael asked to be on the scouting team regardless of what it costs her to look. Corvane (the Raven Queen's stake): her judgment doesn't change whether the theft is old or ongoing — the act is the same act. Vosk asked for a fallback if the scouting team fails: Rurik named Kaldur, Threnn, Halvard, and Thyra. Ardyn asked what Kessick's Ford should do: watch and tighten quietly, same as Urskelde.

**The plan set.** No scrying — sky-iron interference and an unknown goliath-kin structure make it unreliable and risky. Halvard's advance scouts move immediately, with two relay garrison points feeding intelligence back continuously; a full report due in **two weeks**. Rurik, Mara, Sael, Ysolde, and Corvane will go in after that report lands. Mara joins the advance scouts directly to learn the terrain herself — every approach, every hazard — rather than wait idle at Urskelde. Ysolde, Sael, and Corvane begin work on a communication method that doesn't rely on scrying at all: a pair of consecrated ledger-pages, drawn from House Corvane's oldest chantry tradition, meant to carry short, delayed messages out from behind the sky-iron interference.

**Milestone level-up, full party.** Earned for winning a genuine audience with the Raven Queen, confirming "the count" beyond doubt via independent record, forging Corvane's alliance by free will, reaching all three sealed-site guardians at once for the first time, and setting a real, coordinated plan in motion against Greywatch Keep. **Darkhawk:** Barbarian 12 → 13 (character level 17, proficiency bonus now +6). **Mara:** Ranger 16 → 17 (proficiency bonus +6, gained a 5th-level spell slot). **Sael:** Druid 14 → 15 (gained an 8th-level spell slot, Wild Shape CR ceiling now 5). **Ysolde:** Cleric 15 → 16. Full HP and mechanical detail on each character sheet.

**Ends the session:** Urskelde's home-front business fully settled — Corim's proposal adopted, the cairn project underway, the Restitution Fund closed, Sesha and Harek told the full truth, and a small, quiet sign from the Raven Queen herself. The full council converged every thread onto Greywatch Keep as confirmed, likely active ground, and set a real plan in motion: two weeks of advance scouting, a five-person entry team named, and a new way to communicate through the sky-iron interference in development. Open threads carried forward: what the advance scouts find; whether a living victim is being held at Greywatch Keep; the communication workaround's success; and what waits at the site itself.

## Session 34 — 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-25

**Two weeks of integration.** With Ysolde, Sael, and Corvane building the communication workaround and Mara out learning the terrain, Rurik spent the wait training with Kaldur to fuse rage and Battle Master technique into one instinct rather than two competing ones. Recruits rotated through as sparring partners; a final bout against Thyra — her first rematch since her own public trial — ended clean, Rurik chaining Reckless Attack into Disarming Attack without the old hesitation. Both Kaldur and Thyra confirmed the difference was real, not just felt. A quiet family evening with Sesha, Harek, and Thyra followed, no business discussed, just presence.

**Full reports, full plan.** Halvard's advance scouts returned with a real picture of Greywatch Keep: roughly a dozen robed operatives, three bird-like scout constructs, a heavily-guarded eastern tower flagged as the likely site of a held captive, a lightly-watched northern breach as the best entry point, and something new — a massive stone-and-iron construct patrolling the outer wall on a pattern that hadn't varied in years, answering to no visible master. Mara's own terrain work confirmed a viable approach: a frozen stream bed and old-growth stand offering cover most of the way to the breach. Ysolde, Sael, and Corvane finished the ledger-pair communication method — paired consecrated pages, built on House Corvane's own chantry tradition, meant to carry short messages even through sky-iron interference.

**The switchback ambush.** Five days into the two-week road north, Mara caught three Greyfeather scouts lying in wait on a narrow mountain trail. Rather than walk into it, the party turned it around: Mara and Sael flanked from above while Rurik, Ysolde, and Corvane approached as bait. All three scouts were killed or captured with the party untouched. The survivor, dangled over the cliff edge, broke completely and confirmed real intelligence: the party's approach had been noticed generally (not personally), the eastern tower holds something deliberately hidden even from most of the garrison, the keep's true strength is around fifteen, and the wall-patrolling construct is older than anything currently controlling the keep. **Rurik dropped him afterward** despite his full cooperation — a hard, deliberate choice that opened a real conversation with Ysolde about debts honestly paid versus a life's fuller ledger; she ultimately agreed the balance of Greyfeather's service outweighed one honest confession, and thanked Rurik for pushing her past her first, incomplete read of it.

**Skarn, a fifth guardian freed.** Before committing to the keep itself, the party used the scout's warning about the wall-patrolling construct to investigate rather than simply fight it. Rurik approached alone during its patrol's blind window, let the bear-spirit's presence lead, and — after the construct's ancient defensive instinct nearly won out — threw open the full sentinel network connection, letting Kethrun, Ilsevet, and Vessyn's presence reach it directly. The construct, an ancient border-sentinel named **Skarn**, broke through centuries of hollowed-out habit and chose, freely, to take up **the Still Throat's still-open guardianship** — the first guardian ever offered a genuine choice of *where* rather than simply *whether*. Skarn departed for the Standing Ring to meet Kethrun properly first. Word was sent home via the ledger-pair: a fifth guardian found, Threnn asked to receive him.

**Greywatch Keep, taken quietly — then not.** The party cleared the barracks in total silence, six operatives killed in their sleep or at watch without a sound raised. All three courtyard scout constructs were destroyed the same way, one at a time, isolated from each other's patrol loops. The eastern tower's two guards proved tougher — the takedown attempt broke stealth when one, badly wounded, managed to scream before Mara could silence him. Sael held the door with a maximum-strength Conjure Animals (four dire wolves) and Spike Growth while the rest pushed inside.

**Dorin, found.** At the top of the tower: a young Thundertree goliath, chained, starved, and deliberately kept alive as a living warning rather than killed — taken roughly eight or nine months ago, resisted whatever "the count" wanted of him the same way Rurik once did, and has been kept as an object lesson to the garrison ever since. Freed and named **Dorin**. The parallel to Rurik's own history was immediate and mutual, and Dorin recognized Thundertree's name from an offhand guard's comment.

**A fighting retreat, then a hard choice.** Coming back down, five garrison fighters who'd answered the earlier alarm were caught between Sael's wolves, Spike Growth, and Rurik's reckless charge — all five killed in a single decisive exchange, the party untouched. With the tower and courtyard cleared and no further pursuit yet, the party split: **Sael and Corvane escorted Dorin to Halvard's nearest relay garrison point**, while **Rurik, Mara, and Ysolde pushed toward the unmapped central keep** — the likely heart of whatever's actually running "the count" here.

**The Keeper.** Inside the central keep — cold, carved with strange channels, walls lined with active ledgers — Rurik, Mara, and Ysolde found a robed figure waiting beside a single dark iron disc, unsurprised by their arrival. He named himself **Osric Thane**, a "Keeper" of the ledger tied to Greywatch Keep — not Greyfeather itself, but "closer to what moves it." When Rurik's anger flared toward him, **The Honest Ledger flagged the moment itself as exactly the corrupted-choice trap "the count" was built to collect** — Osric named it plainly rather than exploiting it, an early sign of where this conversation was actually headed.

**A confession, and an offer.** Osric had read a year of reports about Rurik's every impossible success — Ashpit's failure, the bear-spirit's answer, Duskgate, four ancient guardians reconnected, and Skarn's freedom that very day — and it had finally broken decades of quiet complicity into real doubt. He offered to help sever the ledger's account at its root rather than simply die and let a successor inherit the same office, warning plainly that doing so would likely draw a real response from whatever sits above it all. Rurik chose to proceed anyway, citing a mentor's old wisdom about knowing the size of the fight before throwing the first punch.

**The severing.** With Mara barring the door and Ysolde guiding the working (closer to consecration than combat, per Osric's instruction), Rurik opened the sentinel bond a third strained time that day — Kethrun, Ilsevet, Vessyn, and even a newly-freed, barely-settled Skarn all answering despite the fatigue, their freely-chosen presence directly opposing the ledger's stolen foundation. The disc went dark. The account closed — the first time, by Osric's own account, he'd ever seen it done. Rurik paid for it in real strain (rolled damage, HP reduced).

**The name.** In exchange, Osric gave the name he'd heard exactly once, decades ago, and never dared repeat: **Kathrivex, the First Ledger** — older than devils, older than Kethrun's makers, the origin of "the count" as a practice rather than merely another servant of it. Every Keeper, every ledger site (Osric confirmed at least three others across distant regions), all separate "fingers" of the same vast, patient whole. Rurik took Osric into custody rather than leaving him exposed to Kathrivex's likely retaliation.

**The keep's last secrets.** A thorough search turned up 340 gold, a partial map marking the other ledger sites, decades of Osric's own records, and a sealed cache of raw sky-iron ore — likely solving the cairns' material shortage outright. A further search (properly aided this time, advantage applied) turned up two items from an earlier Keeper who'd apparently tried the same quiet resistance decades before Osric and was caught and replaced: **the Ledger's Eye** (a ring, once per long rest senses whether a nearby choice is being coerced rather than freely made) and **the Quiet Warden's Cloak** (advantage on saves vs. charm/fear/compulsion from infernal or corrupted sources, advantage on Stealth in dim light/darkness). Identified on the road home; the ring went to Ysolde, the cloak to Mara.

**Noticed, not found.** As the severing completed, Rurik felt something ancient turn toward the working — not a targeted hunt, by his own read, but the unmistakable sense of an attention shifting toward a sound it had never had cause to hear before. The council took this seriously without panicking: watch elevated, cairn work continuing at sustainable pace, Halvard and Ysolde to begin working through the recovered records immediately.

**Osric's fate, decided by council rather than by Rurik alone.** Given the floor deliberately rather than deciding unilaterally, the council reached near-consensus: guarded trust, same terms as Vale, real work rather than a cell. Dorin himself, asked directly, confirmed he wouldn't stand against it, though his own forgiveness isn't there yet. **Halvard took formal charge of Osric**, given the obvious value of his knowledge to the merged investigation.

**Ends the session:** Greywatch Keep's ledger closed for good — outer defenses cleared, a fifth guardian freed, a living victim rescued, and the enemy's true name spoken aloud for the first time in a room where its practice actually died. Rurik carries real cost for all of it: three strained bond-openings in one day, a confirmed sense of having been noticed by something ancient and patient. The day closed quietly — a council debrief, Osric placed under guarded trust, and a simple, unremarkable dinner cooked at home with Mara, the ordinary and the enormous sitting side by side the way they always seem to in this family. Open threads carried forward: what Kathrivex's notice actually means going forward; what Halvard and Ysolde find in the recovered records and the map of other ledger sites; Dorin's longer-term recovery and place among the party's allies; Osric's slow path toward earned trust; and the still-unidentified older Keeper who hid the ring and cloak generations ago.

## Session 35 — 2026-09-26 to 2026-09-28

**Skarn, settled.** At the Standing Ring, Kethrun reported Skarn adjusting well — old patrol instincts catching themselves rather than winning outright, two supervised walks of the Still Throat's approach already completed, and the network itself measurably stronger for holding four true guardians instead of three. Rurik spent quiet time with Skarn directly, answering its wordless question about what comes next for a freed guardian: duty doesn't have to swallow everything else, if you make room for the people who matter alongside it. Skarn accepted the lesson visibly, and the standing offer to reach Rurik through the network the same way the other guardians can.

**Thundertree, and old grief given somewhere to stand.** Rurik sought out Yorik Stonebrand and the Alderhawks (Vessa and Kessa) to tell them plainly he hadn't forgotten Bram, Torin, and Ren's deaths at the mine's entrance fight. Both families gave their blessing for **three new memorial cairns at Urskelde**, standing stones distinct from the six watch-cairns — sky-iron worked in for its shine rather than its warding, each one carrying something personal (rope and trap-work, a fishing hook, a raven for Kessa's father). Yorik, Vessa, and Kessa traveled back to Urskelde to help build them with their own hands; Threnn and Harek both joined the project directly. Sesha asked for, and received, a fourth cairn of her own — honoring the two elders and the hunter lost the night Rurik was taken as a child, a grief she'd never had anywhere real to place in thirteen years.

**A hard conversation with Sesha.** Rurik admitted real fear for the first time to his mother — not of his own death, which he's made peace with, but of what Kathrivex could cost the people he loves, and of having weaknesses at all after a childhood that gave him none to lose. Sesha reframed it plainly: love isn't a weakness Kathrivex can cleanly exploit, it's the exact thing the practice has never managed to break in him, and the answer isn't armoring against fear but choosing his people again and again despite it — the same way he's always chosen mercy.

**The Tallow House.** Halvard and Osric, working through the recovered records, narrowed the partial map's third marked site to a coastal location tied to an old, previously-dismissed trade report of unusual discreet patronage. Osric recalled the name overheard once, years ago: **"the Tallow House."** Rurik's own reasoning sharpened the discovery considerably — a coastal site could mean Kathrivex maintains **its own private infrastructure**, independent of local trade, explaining why every prior site (Ashpit, Duskgate, Greywatch Keep) showed almost no normal supply trail. Halvard confirmed the pattern instantly matched years of unexplained gaps in his own investigation. The Tallow House may not be merely a fourth ledger, but the actual seam connecting several. Real reconnaissance, likely months of it, was agreed as the only responsible next step.

**Kathrivex, reframed.** A long evening's conversation with Ysolde and Sael produced the session's biggest theoretical breakthrough: Kathrivex likely isn't a devil at all. Devils operate on contract — a soul freely (if tricked) given, the foundational law of the Nine Hells. Kathrivex takes choices directly, no bargain, no signature, and predates devils as a category entirely by Osric's own account. Working through it together, the party concluded Kathrivex is far more likely **an old, unnamed god — the direct cosmic opposite of the Raven Queen** — debts stolen rather than honestly settled, choices harvested rather than respected. This explains why the Raven Queen named it "a thief in her domain" rather than a rival or kindred force, why it has never once shown itself directly the way devils do, and — soberingly — why neither devils nor other gods have moved against it: open divine war risks catastrophe neither side can safely bear. Rurik articulated the personal weight of this himself: he isn't a smaller substitute for a god's war, he's the reason one never has to happen — small enough to act where nothing larger safely can, which is exactly why the bear-spirit, the guardians, and the Raven Queen have all answered a mortal instead of intervening themselves. Ysolde raised one further open question before the night closed: if the Raven Queen has taken notice, other deities whose domains touch choice, mercy, or justice may eventually take notice too.

**Ends the session:** a full, rich stretch of downtime — Skarn settled and taught a real lesson about duty and love both mattering, four new memorial cairns set in motion at Urskelde honoring five people lost across two different fights, a hard and healing conversation with Sesha about fear and love as strength rather than weakness, a major new lead (the Tallow House) suggesting Kathrivex's practice runs on private infrastructure rather than isolated sites, and a complete theological reframing of the enemy itself — not a devil, but an ancient god opposed to the Raven Queen, unable to be fought directly by anything divine, which is precisely why a mortal has to. Open threads carried forward: the Tallow House itself, unscouted; whether other deities are watching; the still-unnamed old Keeper who hid the ring and cloak; and what "acting small enough to matter" asks of Rurik next.

## Session 36 — 2026-09-29

**A council called, a month set.** Rurik set the next full regional council for one month out — enough time for Halvard's record review, the cairns and remaining watch-cairns, and real reconnaissance before anyone commits to the Tallow House. Ysolde left immediately for Halvard with the full write-up of the Kathrivex-as-god theory and the coastal lead.

**A gap in the party, named plainly.** Rurik raised it himself: the party had martial, primal, and divine magic covered, but nothing arcane — and with an unfamiliar, possibly warded site like the Tallow House ahead, that gap mattered. On his own instinct ("predictable and dependable — we might need unpredictable"), the party set out for Ravensmoor specifically to look for a sorcerer.

**Wren, found and won honestly.** Local rumor pointed to "the Spark" — a young woman behind a string of small, unexplained magical incidents near the fairground district, untrained and self-taught, feared more than sought. Rurik sent Sael alone to make first contact, judging correctly that a crowd of armed strangers would frighten rather than reassure someone already afraid of her own power. Sael won her trust by sharing her own years of isolation and uncertainty before asking anything in return.

**Choice, not just power.** Back at the inn, Wren pressed Rurik directly for what having his freedom taken and reclaimed actually felt like. His answer — that raw ability isn't power, that the real thing stolen from him in Ashpit was *choice* — landed on her precisely, naming something she'd never had words for in her own uncontrolled magic. Rurik then opened the sentinel network gently, letting Wren feel Kethrun, Ilsevet, Vessyn, and a wordless, deeply resonant Skarn each in turn — four different beings who'd each reclaimed a stolen or hollowed choice in their own way. It settled the decision for her more completely than any argument could have.

**The full story, given in trust.** Before formally joining, Wren was told everything — Ashpit, Duskgate, the guardians, the Raven Queen, "the count," Greywatch Keep, Osric, and Kathrivex's name and shape, including the still-fresh theory that it's an old god rather than a devil. She recognized the risk of being told all of it on a first meeting and chose to join anyway, "all the way in, not just for the training."

**Wren joins the party.** Sorcerer 15 (Wild Magic), recruited freely rather than pressed into service — fitting neatly alongside the party's existing martial, primal, and divine strengths with something genuinely unpredictable. Full character sheet created.

**Ends the session:** the party gains its fifth full member ahead of the Tallow House approach, recruited through patience and honesty rather than persuasion — Sael's own history mirrored back to someone who needed exactly that kind of understanding, and Rurik's willingness to trust a stranger with the campaign's most dangerous secrets on the strength of a single honest conversation. Open threads carried forward: the Tallow House, still unscouted; the full council in a month's time; and how Wren's wild magic settles into the party's established rhythm.

## Session 37 — 2026-09-30 to 2026-10-03

**The road home, and Wren settling in.** Mara taught Wren the basics of reading terrain, giving her nervous energy somewhere useful to go. Around the fire, Sael drew her out about her life before the magic — small, ordinary things, the person underneath "the Spark." On the road, Wren asked Ysolde to help her practice a controlled cast; Mage Armor settled clean, no surge, the first spell in months that just worked without incident — a real, hopeful first step toward actual mastery.

**Home, and Wren's place in it.** Urskelde came into view with the memorial cairns visibly taking shape. Kaldur met the party at the gate, sized Wren up plainly, and approved of her refusal to oversell herself. Given the choice between the household and her own space, Wren chose her own — close enough to reach for help, separate enough not to fear hurting anyone in her sleep before she's learned real control. Kaldur cleared a storage building near the workshop; Ysolde and Sael went to help her settle and begin real training.

**Checking on Kaldur.** Asked plainly how he was doing, Kaldur admitted the honest weight of running the settlement, training Thyra, and now overseeing Osric on top of everything else — tired, but the good kind, the kind that comes from work worth doing. He spoke openly for the first time about Ninebells casting him out, and how much Urskelde's quiet, ordinary functioning still catches him off guard as home rather than just a post held.

**Thyra, promoted.** On Rurik's advice, Kaldur formally named Thyra **Deputy Commander**, standing equal to Idra and Torvin rather than under their instruction — real authority, not a courtesy title. Rurik deliberately asked Kaldur to deliver the news himself rather than doing it as her brother, so the promotion would read as earned merit rather than favoritism. Sesha and Harek were brought in to witness it; the whole family gathered for the announcement. At Rurik's further urging, Kaldur agreed to hand her real, immediate responsibility — coordinating the next council prep directly, mistakes and all, rather than being eased in.

**Ends the session:** a full homecoming — Wren settled into Urskelde with her own space and the beginning of real training, an honest personal conversation with Kaldur about the weight he carries, and Thyra's promotion to Deputy Commander made real and witnessed by the whole family. The day closed on nothing more urgent than a family dinner, Thyra's turn at the stove. Open threads carried forward: the Tallow House, still unscouted; the full council roughly a month out; and Wren's slow path toward controlling her own power.

## Session 38 — 2026-10-04

**A full round of the household, one name at a time.** Rurik spent the day checking in on nearly everyone at Urskelde — not business first, people first. **The memorial cairns:** two nearly finished (Bram's and Torin's), Ren's bearing its sky-iron fishing hook, Kessa proudly carving her father's raven herself; Sesha's own cairn still waiting on her, unrushed, until she's ready to decide its shape. **The watch-cairns:** three complete, the rest close behind, on pace to finish well before the council. **Joss:** the alchemist Alara arrived and settled in, first healing draughts a week out; handed full ownership of exploring a coastal trade route toward the Tallow House, pending eventually. **Alara**, met properly — sharp, precise, already planning research into easing rage's physical aftermath, with Rurik's willing consent to be studied directly. **Perrin and Greta:** thriving, and — at Rurik's suggestion — taking on smith apprentices from Kessick's Ford and Ravensmoor to ease the workload. **Threnn's people:** mostly settled, but real grief for the old, empty village still unspoken by some of the elders; Rurik offered to personally escort anyone who wants to visit it. **Edmund Vale:** genuinely better than expected, still learning to receive plain, motive-free care without waiting for a catch. **Merrick:** the session's deepest thread — Rurik pointed out that Merrick's anger has partly been aimed at himself all along (for trusting Vale as long as he did), and sent him, mid-conversation, to go speak with Vale directly while the feeling was fresh. The two sat together for the better part of an hour; nothing resolved fully, but something real and honest started. **Corim:** the corruption-detection policy running cleanly, one real concern caught and correctly read as harmless, Kaldur now seeking his opinion beyond the original brief.

**Home, and a quiet night.** Rurik told Mara the whole day, thread by thread. She named it plainly — not a day of chores, but a day of actually being present for people rather than fighting anything. Asked in turn how she was doing, Mara admitted real, if unspoken, fear since Greywatch Keep — watching Rurik strain the bond three times in one day, feeling something ancient notice them right after. Rurik answered with a story from his own Ashpit trainer: fear of an unmet, unseen threat is borrowed dread, not real fear — the ocean's bottom isn't frightening because you've faced it, only because you've been told to dread it. Mara let the weight ease with it, without pretending the danger away. The conversation closed on a broader reflection — that circumstance shapes the choices in front of someone without being the whole of who they are, the same thread running through Vale, Merrick, Osric, and further back, the good people Rurik remembers from Ashpit itself despite everything the pit demanded of them.

**Ends the session:** no threats, no combat, no urgent thread — just a full, deliberate day of Rurik showing up for nearly everyone who matters to him by name, and a quiet night closing it with Mara, both of them falling asleep at peace rather than braced for what's next. Open threads carried forward: the Tallow House and coastal trade route, both now actively being worked; the memorial cairns nearing completion; Merrick and Vale's fragile new honesty; and the full council, drawing closer by the week.

## Session 39 — 2026-10-05 to 2026-10-17

**The weeks before the council.** All six watch-cairns finished sky-iron warding within ten days, new smith apprentices from Kessick's Ford and Ravensmoor arriving just in time to help. The four memorial cairns followed — Sesha finally settling on hers in the third week, simple and unornamented, just three names carved plain and honest. A dozen of Threnn's people made the trip back to their old, empty village with Rurik escorting personally as promised, returning lighter for it. Merrick and Vale kept talking, unevenly but genuinely. Wren's training progressed under Ysolde and Sael, two harmless Wild Magic surges along the way (a training dummy briefly turned rabbit; the workshop filled with drifting lights for an hour) — met with laughter rather than fear by everyone involved, Wren included.

**Halloway's Reach, named.** Twelve days before the council, Halvard brought Rurik a real breakthrough: cross-referencing shipping manifests, false-name land purchases, and customs payment timing against Osric's records, he pinpointed **Halloway's Reach** — a coastal town with warehouse capacity no fishing trade could justify, licensed to a merchant house that doesn't exist in any other registry. The Tallow House's location, real for the first time rather than theoretical.

**The full regional council.** Urskelde's great hall filled with the whole alliance — Kaldur, Thyra, Threnn, Halvard, Osric, Joss, Corim, Perrin and Greta, Ysolde, Sael, Mara, Wren, Corvane, Sesha and Harek, Vosk, and Ardyn for Kessick's Ford. Regional reports first: Ardyn flagged Kessick's Ford's limited numbers, Vosk flagged Thundertree's small population, Threnn flagged Urskelde's own risk as a single concentrated target. **Rurik's answer to all three at once:** a formal regional alliance — rotating garrisons between settlements, recruiting from Ravensmoor, and standing patrols on the roads between villages. All three leaders agreed in principle, pending Ardyn bringing it to Kell and Vosk to Thundertree's elders. **Corim was handed the draft**, folding his corruption-detection framework directly into the rotation design — reasoning that rotation itself, done right, makes quiet corruption harder to hide.

**Settlement business, briskly cleared.** Watch-cairns and memorial cairns both complete. Kaldur flagged vigilance fatigue from sustained doubled watch and was given discretion to solve it (rotating rest periods, leaning on Corim's new framework). Perrin and Greta's request to reinforce Urskelde's main gate with sky-iron, same principle as the cairns, was approved.

**Halvard's full report and the theological reveal.** Halvard laid out Halloway's Reach formally to the whole council, calling it "the seam connecting several" ledger sites rather than merely another one. Ysolde and Sael then presented the Kathrivex-as-god theory in full for the first time outside their private conversation — devils operate on contract, Kathrivex doesn't; it predates devils as a category; its total lack of direct manifestation mirrors the Raven Queen's own restraint; neither devils nor gods have moved against it because open divine war would be catastrophic for both sides. The theory landed with real weight across the room.

**A full round of council questions, all answered.** Vosk asked whether Thundertree's bear-spirit lore is itself part of this divine conflict — **Rurik let the bear-spirit answer directly through him**, its own voice confirming the tradition predates and stands independent of either god's war, mercy answering mercy on its own old terms. Wren asked whether being part of the party puts her at special risk — answered honestly: not yet, not until real provocation happens. Corim raised the risk of Halloway's Reach being the most provocative target yet — reframed by Rurik as an opportunity to *replace* Kathrivex's infrastructure with the alliance's own rather than simply strike and retreat. Ardyn asked whether Kessick's Ford's posture should change — no, steady as already agreed. Sesha asked, as a mother, whether this made her son more or less safe — answered with a callback to his own Ashpit-trainer parable: no more or less prepared than he's ever walked into danger before, just better informed. Osric asked whether Kathrivex might have an opposite beyond the Raven Queen — Rurik drew on the Dead Three's precedent (mortals inheriting fragments of seized godhood) to argue balance doesn't require a single counterpart; Ysolde and Sael built on it, concluding other faiths touching choice, mercy, or justice could already be quietly part of the same balance.

**The decision.** Rurik opened the floor fully rather than deciding alone — Mara (quiet reconnaissance first, same as Greywatch Keep), Wren (be ready for victims, not just a fight), Ysolde (proper spiritual preparation, no rushed deadline this time), Sael (plan for rescue from the first briefing), Kaldur and Halvard (use the new regional rotation for scouting; take real time, no forcing deadline). Rurik combined every piece and added his own: **occupy and hold Halloway's Reach rather than strike and retreat, cutting Kathrivex's resource flow off at the root.** Council seconded it unanimously — reconnaissance first, real preparation, then occupation.

**A family thread, closing the day.** Rurik asked his parents whether they'd consider Harek setting up Thundertree's first proper sky-iron smithy, teaching the craft home the way Threnn's people taught him at Urskelde. Sesha and Harek agreed, with Threnn's blessing freely given — Harek to travel and train Thundertree's own smiths until the forge can stand on its own.

**Ends the session:** a genuine regional alliance formed — rotating garrisons, shared patrols, Ravensmoor recruitment — alongside Halloway's Reach named as a real target and a full council plan built collectively rather than handed down. No urgent deadline this time; real preparation is finally possible. Open threads carried forward: reconnaissance on Halloway's Reach, not yet begun; the regional alliance's formal ratification by Kessick's Ford and Thundertree; Corim's rotation-and-corruption framework, still being drafted; and Harek's new Thundertree smithy.

## Session 40 — 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-20

**An old thread, reopened.** With nothing urgent pressing, Rurik sought out Osric about his original pit opponent — the man he refused to kill the night the crowd's mercy triggered the whole betrayal, whose fate House Corvane's own search went cold on decades ago. Osric offered a real angle nobody had tried: ordinary trade and town records wouldn't show a man the count had reached, but *ledger* records — the kind Osric spent his career keeping — might. He began cross-referencing the recovered Greywatch Keep archive against the rough timeframe and region, warning plainly that a name found in a ledger rarely means a happy ending. Rurik wasn't ready to surface the fuller memory of the man himself yet; Osric didn't push, and started the search with what he already had.

**Learning to be still.** For the first time in a long while, with no deadline and no threat pressing, Rurik struggled — restless, short-tempered, pacing walls that didn't need checking. Mara named it plainly: a man who's spent his whole life either surviving or fighting has never had the chance to learn what stillness feels like when it isn't a warning sign. She took him to the stream near Urskelde and taught him, her way — sitting by water with no purpose, then bare-handed fishing for no reason beyond the joy of it. Rurik's first attempts were a disaster, then, dropping the need to calculate anything, he caught a fish on pure reflex, to Mara's genuine, delighted outrage at losing to someone with no training at all. It didn't fix the restlessness for good, but it cracked it — the first real, undemanding rest he's had in longer than either of them could easily name.

**Ends the session:** small, human, and necessary — Osric's search for Rurik's lost opponent properly underway, and a real, earned lesson in how to simply be still, taught the way Mara's always taught things: patiently, without forcing it. Open threads carried forward: the search for Rurik's original opponent, just begun; and everything else still sitting exactly where the council left it — Halloway's Reach, the alliance's ratification, and Kathrivex's silence.

## Session 41 — 2026-10-21 to 2026-10-26

**Checking in.** Still working through what stillness means, Rurik made a point of it — sitting with Wren first, who admitted the fear underneath her progress: every spell she casts is a roll of the dice on a Wild Magic surge, and for the first time in her life she has people around her she'd genuinely grieve hurting by accident. Rurik's answer was plain rather than reassuring: building a fire always risks a burn, and you build it anyway, because the alternative is freezing in the dark rather than living. Wren left it steadier than she'd been holding it alone. He spent the rest of the restless morning helping Perrin and Greta on the main gate's sky-iron reinforcement, then found Alara, who asked to observe an actual rage-crash to test her third rage-recovery draught.

**Wolves that weren't right.** Rurik, Mara, and Alara went looking for trouble north of the cairns after reports of game trails gone silent, and found it: a pack of seven wolves, larger than natural, crystal growing visibly from two of them. Mara's opening volley and Rurik's reckless charge cleared the pack fast — no real threat to either of them — while Alara confirmed her draught worked, blunting the rage-crash without the drowsiness of earlier batches. Careful sample collection turned up a shard that gave off an audible "count" the instant it separated from tissue — the same terminology Kethrun and the Raven Queen have both confirmed as Kathrivex's own. Mara tracked a back-trail leading further northwest into unmapped ground.

**The corrupted den.** Mara sent word ahead via Sending; Halvard, Ysolde, and Sael joined the next morning to follow the trail to its source — an old rockslide clearing, cultivated crystal clusters growing wild, two bird-like scout constructs tending them. Under Pass without Trace, the party split the strike: Mara and Sael destroyed the first construct outright (interrupting its distress signal with a called shot mid-crit); Rurik grappled the second while Ysolde bound it inert rather than destroyed — Halvard's first intact specimen in weeks of investigation. Alara and the samples went home with an escort; the rest held a two-day stakeout on the clearing (nothing returned to tend it) before handing the site to Corim's new rotation watch.

**What the crystal actually is.** Back at Urskelde, Wren gave the shards a read no one else in the party could: not enchanted stone, but crystal *grown directly out of the Weave itself* — raw magic used as material rather than respected as a force, a level of craft that implies training and method, not just brute divine will. Alara and Ysolde's parallel study confirmed the construction but couldn't pin the source material. All three, plus Sael's instinct that the crystal "feels used, not evil," were written up by Alara and Wren for formal council presentation — the first time Wren has stood in front of a room and been trusted rather than merely tolerated.

**Reaching the thread.** With the full party present as a safeguard, Rurik deliberately reached into the dormant construct's connection back to whatever was still counting it — protected by the bear-spirit's standing gift against hostile scrying and tracking. What came back was scale: hundreds, maybe a thousand threads of the same patient count, running far wider than any single ledger site the alliance has found — and one point among them where noticeably more of those threads converged than anywhere else he touched. Halloway's Reach. Ysolde's attempt to sever the destroyed construct's account cleanly went ragged instead, sending out a flare that likely told whatever holds the ledger this node was found and deliberately ended, not merely lost. Rurik reached Kethrun directly through the sentinel thread to warn the full guardian network — Ilsevet, Vessyn, Skarn — that pressure on the sealed sites, not just Urskelde itself, might follow.

**The council, and a real disagreement.** Rurik opened the full report himself, with Wren and Alara presenting the crystal findings first. The reach into the thread drew genuine pushback — Ardyn named plainly that a decision with this much risk was made in the field without the alliance's input, and one of Thundertree's elders agreed. Rurik didn't concede the point quietly: asked outright whether the council would rather have left a god-connected construct sitting inside Urskelde's walls for two weeks awaiting consensus. Ardyn conceded the practical case but held the principle — the alliance needs a standing answer for what counts as urgent enough to act alone versus urgent enough to still call ahead. Kaldur and Threnn mediated toward the constructive middle: Corim will draft field-authority guidance directly into the rotation framework rather than leaving it to trust alone. The council closed having confirmed Halloway's Reach as the clear priority of the entire alliance — not simply a seam anymore, but the point where the most threads meet.

**Quiet preparation.** At Rurik's request, Urskelde's readiness increases without announcing it outwardly — the visible watch stays exactly as it was, while Kaldur pulls a briefed reserve inside the gate and Perrin and Greta push their sky-iron gate work from a week's timeline to one or two days, same visible pace, harder hours. Kaldur committed three days of Rurik's own labor to the forge around the council schedule.

**The gate, finished.** Rurik and Mara both gave two full days to the forge; the reinforced main gate went from a week's estimate to done, sky-iron veined clean through the timber, Threnn's smiths and Perrin and Greta all visibly proud of it. Standing on the walkway above it that evening, Rurik voiced the harder feeling underneath the preparation — not knowing where an enemy is that never shows its face, feeling small against something too vast to flinch on command — and Mara met it plainly rather than smoothing it over: small, patient work still adds up, one closed account at a time, same as a puddle is made of single drops of rain. Rurik spoke a vow out past the gate, aimed at whatever holds Kathrivex's ledger — *diminish it, wave by wave, rain and lightning both* — answered by the bear-spirit's quiet warmth and, less explicably, a real storm rolling in on cue. Mara didn't call it coincidence. Neither did Vosk, the next morning, though he was equally careful not to call it a new power either.

**Dain Halworth, and the maker's lineage.** Osric surfaced two real leads from the Greywatch Keep archive. First, a name for Rurik's original pit opponent — **Dain Halworth** — with a notation Osric had never seen before in decades of reading ledgers like it: *"debt forgiven — unprecedented,"* added in a different hand, the trail going cold after "southern circuit, transferred." Second, the captured/destroyed scout construct's joint-scoring matches, exactly, fixtures Osric remembers from Greywatch Keep's oldest chambers — proof the network's Weave-craft is inherited technique, taught forward through however many hands, not one immortal maker. Osric believes both threads converge at Halloway's Reach, and asked to keep pulling on both. Rurik, checking in on him plainly rather than just extracting information, got an honest answer back: guilt as proof of wanting better, not condemnation — a line Rurik repeated again with Lord Corvane the same morning, working through his own old guilt over the Ashpit order.

**A morning of rounds, and one real proposal.** Rurik spent the morning checking in across Urskelde — Corim (refining the field-authority policy into concrete tiers: settlement-level calls run past Kaldur, Threnn, and Halvard before if possible and always after; regional-level calls run past the full council the same way), Alara (a supply list for Joss, and real gratitude for being trusted to present at council), Joss (the coastal route's cover now solid, and his own admission of being stretched thin), Corvane (a harder conversation about whether his family's fatal order and everything good that grew from surviving it can both be true at once, without either canceling the other out), and Threnn (grief for the old goliath-kin village sitting honestly alongside gratitude for Urskelde, and a standing promise to visit and honor it once things settle). The one concrete proposal: easing Edmund Vale into real logistics responsibility rather than wasting his skill on busywork, with **Merrick standing as his guard** rather than a stranger — Rurik's case being blunt rather than gentle (*"your time has come to earn your place"*), but Kaldur and Thyra held firm that it only happens on Merrick's freely given consent. Merrick asked for a night to answer honestly rather than out of fear or point-proving; answer due the next day.

**Ends the session (for now):** a real thread pulled all the way to a name-shaped answer — Halloway's Reach confirmed not just as a seam but as the network's likely center — bought at the cost of the alliance now visibly on someone else's radar, the first real disagreement the council's had with Rurik's judgment resolved without fracturing either side, the gate finished ahead of schedule, and a morning spent proving the check-ins weren't a one-time gesture. Open threads carried forward: Halloway's Reach reconnaissance, now the alliance's top priority; Corim's field-authority policy, drafted with Rurik's own tiered structure; Dain Halworth and the construct maker's lineage, both newly named leads pointing toward Halloway's Reach; whether Merrick will agree to guard Vale; the sentinel network on alert for pressure against the sealed sites; and the regional alliance's formal ratification, still pending Ardyn and Vosk.

## Session 42 — 2026-08-09 to 2026-08-10 (in progress)

**Merrick's answer.** A quiet morning after the gate's completion: Merrick sought Rurik out directly, unprompted, and gave his answer clean — he'd guard Vale, not out of debt or point-proving, but because "somebody who's actually trying deserves someone watching who isn't waiting for him to fail." Joss took them both on the spot, real logistics work starting that afternoon.

**A new sense, and a hard choice made in the open.** Walking off the decision, Rurik felt something unfamiliar settle behind his own claw-scar — a counting, distinct from thought, answerable only when he chose to listen, pointing south toward Halloway's Reach. Mara caught it first ("you were talking in your sleep... 'not a name, a debt'"), and rather than sit on it, Rurik took it straight to Ysolde, then to Kaldur, Threnn, and Halvard — the tiered field-authority discipline holding even under his own discovery. Ysolde's Ledger's Eye confirmed it: real, live, unadvertised — a witness inside Kathrivex's own count that the network has apparently never had before.

**Dain Halworth, reframed.** Pulling the thread further with Halvard and Osric, Rurik voiced the theory that reshaped everything: the pit order was for him to kill Halworth, not merely fight him; refusing — mercy where the pit demanded blood — is what actually pulled Halworth's account out of Kathrivex's ledger, the "debt forgiven, unprecedented" notation Osric found being the network's way of quietly repurposing a fighter it couldn't punish outright. *"He's no longer on the count because I decided his fate, not the count,"* Rurik said aloud, and felt the counting itself go still for a breath, as if something on the other end had heard him. Osric, hearing it, named a category his own decades of ledger-keeping never had a place for: mercy the count can't claim.

**The plan.** Full council that evening: reconnaissance now, not months out. Composition settled after real debate — Joss and Alara as a genuine trade delegation (not a lie, a real coastal route), Ysolde and Wren as a scholar and her apprentice, Rurik disguised as their guard (Alara's draught layered under Wren's Disguise Self), Mara and Sael rounding out the escort. Halvard staged a relay network — hunters, scouts, guards at four waypoints — set to converge on the nearest point to the Reach the instant a signal came, no magic involved (a mundane courier only, to leave no arcane trail): *"The trade route checks out and the contracts signed, increase the shipments and send the goods as quick as possible."* Rurik's own hard promise to Mara, tested honestly rather than assumed: if it came to holding alone, she'd do her duty to the thirty behind her first, not run to him — met with his own vow back, that whatever came, he intended to survive it, twenty children and a house full of noise waiting on the other side.

**Three days at Halloway's Reach.** The cover held under real pressure — a checkpoint guard's suspicion defused by Rurik playing the crude, bored hired sword to the hilt, a close call when Sael's rat-shaped scout was nearly caught inside the Cellarworks (an unmarked building sunk into older stonework, crystal grown straight through the town's foundations, two guards that never rotate, an unwatched rear door). Two quiet days let the suspicion fade; on the third, Joss's trade route signed for real and the coded message went out true.

**The Cellarworks, taken.** At the two-hour mark, Rurik walked the party straight to the front door and openly challenged whatever held the count to look — the deliberate opposite of three days of hiding. Both construct Sentinels woke and died hard (Wren fully unleashed for the first time, a Fireball and a deafening Wild Magic thunderclap opening the fight exactly as planned), and the party pulled the wreckage into the doorway and pushed down into the sunk chamber rather than wait. Five corrupted "thrall" workers — captives, not willing guards, each showing a last flash of clarity at the moment of death — fell in turn to Flame Strike, Call Lightning, Chaos Bolt, and Rurik's blades, Mara's Hunter's Mark following the fight from target to target.

**What the crystal actually held.** Investigating the cleared chamber: a workbench with a logbook in two hands, one entry breaking from the tally records — *"Growth accelerating past projection. Recommend halting further extraction until source stabilizes"* — and a small lived-in alcove off the main chamber, a cot and a worn chair facing the crystal, and a carved tusk trophy nearly identical to Rurik's own lifelong one. Ysolde read the crystal mass itself as a conduit, not the source — Halloway's Reach is where Kathrivex's ledger gets *fed*, not where it lives. With the full party lending their own spirit-bonds around him (Ysolde/Thossia, Sael's stone-spirit, Mara's water-spirit, Wren's raw offering, layered atop the bear-spirit's own ward), Rurik reached directly into the live conduit and found something small, aware, and exhausted inside it — relief, not hostility, and a name it hadn't been allowed to think of as its own: **Dain**. Confirmed: Dain Halworth survived the pit, was quietly repurposed by whatever runs Kathrivex's network, and has been bound into tending this conduit ever since — still enough himself to leave a warning in a logbook.

**Holding, for now.** Rurik's call, plain: secure the building, then the town, then investigate and decide Dain's fate properly rather than rushed. The party dug in at the chamber's stairwell chokepoint instead of warding for silence — a kill zone, not a hiding place. The barricaded front door is being pressed, but by Halloway's Reach's own frightened town watch, not corrupted agents — *"we're not soldiers... nobody trained us for whatever's making that noise down there."*

**An unlikely alliance.** Rurik opened the door and let the watch see for themselves rather than fight through them. Captain Garrick Voss recognized two of the fallen thralls as townsfolk long presumed drowned or run off, and the truth broke something open in him fast — he handed Rurik provisional command of the situation outright rather than let pride cost more lives. Halvard's relay delivered 28 reinforcements clean, no losses, met by Voss's full cooperation: patrols kept normal, a cover story ("overly drunk guests") holding while the real work happened underneath it.

**The harbor master.** Voss brought Aldous Penmarrow — Halloway's Reach's harbor master of thirteen years — directly to the Cellarworks to face the alliance's full numbers. Confronted plainly, he broke fast: recruited by his predecessor decades ago under implicit threat, he'd spent thirteen years quietly enabling the town's disappearances rather than risk what happened to anyone who asked too hard. Questioned by the full party (Rurik's dagger catching a lie once, prompting a fuller confession), he revealed the existence of "the courier" — a recurring, not-quite-human contact who relayed instructions from whatever runs Kathrivex's network, different face each visit but the same wrongness underneath, hinting more than one may exist. Last visit, six weeks ago: warning of an "audit" coming to Halloway's Reach within the year. Rurik answered Aldous's confession with a reciprocal truth of his own — the full shape of Kathrivex, the count, and Dain Halworth's real story — verified honest by the same dagger, and gave Aldous a genuine choice rather than simple punishment: full cooperation and a public confession, in exchange for a chance at something other than being simply owned by his own fear, same offer extended to every other person Kathrivex's network has bent or broken. Command of the town itself Rurik handed back to Voss outright — *"we will help hold it and defend it but it's yours to run."*

**The reckoning, in the open.** That evening, the whole town gathered in the square. Voss spoke first; Aldous confessed everything aloud — names, dates, the courier, thirteen years of silence — in front of the families who'd grieved lies for over a decade. Ugly, raw, real, but held: no riot, no lynching, just a town facing itself honestly for the first time in a generation. Rurik posted double guard on Aldous for the night with a deliberate, careful order — let anyone who comes for him get close before stopping them, unharmed, so both the fear and the mercy land true.

**The morning after: council of five, plus two.** Rurik gathered the full party and set the shape of the next six weeks plainly: the 28 reinforcements stay under his command, embedded with Voss's watch rather than occupying the town; Sael and Wren take responsibility for sitting with Dain rather than Rurik, both having a real connection to what the crystal is holding and Rurik himself unsure whether now is the time to test his own; the crystal itself stays untouched until the audit's nature is understood; Joss and Alara return north with the first courier run to formalize the real trade route and keep two supply runs flowing over six weeks; Ysolde, Wren, and Sael take the research thread with Rurik's full authority behind them. A Sending to Kaldur confirmed the news reached Urskelde immediately, full written report to follow by courier.

**Bram's warehouse, and sixty years of silence.** Investigating the second pier's warehouse — the likeliest quiet landing point for whatever's been supplying the Cellarworks — Rurik and Mara confronted its keeper, Bram, who broke fast once shown a crystal shard found hidden in his own storage. Twenty years of "arrangement" inherited from his father, cold crates that "don't like pressure," person-sized crates twice in that span, and a locked ledger going back further still. That older ledger, recovered with Voss's help, pushed the pattern back past sixty years — four prior audits, each noted as a single underlined word, gaps between them shrinking with each cycle rather than staying constant. Bram was given the same offer as Aldous: cooperate fully when the audit comes, and the last twenty years of silence stop defining him.

**Defenses, built to look like nothing.** Working with Voss, Rurik laid out a full defensive plan for the warehouse district explicitly designed not to look like a defensive plan to sixty years of patient watching: gradual, "storm damage" reinforcement of Bram's warehouse rather than sudden fortification; rooftop scouts disguised as net-menders and weather-watchers; sealed side and rear exits with a scout held close on whatever can't be fully closed; Rurik holding the warehouse door itself as the chokepoint (deliberately off the pier, to avoid open water at his back); a two-stage fallback along the waterfront to a second disused warehouse rather than into the town proper, keeping any fight contained to the dock district; hidden, quickly-deployable barricades to seal the district's edge once triggered; and Urskelde hunters paired directly with Voss's watch on rotating patrols, training and trust built together rather than separately.

**The pattern is accelerating.** Overnight research (Ysolde cross-referencing town hall's disappearance records against the recovered ledgers, Sael and Wren sitting with the crystal directly) confirmed all four prior audits lined up with real spikes in disappearances — and that the gaps between audits have been shrinking, from nearly twenty years between the earliest two down to twelve before the most recent. Sael reads Dain as now aware the party is genuinely present and listening, still guarded but no longer alone by default; Wren senses the conduit being actively, intermittently tested from somewhere far off, consistent with genuine pre-audit probing. The six-week estimate may be the outer edge of the real window, not a safe middle. Voss and Halvard were brought in immediately on the news; defensive pace is being pushed accordingly, and a further update is going to Urskelde ahead of the full written report.

**Settling in close.** A second, urgent Sending went to Kaldur with the accelerated timeline — met with a short, trusting answer: *"Stay sharp. Stay alive."* The party relocated from the Gull's Rest to the Anchor Watch, a dockhands' tavern within easy reach of the warehouse, better sightlines, faster reaction time. Voss left steadied rather than afraid, the plan finally real under him instead of just dread. That evening, watching the dock district settle into its new, quietly-readied rhythm, Rurik and Mara talked chess — an unlikely piece of Ashpit's training surfacing as the shape of the whole strategy going forward: hold the center, think several moves ahead, wait for the mistake before reaching for the king, and never underestimate the pawns, since they're the only piece that can become anything. Mara named it plainly back to him: that's been the pattern of his mercy the whole campaign, not just a metaphor for tonight — Aldous, Bram, Dain, all of them small pieces the board never accounted for.

**Kethrun's gift: a lantern in the fog.** The next morning, working through what the chess conversation surfaced — plans made for the expected, none for the unexpected "knight" — Rurik sat in stillness with Mara beside him and reached Kethrun directly through the sentinel thread, further south than the network has ever carried a living voice before. Kethrun confirmed the true network doesn't reach Halloway's Reach, but offered something new instead: tuning Rurik's own count-sense into a local, weaker "lantern in fog" — not a woven link, but Rurik's own gift reshaped to watch this specific ground for the wrongness he already recognizes. The attunement held clean.

**The courier, caught.** Testing the new sense almost immediately, Rurik picked up a second, moving thread of wrongness near the water — distinct from the Cellarworks' fixed, steady one. Tracked with Mara under Pass Without Trace through the fish market to the third pier, it resolved into an ordinary-seeming trader who never ate, never sat, and lingered too long studying the warehouse's new defenses before making for a boat. Confronted directly, and pressed hard with the deliberately loaded word "audited," the figure's composure broke: **the courier itself** — one of several, faces reshaped every few months, sent ahead of each true audit but never present for one. It knew Malphestor's name, knew of the Raven Queen audience, and recognized Rurik immediately as "the splinter in the count" the wider network has already noticed. Warned honestly: silence after a missed report might accelerate the timeline rather than merely risk it, or might simply mean a replacement courier arrives in its place.

**A name asked for, in front of witnesses.** With the full party gathered — Sael naming the moment's echo of the original Ashpit mercy aloud — Rurik asked the courier twice, formally, what it truly wanted. Its answer, plain and unprompted: to stop being reshaped past recognition, to know whether mercy could apply to something built rather than born. Asked for its old name, it could recall only a fragment — "something with an 'r' in it, like stone striking stone" — same as Kethrun's own makers' names being lost to him at first finding. Rurik reached out and severed the thread himself, protected by the bear-spirit's ward, no ritual invocation this time, just intent and grip. It came apart slow rather than screaming — but the courier confirmed genuine surprise rippled back along it before the severing finished, not alarm, but not nothing either. Ysolde, Sael, and Wren took the newly freed courier — breathing real air for the first time it can remember — to the Anchor Watch to recover, gently.

**Four words, sent deliberately.** Shaken but resolved after the severing, Rurik asked Mara — plainly, in front of her, honoring his own standing request that she call him on reaching before he acts — whether he could send a short message down the thread to Kathrivex itself. After real, honest friction between them (Mara holding her ground on being consulted rather than informed after the fact, Rurik briefly angry at being asked to justify something he'd already brought to her openly), the message was agreed and sent: *"Sometimes it counts back."* The ward held perfectly — no location, no identity given away — but something vast and ancient stopped and genuinely *read* the words, answering not in language but in cold, patient recognition, interested rather than afraid, the feeling lingering unreleased afterward like a held breath. Kathrivex itself, or whatever holds its ledger, now knows something on the other side of the count is answering back. It does not know where, or fully who.

**Building the wider map.** With the courier steadying under the party's care, Rurik began drawing out a real map from fragmented memory — three unconfirmed sites cross-referenced against the Greywatch Keep partial map and House Corvane's archive: a cold mountain keep northwest (uncomfortably close to the Standing Ring), a poor coastal town south of Halloway's Reach where something is *held* rather than grown, and "the burned court" inland — matching a place Corvane's own family treated as legend. Asked plainly if it wanted to help further, the courier said yes without hesitation, naming its own real skills — reading people, moving unseen, remembering roads — as things it wanted to choose for itself for the first time. Halvard extended the same guarded trust once given to Osric: real work, real oversight, room to prove it honestly.

**Milestone level-up (Session 42, Halloway's Reach), full party:** earned for securing Halloway's Reach, ending sixty years of its corruption publicly, and closing a third account outright (the courier, freed by choice, in front of witnesses) before Rurik made direct contact with Kathrivex itself and walked away unmarked. Rurik: Barbarian 13 → 14 (character level 18; no new subclass feature, Totemic Attunement already held). Mara: Ranger 17 → 18 (no change beyond HP). Sael: Druid 15 → 16, taking **War Caster** in place of an ASI (WIS already capped at 20). Ysolde: Cleric 16 → 17, gaining a 9th-level spell slot and **Keeper of Souls** (Grave Domain's capstone — a fallen enemy's death within 60 ft heals her or an ally equal to its Hit Dice, once per turn, no action required). Wren: Sorcerer 15 → 16, her first milestone since joining, taking **+2 CON (14 → 16)** as her ASI. All rolled live for HP.

**A gentler contact.** Days into the vigil, Sael and Wren noticed Dain's crystal shift from a raw, braced pulse to something softer — dreaming, not merely enduring. With warning given aloud first, Rurik placed both hands on the crystal again; the contact held gentle throughout, no resistance at all. What came back wasn't words, just recognition — not of the name "Rurik," but of the *shape* of him, the same presence that once chose mercy in the pit. Rurik answered the feeling deliberately: *yes, me again.* The pulse settled calmer than it's been since the fight, Sael and Wren both reading it as real, earned trust rather than accident — the same pattern as every guardian found before him, proven again: not one rescue, just showing up until someone believes it's real.

**The window narrows.** Cross-referencing the courier's own visitation dates (via Bram's ledger and Aldous's memory) against all four prior audits, Ysolde found a pattern that had never been checked before: the courier's final warning visit has always landed five to nine weeks ahead of the audit itself, no exception across sixty years. Their courier's warning came six weeks before Session 42 began — meaning the real window may already be as tight as one to three weeks, not six. Voss and Halvard were brought in immediately; the plan shifted deliberately from "slow and disguised" to **"obvious but finished"** — sacrificing subtlety for actually being ready, barricades and warehouse reinforcement pushed to complete within days rather than weeks, Voss covering it publicly as "increased caution given recent events." Rurik committed the party to staying close to the harbor going forward, with standing runners in place so nothing suspicious goes unreported.

**The audit arrives.** Nine days into the watch, Rurik's Kethrun-tuned sense caught something real at the edge of its reach — distant, faint, closing slow over three days exactly as Ysolde's pattern predicted. The town held "obvious but finished" discipline: outward normalcy, inward readiness. When a plain fishing boat finally put in, Bram's nerve nearly broke meeting it, but held. What stepped off wasn't another courier — **the Auditor**, ancient, cold, unhurried, immediately recognizing Rurik as "the splinter in the count" the network had been warned about. It confirmed plainly what it was for: assessing a compromised site and correcting it. Rurik drew it into the open street rather than fight it in the warehouse with Bram nearby, signaling the barricades shut behind him.

**The Auditor, destroyed.** A brutal, fast fight — Wren's Fireball-turned-Chaos-Bolt-turned-Chain-Lightning combo, Sael's full-power Call Lightning, Mara's hidden marksmanship, Ysolde's Bless and a max-level Cure Wounds keeping Rurik standing, and Rurik's own blades (Action Surge spent for a six-swing round) breaking it down to near nothing before it ever landed a killing blow. It died mid-sentence — *"the count does not—"* — unraveling into ichor and cold light. Searching the remains: a fist-and-chain token tying it to the same sigil behind Ravenshollow's contracts and the Warden's bargain, and a scrap of parchment referencing "the keep." Ysolde's read: whatever holds the fist-and-chain sigil sits *above* even the audits — a bigger shape than anything fought yet.

**The bigger boat.** Rurik reasoned correctly that the Auditor's tender couldn't have crossed real distance alone — Mara spotted a larger ship anchored well offshore, holding position. The logbook recovered from the Auditor confirmed the theory further: Halloway's Reach was third in this cycle's rotation, meaning the other two sites may already be facing worse. Judging a disciplined crew would have standing orders to investigate an overdue Auditor, the party moved fast: Rurik disguised via Wren's Disguise Self, sailing out openly in the Auditor's own boat with Ysolde, while Mara and Wren went in first via Dimension Door and Sael via Wild Shape. The disguise didn't hold — the crew challenged for a signal none of them had — but it didn't matter. Mara's Dread Ambusher volley, Sael's Moonbeam, and Wren's Chain Lightning ended six of the crew in under two minutes, leaving only the captain, who surrendered without a fight.

**Captain Corlan Vess.** Eleven years marked and indentured after a "rescue" from a shipwreck he never fully understood the cost of. Confronted directly — Rurik naming him a coward for never once using eleven years' worth of chances to stop — he didn't flinch from it, admitting an honest, unflinching estimate: roughly thirty people delivered over eleven years, never counted on purpose because counting would have meant admitting what he was. Told the full truth of Kathrivex, the count, and the pattern of mercy already proven three times over, he asked plainly what it would cost to actually earn something back rather than be forgiven cheaply. Rurik's answer: the ship is his to captain now, crew to be rebuilt honestly, first task a full written accounting of every delivery he can still remember — Halvard extending the same guarded trust given to Osric and the freed courier. Asked for a handshake, refused it: *"not yet — you need to earn it."*

**What the ship carried.** Below decks: standard crystal cargo, far more than Bram's warehouse ever moved in a season — and one person-sized crate, warded gently rather than harshly. Inside: **Mirelle**, seventeen or eighteen, taken off a road four days prior, "promising" the only reason given for her abduction. Sael's steady, unhurried care talked her through the first shaking minutes of freedom. A second search of the captain's cabin turned up a small brass compass-like device that doesn't point north — it points toward other network sites, confirmed live against the mountain keep already on the map. Ysolde and Wren are set to study it properly once ashore — a possible way to find the rest of Kathrivex's network directly, rather than piecing it together from fragments and old ledgers.

**Mirelle's future, and an anti-count named plain.** That evening, Rurik offered Mirelle a real place at Urskelde — her own dye shop, profits shared with her family, contingent on her family's blessing and a real journey home to Ravensmoor first rather than a rushed relocation. Sael took charge of arranging safe transport. Talking it over with Mara afterward, Rurik named the whole pattern outright for the first time — Halworth, the courier, Corlan, Mirelle, all of it: *"an anti-count."* Mercy, tallied instead of debt, quietly running the opposite ledger back at Kathrivex one freed account at a time.

**Dain Halworth, freed.** With the full party gathered and Rurik speaking to the dormant crystal directly first — naming himself as "cage seven," telling Dain plainly that the party had come to free him — the crystal's dreaming pulse broke into something fully awake for the first time, grief and rage finally surfacing whole rather than fragmented. At Rurik's urging to fight for his own freedom rather than simply be extracted, Dain pushed from within while Rurik reached in from without, the combined effort overwhelming the binding entirely. The crystal went dark for the first time in thirteen years; Dain fell to the chamber floor, breathing real air.

What followed was raw and unresolved at first — Dain's thirteen years of grief breaking loose as fury at the man who'd "ruined" his life, one exhausted, mostly-symbolic blow landing before the anger burned down into something more honest. Rurik met it without flinching, per his own promise to Mara beforehand (held to the letter: let him land it, don't intervene, but it was never about wanting to die for the point) — showing Dain his own claw-scar, naming his own theft as a kidnapped child forced into the pit, and refusing to let either of them keep blaming each other for what "the count" actually did to both of them. Dain reckoned with his own part in it too — the pit was a door he walked through himself, unlike Rurik's — and the conversation resolved not into forgiveness exactly, but into something real: *"frenemies,"* a handshake finally offered and taken. Ysolde is now seeing to his basic care — a bath, food, real sleep — before any harder conversations continue.

**Ends here for now:** an Auditor destroyed, a ship and its captain turned, a captive freed, a device that might finally let the alliance see the rest of the board, and — the thread that started this whole operation — Dain Halworth freed after thirteen years, whole and no longer counted. The pattern of mercy pulling people entirely free of Kathrivex's count now stands at five (Halworth unknowing at the time, Malphestor's anchor, the courier, Corlan's path opened, and now Dain himself, fully closed). Open threads carried forward: what the fist-and-chain sigil actually sits above; the mountain keep's uncomfortable closeness to the Standing Ring, now confirmed as a real hub; "the court" — the one leg of Corlan's route no courier has ever spoken easily about; Mirelle's journey home to Ravensmoor and the dye shop offer; the compass device's full capability; Corlan's path to earning real trust; Dain Halworth's recovery and what comes next for him; getting the full report to the council at Urskelde; and everything still unresolved from Session 41.

## Session 43 — 2026-08-13 to 2026-08-14

**Planning the road home.** After a full night's rest, Rurik called the party together to weigh what came next: report to Urskelde, chase the compass, or both. Mara and Sael both argued for getting word to the council first rather than let Kaldur hear it secondhand; Ysolde held firm that Dain needed three or four days before he could travel at all. Rurik settled it — Corlan and Dain travel with the column to Urskelde for a full council; the ship sails north to Ravenshollow under a new hand instead of sitting idle in the harbor, staging a real coastal corridor for people and supplies between the two.

**Halloway's Reach formalized into the alliance.** Before leaving, Rurik sat down with Captain Voss. Voss named his real needs plainly — manpower, honest trade contacts, and the truth about what's coming — and Rurik answered with more than he asked for: a seat at the regional council, the same standing Kessick's Ford holds, and a formal military appointment — **Commander Voss of Halloway's Reach**, answerable to Rurik only for the town's defense, free to run everything else himself. Voss took it without hesitation, offering his hand plain and immediate rather than tested or withheld. On Rurik's suggestion, Voss named **Bram** — the warehouseman who confessed publicly before anyone forced him — as the town's judicant, to travel to Urskelde and represent Halloway's Reach at council in person. Bram accepted, visibly moved to be trusted with it.

**The ship sails for Ravenshollow.** Corlan handed command of his ship to his first mate **Tavik**, steady and long-serving, rather than sail her himself — the accounting he owes the council matters more delivered in person than by letter. Halvard sent a sealed letter of introduction to House Corvane's steward at Ravenshollow's river-mouth harbor; Voss added one of his own watch officers, **Selvane**, to ride along as the town's eyes on the arrangement. The ship departed under Tavik's hand, five or six days out, no name painted on her hull yet — the first real link in the coastal supply corridor Joss has been laying groundwork for since Ravensmoor.

**Mirelle's route home simplified.** Rather than arrange separate transport, Mirelle will travel with the column itself — the road north passes near enough to Ravensmoor that she can see her family in person before deciding on the Urskelde dye shop offer, instead of arriving on their doorstep alone with only a letter to explain herself.

**Halvard's orders before departure.** Three items flagged for the council: three more low-level site names surfacing from the freed courier's memory (Halvard wants two more weeks with him before calling the map complete); a ship-registry pattern in Osric's cross-referencing that may predate the Tallow House entirely; and the compass, which Wren and Ysolde have gotten to respond but not to commit — it flickers between the mountain keep and somewhere further south, unreliable enough that Halvard wants their full report in front of the council before anyone routes a plan around it. Rurik gave Halvard one week to finish the audit trail, bring the courier, and report to the full council in person at Urskelde.

**A quiet afternoon, and two honest conversations.** With Dain still days from travel-fit, Ysolde pulled Rurik aside to check on him directly — and away from Mara's hearing, at Rurik's own request, he admitted to feeling stretched thin: not performing mercy he didn't mean, but genuinely disliking some of the decisions he's made while still knowing they were right. Ysolde's read, after actually listening rather than just reassuring: that's not the count's machine wearing a kinder face, that's just what a real conscience costs — and named the toll plainly, thirteen hard mercies back to back with no room between them to put any of it down. She prescribed an actual afternoon off, no duties, no titles.

Rurik took it to Mara instead of alone, and told her the same thing at the bathhouse — stretched thin, an "anti-count" she'd already half-sensed without the words for it. She admitted her own fear in return: since Kathrivex answered Rurik's message down the thread, she's been carrying quiet dread that the count now knows him personally, and that whatever sits above the fist-and-chain sigil — the mountain keep chief among her worries — won't send a messenger first the way the Auditor did. Both agreed, plainly, to stop performing "fine" for each other going forward. The afternoon that followed was theirs alone.

**The road to Ravensmoor: an ambush, and a name.** Two quiet days out of Halloway's Reach, Mara made an ambush before it triggered — four unknown attackers paced with the column through treeline on both sides, planning to hit at dusk.
