The party turned it back on them instead: Wren opened with Haste on Rurik and a quickened Chain Lightning into the treeline, Rurik raged and cut one attacker down outright before it ever got a turn, Sael Wild Shaped into an Owlbear mid-fight, and between Mara's bow, Wren's Cone of Cold and a second Chain Bolt, and Ysolde's Bless carrying the whole party's accuracy, three of the four were dead before any of them landed a single hit back. The last — clearly a handler directing the other three — teleported away wounded and ran; Wren finished him with an upcast Chaos Bolt at genuine range, burning Tides of Chaos to make sure it landed. Not one hit connected against the party the entire fight; Corlan, Bram, Mirelle, and Dain were never in real danger.

Searching the bodies (Mara leading, the whole party helping) turned up plain, deliberately unmarked gear — except a maker's etching on the handler's rapier that Ysolde recognized immediately: **Greyfeather**, the same craft-signature tied to the scout creature and the corruption shards, dormant in the investigation since it merged with the scout-maker hunt. A half-burned coded order, decoded between Ysolde and Wren, named the actual mission: *"the Reach witness — take alive if clean, silence if not"* (Bram, the primary target) and *"the Halworth thread — confirm status, report only"* (Dain, surveillance rather than a kill order — someone wanted to know he was free before deciding what to do about it). Real, current proof Greyfeather is still active and now moving against people connected to Rurik specifically, within a day of Bram's council appointment being made. 140 gp recovered off the bodies. Halvard will want this note the moment he reaches Urskelde.

**Ravensmoor: Mirelle home, Dain finds his footing.** Four more quiet days brought the column into Ravensmoor. Mirelle's parents found her in the market square before the party even caught up — a real reunion, held back from deliberately so it stayed hers rather than the party's story to collect thanks for. That evening the whole column shared a table at an inn: old friends and new sitting together without a ledger being kept on any of it, capped by Rurik standing to toast "the people we help are brothers and sisters, sons and daughters" and draining his ale in one go. Dain lingered after the room emptied and asked, plainly, whether Rurik meant the toast or the ale was talking. It opened into the realest conversation the two of them have had since the handshake in the Cellarworks — Dain pushing back that "the needs of the many" was uncomfortably close to the count's own logic, Rurik clarifying that the many are everyone still caught in Kathrivex's ledger and the few are the party spending themselves to free them, and telling Dain plainly it isn't all about him anymore. Dain took it rather than bristling at it, and asked what came next for him practically. Rurik laid out three honest options — resettle and rebuild quietly, sink into self-pity, or fight back and make himself useful — and stood to declare "the past doesn't shape me, I shape me." Dain chose the third outright: put to work at Urskelde, teaching what surviving a pit actually costs, standing a line when it's needed, earning it rather than being carried. Sealed with a real handshake, no hesitation left in it either way. Word went to Mirelle before departure: no deadline on the dye shop offer, family comes first, she reaches the party when she's ready — her answer back, through Sael: *"tell him thank you for not making me choose today."*

**Home.** The road from Ravensmoor passed easy, Dain visibly stronger by the day. Urskelde's watch-cairns came into view finished — all six, sky-iron worked through the stonework — and the main gate rebuilt and reinforced to match, Threnn's people standing watch alongside Kaldur's regulars like it stopped being notable a while back. Kaldur met the column at the gate himself, delighted at the size of the "small delegation" Rurik came home with. A full day was spent settling in before the council convened: Corlan, Bram, and Dain housed under guarded trust same as Osric and the courier before them; Sesha and Harek told about Dain directly (hard for them at first, easing once they understood he was a debtor forced into the pit same as any other victim of the count, and asked to meet him properly when he's ready); Thyra warned in advance that the council might not love everything in the report, briefed only that it touched all three of Halloway's Reach, the ship, and the count itself, and seated Rurik beside her at the table so she could keep him from making it worse live.

**The full council.** Halvard returned within two days as re-estimated, courier in tow, sealed report ready. The council convened in full — Kaldur, Threnn, Idra, Torvin, Thyra, Halvard, Corim, Vosk, the whole party, Joss (specially sent for), and Corlan, Bram, and Dain standing to speak for themselves. Rurik opened plainly: Halloway's Reach was never the center of the operation, only the beginning of one that may span the continent. What followed, delivered in full:

- **Dain Halworth's own account**, from the pit to thirteen years bound in the Cellarworks' crystal to freedom and his new purpose — met with genuine, visible emotion from the room, Kaldur naming it a fourth account closed outright.
- **The freed courier's testimony**, unprompted and raw: no name he can recall, routes walked without reason or choice, and confirmation of a second mountain-country site distinct from Greywatch Keep, "wrong" all the way down, that he's walked twice.
- **Ysolde's briefing on the audit cycle** — the Auditor arrived nine days after the courier's thread was severed, and its own logbook placed Halloway's Reach third in that cycle's rotation, meaning two other sites faced the same correction with nobody there to help.
- **Wren's report on the recovered compass** — genuinely responsive, confirmed against the mountain keep, but unreliable, flickering between the keep and a second reading further south; never once pointed toward the burned court.
- **Corlan Vess's full confession**, standing and unprompted: roughly thirty people delivered over eleven years, the ship handed to him to captain honestly, no handshake yet, a daughter who still doesn't know.
- **Ysolde's presentation of the fist-and-chain token**, tying the Warden's bargain, Ravenshollow's brokerage, and the Auditor itself to one hierarchy sitting above all three — confirmed real for the first time, still unidentified.
- **The Auditor fight itself**, told in full — Wren's wild-magic-chained Fireball-into-Chain-Lightning, Sael's sustained Call Lightning, Mara's unseen marksmanship, Ysolde's Bless and a max-level Cure Wounds, Rurik's six-swing Action Surge round, and the thing dying mid-sentence: *"the count does not—"*
- **Mirelle's rescue and the open offer** of a place and a dye shop at Urskelde, pending her family and her own timeline.
- **Halloway's Reach's new standing**: Captain Voss made Commander, answering to Rurik and Kaldur directly on military matters only, full autonomy otherwise, a genuine council seat; Bram appointed judicant and speaking for the town himself, plainly and without polish, closing with an outright request that the council trust the town the way Rurik already had.
- **The Greyfeather ambush** on the road to Ravensmoor — Ysolde presenting the recovered rapier's craft-signature and the decoded orders naming Bram as a capture-or-silence target and Dain as surveillance-only, confirmed proof the organization is active and now reacting to this alliance in real time. Handed directly to Halvard.
- **Joss**, brought in specially, told the ship posted to Ravenshollow is the first real link in his coastal corridor — visibly thrilled.

**Decisions made.** After real debate — Kaldur pressing on whether Voss's chain of command should run through the two of them personally or through the council as an institution, settled as an interim personal arrangement pending Halloway's Reach building trust in the council itself, revisited later — the council set its priorities plainly: **the mountain keep first** (closest to home, sitting on Thundertree's own ground near the Standing Ring), **the poor coastal village second**, **the burned court third**. Thundertree and Threnn's people will lead the approach and reconnaissance on the keep, Rurik's party in support, the same careful method that's solved every sealed site so far. Kaldur raised the alliance's manpower being stretched across six live fronts at once; Rurik's answer was a real recruitment campaign drawn from every allied settlement, vetted carefully given Greyfeather's proven reach. Armored guard escorts and a doubled rotation were ordered for every shipment to and from Halloway's Reach, Corim and Joss coordinating directly. Rurik opened the floor fully before closing — Vosk voiced the coming question of why Thundertree never found the keep themselves and was told plainly to answer it honestly rather than comfortably; Ysolde raised whether any other deity beyond the Raven Queen has noticed Kathrivex, unconfirmed either way; and the session closed warm rather than heavy, on real gratitude rather than dread.

**Ends here for now:** Halloway's Reach fully seated in the regional alliance under Commander Voss, Bram traveling as its judicant, the ship posted to Ravenshollow and a coastal corridor genuinely underway, Mirelle's offer open and respected, Dain given real purpose at Urskelde, and — after the single largest council session this alliance has held — a clear, ordered priority list for what comes next: the mountain keep, then the coastal village, then the burned court, with recruitment and doubled rotations underway to meet all three.

## Session 44 — 2026-08-15

**A season's weight, set down properly.** Alone in the emptied council hall that evening, Rurik asked the bear-spirit outright whether it had known how big this would become. Its answer, spoken plainly through him: no — it knew the shape of an uncollected debt, not a ledger spanning ground it had never walked. It named the actual test as never having been the size of what he'd face, only what he did the one time it mattered most, in the pit, thirteen years ago. A long night's walk through the settlement followed — the cairns, the sleeping smithy district, the memorial cairns, a lit window at his parents' — ending at sunrise atop the gate, looking out toward ground his own family's clan has walked for generations without ever finding the keep sitting on it. Mara found him there. A real, unguarded conversation followed: Rurik naming his fear as not the scale of the threat but its unknown reach, admitting he'd trade all of it — the growth, the found family, everything — to end the count and keep everyone safe if it were truly on offer, and voicing his deeper fear of someday letting everyone down. Mara's answer, steady: he already has, more than once, and stayed anyway — that's the only promise that ever mattered to her, not perfection, showing up after. Both left it there, whole rather than resolved, exactly as intended.

**Rest, properly taken.** The days that followed were the real thing Ysolde prescribed back at Halloway's Reach, finally fully claimed — fishing, a stealth contest, and an archery duel with Mara, Rurik improbably taking two of three despite having no business winning any of them (Mara's read: "something is wrong with the universe today"). A quiet, easy afternoon closed with real tenderness between them, and Rurik naming plainly, without sadness, that he never learned how to play as a child — only to thank her for the years since that taught him anyway.

**Dain, formally a friend.** Rurik found him steadier by the day, asked whether he'd be willing to brief whichever Thundertree hunters walk the keep's approach — not secondhand, face to face, given what he alone knows about being held by something like it from the inside. Dain agreed without hesitation, wanting the chance to spare someone else his own blind entry into it. The two shook on it properly this time — not "frenemies," but **friend** — Dain naming it the first he's had in longer than he wants to think about.

**Vosk, the sensing plan, and old bones.** Rurik and Vosk compared methods directly: his count-sense reads Kathrivex's specific fingerprint, targeted rather than ambient; Thundertree's cairn-verse tradition reads the land's own accumulated wrongness, blunter but longer-lived. Agreed: both together, plus the whole party's own gifts (Mara's Primeval Awareness, Ysolde's Eyes of the Grave, Sael's bear-kin instinct), reading the same ground on the approach to the keep. Vosk had two hunters, **Sarna and Kell**, teach Rurik on the spot — the difference between ordinary quiet and a place gone wrong. A test on real ground found a genuinely dead-quiet hollow; Rurik's own sense confirmed it wasn't the count's doing, just old land-wrongness, exactly the distinction the lesson was for. Digging further turned up **scattered human-shaped bones and a broken, old-pattern goliath spear head** — older than any story either hunter recognized, one person, alone, forgotten. Sarna took the fragment to Thundertree's elders to search for a name and promised proper rites either way, named or not.

**A genuinely good day.** Alara ran a real trial of her rage-recovery tonic on Rurik — a full rage, held and let lapse naturally, then dosed — with measurably better results than her last batch, meaningful progress on real data. Joss reported the coastal corridor already moving fast: first shipment inside two weeks, Corim's armored rotation timed to the same run as its first live test, and — at Rurik's suggestion — a request in for Halloway's Reach's bathhouse designs, Perrin and Greta immediately keen to build sky-iron-lined piping for it. The day closed with Rurik roundly and affectionately mocked by Alara, Joss, Perrin, and Greta in turn for his hygiene, a running joke the whole settlement seemed to have agreed on independently.

**The mountain keep: a plan, and a horrifying theory.** Four days after the council, word came that Vosk and Halvard were both ready. Rurik brought Dain in to review the plan directly, as promised. Vosk presented her approach: six hunters (Sarna and Kell among them), staged travel — Standing Ring first, checking with Kethrun directly, then two careful days pushing northeast, reading the ground the whole way, stopping at safe visual range before any closer approach. Halvard presented three findings: a buried mention in Osric's archive of a "northern seat" predating the Keeper's own tenure, matching the courier's description; a decades-old, unregistered supply chain tracing the same direction; and the courier's own account, pressed further — the people or things held there described as "stored... like furniture," not moving, not dead, held rather than imprisoned in any ordinary sense.

Rurik connected it: constructs have to be built somewhere, and finished ones stored somewhere before deployment — which would explain the corrupted den northwest of the cairns (a delivery point, not a source) and Greywatch Keep's own garrison (a destination, not where they were built). The room took the full weight of it: this may not simply be a threat sitting on Thundertree's ground, but the actual manufacturing source behind every construct-based threat the alliance has fought since Greywatch Keep. Dain added a hard warning from his own experience: what's "stored" may still be dimly aware even inert, meaning arriving quiet may not mean arriving unnoticed.

Rurik proposed a role swap for the engagement itself — Thundertree leading reconnaissance and approach as agreed, but command passing to his own party, battle-tested against exactly this kind of threat, once real contact begins, Thundertree shifting to support rather than absorbing the first strike. Vosk agreed without pride getting in the way of it. **Departure set: two days from now, first light** — Dain to brief the hunters beforehand, the party using the time to finish resting.

**Ends here for now:** a full council's worth of personal and practical groundwork laid — Dain's friendship and purpose settled, Vosk's sensing plan agreed, real progress on Alara's tonic and Joss's coastal trade, a small piece of forgotten Thundertree history uncovered and honored — and a genuinely alarming new theory about the mountain keep's true purpose, with a concrete operational plan and a firm departure date now standing behind it.

**Last prep before departure.** Rurik commissioned a sky-iron shield from Perrin and Greta, kept secret from the whole party — a deliberate switch from dual-wielding to sword-and-shield ahead of a fight expected to mean protecting allies against constructs more than pure offense, restoring the long-unused Protection fighting style. He also raised the Auditor's psychic damage with Perrin, Greta, and Ysolde — the one gap in the bear-spirit's resistance — and was pointed toward Kethrun as the real authority on it.

**Kethrun's Ward.** At the Standing Ring, Rurik gave Kethrun the full account of Halloway's Reach and the council. Kethrun confirmed the psychic vulnerability was no accident: Kathrivex's true domain is worth and judgment, not flesh, and the bear-spirit's totem was never built to guard the self against being told it's insufficient. He offered a working old enough to close that gap — at a cost matching every prior gift of its kind: naming a real doubt aloud, in front of witnesses, and choosing to set it down rather than let it stand as final. In front of the full party and Vosk's hunters, Rurik named a doubt he'd never spoken — that he's felt unworthy to lead Urskelde — and chose, out loud, to set it down. The working completed: **permanent resistance to psychic damage** from anything weaponizing doubt or judgment against him. Kethrun also reached out through the restored sentinel network to ask Ilsevet, Vessyn, and Skarn whether they'd felt the same unexamined wrongness toward the northeast, and Rurik thanked all four guardians for their long, unthanked vigil — Kethrun visibly moved by it. The evening closed light again: Mara caught the secret shield immediately, games and easy affection between them, Rurik's own admission — surprising the whole chamber — that he'd shown Kethrun the shield first as a private joke with his wife before anyone else knew.

**The road northeast.** Two new DM tools adopted this leg: **Travel Risk Tiers** (three tiers of ground, escalating trigger odds for a random encounter) and **Camp Risk Tiers** (same tiers, modified by how well a camp's actually prepared). Day one past the Standing Ring (Tier 2, frontier ground) passed quiet. Day two, pushing into genuinely unmapped ground (Tier 3), the combined sensing plan proved itself for the first time: Sarna, Kell, and Rurik's own (now steadier) sense caught three Weave-grown guardian constructs patrolling a boundary marker stone before the constructs ever noticed the column. Rurik called for a ranged alpha strike — Vosk's hunters and Mara opening from range — followed by Sael Wild Shaping into an Owlbear to intercept rather than charge, and Rurik and Wren finishing the rest. All three constructs destroyed; the only damage taken was a retroactively-caught missed turn (Construct A got one swing at Rurik for 12 before dying) and one solid hit on Sael's Owlbear form, both healed clean by Keeper of Souls triggering twice. Examining the aftermath: the marker stone carried the fist-and-chain sigil worked directly into the stone (territory marking, function otherwise uncertain — possibly already triggered), and the construct remains matched the exact Weave-grown craft and maker's signature found at Halloway's Reach and the corrupted den — one hand behind all of it, confirming the foundry theory further. Rurik proposed and Vosk approved a V-formation with an invert signal for fast command handoff going forward.

**First sight of the keep.** The column reached visual range late the next day — dark, deliberately-built stone, walls too tall and too even to be anything but made. Camp held safe overnight (Camp Risk Tier 3, well-prepared, no incident). The next morning, Sael (in scouting form) and Sarna/Kell scouted closer from two angles. The hunters returned with a clean structural read: one closed main gate, two smaller unguarded side openings, no visible patrol pattern, no smoke, no light, no sound. Sael's read was worse — a low stealth roll took her close enough to see rows of unmoving shapes through a high window, and she felt something like being watched without eyes to do the watching right as she banked away, confirming Dain's warning that "stored" things might sense a quiet approach regardless. Judging the site already alerted, Rurik called it: past the recon stage, time to secure the perimeter and go in. Vosk agreed, her six hunters taking up full perimeter positions on the gate and both side entrances, command formally passing to Rurik's party for the engagement.

**Breaching the keep.** The party forced the heavy main gate open — a near-miss first attempt, then success on a second full-party push, Sael's Owlbear strength included. Holding at the threshold, the combined senses read the interior: dozens of count-tainted signatures, all inert, nothing alive detected (Ysolde's Eyes of the Grave, Sael's own hunting instinct, and Mara's Primeval Awareness all came back clean — the last confirming, usefully, that whatever's inside isn't any of the categories that spell tracks, meaning it's built rather than summoned or bred). Wren lit the entry hall with a torch-cantrip arrow, revealing an entry chamber and dozens of ranked, humanoid-shaped Weave-grown constructs in storage, weapons already fitted. A group examination (Ysolde leading, Sael and Wren helping) found the ranks likely share one true activation source rather than each being independently animated — meaning the real danger is finding and controlling that source, not fighting fifty constructs one at a time.

**The hub, and "R."** The party pushed up a stairway to a second, more finished level and found a warded door radiating active energy — the first sign of anything "on" since the gate. Opening it (a missed trap check triggered a network-wide alarm ward the party couldn't avoid) revealed a chamber dominated by an eight-foot hub crystal, veins running through the floor toward the rest of the keep and beyond. Rurik deliberately avoided reaching through it with his own count-sense, wary of the flare such contact might send back at this scale. A careful group examination (Ysolde, Sael, Wren) confirmed it as a genuine network convergence point — a dozen-plus distinct threads, some clearly construct-linked, some reading as live and possibly human, two seemingly running toward the other priority sites (the coastal village, the burned court). Etched into the crystal's base: a maker's mark distinct from the fist-and-chain sigil — a stylized eye ringed by a broken chain. Given the risk to any live threads connected, **Rurik ruled out severing anything blind** and chose instead to occupy and hold the keep rather than destroy it outright.

Vosk's hunters barricaded the main gate and both side entrances from the inside, securing the ground floor. A floor search turned up real evidence of who's actually behind the network's infrastructure: a records room too dense to parse quickly (flagged for Halvard), a study with a half-finished letter referencing an "R." who treats even Kathrivex as a resource rather than a master ("*the eye sees what the count cannot... R. is patient, but not infinitely so*"), and personal quarters holding a child's toy, a hairpin, and a degraded letter ending "*come home when the count is settled, my love*" — the first real proof that whoever runs this isn't hollowed out, has people they love, and is very much a person rather than an abstraction.

Ysolde Sent word to Halvard directly: the keep is occupied, a hub found, "R." traced, reinforcement and his archive needed urgently. His reply came back fast — leaving within the hour. Vosk dispatched a hunter running light and fast toward Kaldur with the same news, expected to arrive within two days.

**Ends here for now:** the mountain keep breached, secured rather than destroyed, and confirmed as a genuine network hub — the clearest proof yet that something above Kathrivex's own count is running this operation, with a name at last: **"R."** Halvard incoming with his archive, Kaldur being alerted by runner, and the party holding occupied ground deep in territory nobody in this alliance had ever mapped before today.

**Down, and Elenna.** Rather than wait on reinforcement, the party took the unexplored third path from the entry hall — down, into the mountain rather than up or further in. A row of long-disused cells gave way to one occupied: a living woman in magically-induced stasis, malnourished but stable. Ysolde brought her back gently. She gave her name as **Elenna** — wife of **Aldren Voss**, a once-honest trader who took on a bad debt years ago and was slowly drawn deeper into Kathrivex's network as payment on a debt that never actually closed, eventually stationed here. A comb pendant at her throat matched the hairpin found in the study upstairs, confirming it was her own room the party had searched. Rurik told her the truth of everything — the pit, the count, every account closed since, direct contact with Kathrivex itself — and she chose to believe it, offering everything she knows in return once Halvard arrives.

She confirmed real, useful detail on "R.": met twice, calm and patient, treats Kathrivex as a tool rather than a master, and spoke once of "scale" bigger than even this keep or the count itself — something still being built toward. She also placed her own hometown as a poor fishing town south along the coast from Halloway's Reach, an uncomfortably close match to the alliance's second priority site.

Continuing past her cell, the party found a message scratched into an empty cell's wall, recent and deliberate: **"A. VOSS — MOVED NORTH, ALIVE AS OF THIS WRITING. THEY DON'T KILL THE USEFUL ONES. ASK FOR THE BURNED COURT."** A locked storage room yielded a cruder transfer ledger tracking people and constructs moved between sites — confirming the burned court and a coastal site matching Elenna's description, plus a wholly new fourth location never before named: **"the Reckoning House."** A forced-open chest turned up an unidentified enchanted silver ring (protective in nature, per Ysolde's Arcana read — no one in the party can cast Identify, so it's logged for a real enchanter later) alongside minor personal effects taken from past captives. Deepest in, a round chamber held a second **black scrying mirror**, the same design as the one recovered years ago from the Warden's man — likely this keep's own line to R. or beyond. A short contact log beside it shows recent use. Untouched and untested as the exploration paused — the party debating whether to risk it or wait for Halvard.

**The workshop, and three more freed.** The last unexplored path from the entry hall led "deeper in" to a full construct-manufacturing workshop — a dozen-plus coerced human workers laboring under two armed overseers, exactly the kind of forced labor Elenna's account had already implied. Rurik walked in alone, party stealthed as backup, and declared himself the keep's new authority outright. An intimidation attempt to force a bloodless surrender failed, and the fight that followed was short and one-sided: Mara's opening volley (Hunter's Mark plus Dread Ambusher) dropped the first overseer in a single turn; the second, watching that happen, surrendered rather than fight on. He gave his name as **Corbett** — a former caravan guard trapped by the same debt-and-rescue trick that's caught nearly everyone else this campaign has freed, two years into service, recently promoted to overseer against his own comfort with it. Two more guards, **Fenwick** and **Tamsin**, answered a shift-bell summons and surrendered without a fight once they saw the numbers arrayed against them, each with their own debt story — poaching fines sold on, an inherited family debt Tamsin volunteered into believing it would clear her whole family's name.

All three confirmed the workshop answers to a **Keeper Ilsa**, absent three days on an errand toward the coast, which explains the keep's thin security. Rurik gave the freed workers and all three former guards the same choice he's offered before: go home once Halvard identifies where that is, or stay and work to actively close accounts instead of opening them. Fenwick chose home; Tamsin asked to decide once she knows if she still has a home to go to; Corbett, carrying real guilt over his two years of complicity, chose to stay and work it off doing something that matters.

**Three accounts closed, and a hub that noticed.** Concentrating on their threads directly, Rurik confirmed all three guards were still actively counted, uncut. He severed all three at once — clean, same as every prior closing — but this time, standing inside the hub's own reach rather than out at its edges, the crystal flared hard and visibly rather than rippling quietly. Ysolde confirmed something felt it directly, close, for the first time. Given the risk, the party held off severing the dozen-plus freed workers' own threads in the same room, choosing physical freedom and Halvard's help home over further disturbing the hub blind.

**Full sweep.** A careful reassessment of the hub (advantage, group effort) confirmed it stable afterward, the three severed threads visibly dark and clean, no active response detected. A second pass at the workshop (properly done with Help this time, after an initial rushed look turned up nothing) revealed the hub crystal is structurally *necessary* to finish any unfinished construct — meaning severing it would leave every incomplete frame in this keep permanently inert — plus a real materials/supply list worth handing to Halvard or Perrin and Greta. A hidden floor compartment yielded Keeper Ilsa's private journal, more detailed than the transfer ledger, giving rough directions toward **the Reckoning House** rather than just the name. Wren tested the compass by speaking that name aloud directly to it — it reacted, confirming the name means something real, but couldn't yet settle on a clean bearing.

A last storage alcove held the session's most unsettling find: an unfinished, more refined construct — notes describing "assessment capacity" and "count-integrity verification," reading like early design work for something in the Auditor's own family, more advanced. A second unidentified initial signed off on its development: **"K."**, distinct from R. Both side entrances checked clean — the west a plain delivery route, the east a maintenance corridor whose fresh wagon and boot tracks confirmed Keeper Ilsa's own three-day-old departure, heading the same direction as Aldren — toward the burned court.

**The keep, fully explored.** Every room, passage, and lead now accounted for or flagged for Halvard's incoming archive work: the hub, the records room, the study, the quarters, the entry hall, the workshop, the cell block, the storage room, the scrying chamber, both side entrances, and the storage-level alcoves.

**Taking stock.** Corbett gave a rough accounting of the keep's provisions — three to four weeks of food for the current reduced headcount, a reliable internal spring for water, thin medical supplies, a modest coin store, and the workshop's own crystal/material stock as the real point of value. Rurik tasked him with a full written inventory ahead of Halvard's arrival, Fenwick assisting — the first real, freely-chosen work either of them has done since surrendering.

**Halvard arrives.** Three days later, Halvard rode in alone, archive satchel heavy, exactly as promised. Rurik walked him through the entire keep in the order it was discovered, nothing disturbed beforehand. Standout reactions: the construct ranks confirmed the same craft signature as the corrupted den and Greyfeather's makers, tying the investigations together further; the hub crystal he examined only from a respectful distance, trusting the party's own read; the records room took him nearly two hours and turned up a shipping pattern matching Osric's ship-registry anomaly, proof the keep's reach extends to fronts the alliance hasn't identified yet; the "R." letter he called the single most valuable piece of paper recovered this whole campaign, and had copied multiple times before touching the original further; the prototype alcove and "K." signature left him visibly grave, with no immediate match in his own records but real leads through his Kessick's Ford contacts to chase once home.

He then took full, careful accounts from **Elenna** (more detail on Aldren and R., a direct promise to search for him properly, "not a courtesy") and **Corbett** (operational knowledge of Ilsa's habits and the keep's rhythms, including the detail that she left on fast horses in a tense final week — likely pressure from above rather than routine). **Fenwick and Tamsin** added smaller pieces, Tamsin's most valuable: an overheard fragment that "the Reckoning House keeps its own count, separate from the rest" — possible evidence of a partially independent operation. Finally, Halvard spent hours with the dozen-plus freed workshop workers, logging every name and hometown and promising staggered, escorted travel home, word sent ahead where he has contacts (the Kessick's Ford man wept openly hearing his family would know he was coming before he arrived).

**Halvard's assessment.** Asked plainly what he thought, he named it clearly: the alliance walked into this campaign believing it fought a god's own network, and today confirmed it's fighting *people* who use that network as a leveraged resource — potentially more dangerous for having patterns and limits that don't follow any god's. Two names, R. and K., at minimum two hands on the wheel. He recommended treating the burned court as urgent rather than merely "third priority," since it's now where both Aldren and Ilsa are believed to have gone — likely toward R. or someone close to R. directly.

**The decision to move fast.** Rather than wait for a full council briefing, Rurik chose to push straight for the burned court and delegated the complete reporting duty to Halvard — everything from the Session 43 council through everything found today, compiled properly rather than piecemeal. Two concerns were raised and addressed: the hub crystal (Halvard's recommendation: a real rotating garrison once word reaches Kaldur, same shape as the sealed-site tending arrangements, not indefinite reliance on the expedition's own hunters) and the alliance's manpower being stretched thinner than ever across seven-plus live fronts now (acknowledged plainly, no easy fix beyond the already-authorized recruitment campaign and careful pacing).

**Corbett placed in charge of the keep.** Rurik's own solution to the garrison question: leave Corbett — who knows the keep better than anyone available — in day-to-day charge as a chance to prove himself, with Vosk's hunters patrolling the perimeter and reporting independently back to the council, the same guarded-trust structure already proven with Voss at Halloway's Reach. Corbett accepted it seriously, asking honestly why he'd been chosen before committing fully. Vosk set the patrol rotation without hesitation.

**Ends here for now:** the mountain keep fully secured, explored, and handed to a real (if guarded) local authority; Halvard finishing his archive work and departing soon after with the complete report for the full council; and the party, resting one final night at the keep, set to push at first light straight toward the burned court — no return to Urskelde first, the freshest lead on Aldren, Ilsa, and possibly R. themself not given time to go cold.

**On the road again.** The column set out southeast at first light, genuinely unmapped ground from here. An hour in, the first Tier 3 travel roll triggered: Sarna found a stretch of ground scorched in small, deliberate, non-natural patches — possibly tied to the destination's name — and Kell found a second, newer set of tracks (six or seven strong) crossing Keeper Ilsa's own trail roughly a day behind her. Rurik's count-sense read the newer tracks as count-tainted.

**The burned patches.** A close examination (Ysolde and Wren, advantage) revealed the scorch marks weren't damage at all — a deliberate trail of ritual "closing" burns, each one containing fragments of destroyed ledger pages. Surviving text confirmed the practice: debts "satisfied by fire," accounts closed by erasing the paper trail rather than freeing the person the debt was ever attached to — the dark mirror of Rurik's own pattern. The burn trail marked a clear route continuing toward the burned court.

**Tracking the seven.** Sarna and Kell worked out the newer trail properly: seven individuals, trained gait, one notably lighter-framed, moving parallel to Ilsa's route rather than following it directly, roughly a day to a day and a half back. Judging it worth the cost, Rurik pushed the pace hard for two days running (a self-imposed +2 penalty to the Tier 3 travel trigger for reduced caution, per the new risk-tier tools) — the first day clean, closing the gap to half a day; the second day rolled a natural 1 on the travel check, and the pushed pace itself caused the encounter: cresting a ridge, the party ran headlong into the group with no warning on either side. Sael scouted ahead as a hawk the evening before and spotted distant smoke, likely their camp, confirming the direction.

**The standoff.** Mutual surprise, weapons undrawn at first. The group's leader — hooded, calm, clearly in command — offered to talk rather than fight, correctly guessing Rurik's reputation ("the anti-count") had reached this far. A tense, genuinely searching conversation followed: the leader admitted working for whoever pays them **by choice**, not debt, and pushed back hard when Rurik challenged that as an easy option — revealing they'd watched someone they loved die taking the "principled" road once, and chose this path specifically to avoid that happening again. Rurik answered with his own raw honesty about the daily weight of real choices, which visibly landed and genuinely shifted the tone. The leader conceded ground, refused to name their employer, but confirmed the group was on a separate delivery errand toward the burned court, not hunting the party. Rurik drew his weapon anyway, judging the vague answer wasn't enough — the leader called it fairly ("you just made the choice for me") before combat began.

**The fight.** Sael opened with Spike Growth centered on the whole group; the leader Misty Stepped clear immediately, abandoning their own soldiers to the terrain. Mara opened with Hunter's Mark and (this being her true first turn of the new engagement) Dread Ambusher's third attack, hitting the leader hard. Rurik raged and closed the distance. The soldiers, still boxed in the spikes, chose to push through rather than hold — collectively took over 100 damage from the terrain alone before ever landing a blow, wounding two hits on Sael, Ysolde, and Wren in return. Wren's Chain Lightning, aimed squarely at the already-wounded soldiers rather than the leader, killed three outright and left a fourth on 2 HP. Ysolde's Bless carried the party's accuracy through the rest of it. The leader, watching their people die in an instant, tried to flee — Rurik's opportunity attack caught them mid-turn. Mara finished the 2-HP soldier and wounded another. Sael's Moonbeam (replacing Spike Growth, since both are concentration) burned two more soldiers badly on their following turn. Rurik closed it out exactly as ordered — two more hits, the second reducing the leader to 0 HP, **knocked unconscious rather than killed**, per his explicit intent. The three surviving soldiers surrendered without further prompting, watching their leader go down and half their number already dead.

**Result:** the Hooded Leader captured alive, unconscious and stable. Three soldiers restrained and compliant. Four soldiers dead. Not one party member seriously hurt the entire fight.

**The interrogation.** Ysolde revived the Leader with a touch of Cure Wounds. Rurik turned the three surviving soldiers to face them directly, naming plainly that their leader had abandoned them in the spikes and nearly fled outright — the soldiers, hearing it said aloud, openly agreed rather than defended her. Offered plain freedom (one sword, one free hand, no strings) rather than a debt or a job, two soldiers stayed uncertain while a third — asked directly why he'd extend mercy to the woman who'd have let him die — gave a genuinely thoughtful answer: he didn't want revenge, handed to him as a "gift," to be the first free choice he ever made on his own terms. Rurik freed him on the spot. The other two stayed restrained for the return to the keep. Offered a harsher choice — fight her own remaining soldiers for freedom, one sword, or take a different path — the Leader broke fully, and Rurik pulled the offer back once the soldiers themselves made clear they didn't want to be handed vengeance either.

Pressed for real answers under direct threat, the Leader gave everything plainly: the crates carry untraceable regional coin and a locked case she was never permitted to open; the actual delivery point isn't the burned court itself but a disguised waystation two days further along the route, where the court's own people meet outsiders rather than allow them near the real site; and her employer pays the burned court **tribute** on a standing arrangement — implying two separate powers keeping peace with each other, not one hierarchy. A thorough search of the four dead soldiers turned up a hidden token sewn into a coat lining: a fist gripping unbalanced scales, the first physical symbol ever tied to **"K."**, distinct from R.'s eye-and-broken-chain mark — strong evidence K. runs an independent operation paying its own tribute to the burned court.

**The retreat.** Weighing the new intelligence — a disguised meeting point, an unopened case of unknown danger, and confirmation the burned court commands enough respect to be paid tribute rather than simply obeyed — Rurik called off the direct push and turned the column back toward the mountain keep instead, judging it wiser to consolidate everything found with Halvard before committing further. The party departs with the Hooded Leader and two soldiers restrained, the newly-freed soldier traveling along undecided, the recovered crates, and the K. token.

**A complication on the road home.** A day into the Tier 2 return leg, the locked case began emitting a faint, regular ticking sound it hadn't made before — unexplained, growing marginally louder. Even the Leader, genuinely alarmed, confirmed it's new behavior. A careful joint examination (Ysolde and Wren) identified it as a schedule-based failsafe ward tied to the delivery route and timing, now counting down since the column diverted from its expected path — likely rigged to alert the intended recipients and/or destroy the contents once triggered, estimated hours to a day out. A tense, hands-on disarming (two close calls where the countdown briefly sped up before being caught) unwound the ward fully rather than triggering it. Rurik chose to hold the case unopened until back at the keep rather than risk it further on the road.

**Home to the keep.** The rest of the journey passed clean. Corbett managed the return arrival smoothly — prisoners separated (Leader alone, the two soldiers together, to limit coordinated stories), cargo unloaded and the case set apart untouched, exactly the kind of competent handling that's continuing to earn him the trust he was given.

**The case, opened — and a devastating realization.** With Halvard present, Rurik opened the case himself. Inside: roughly a dozen small crystal shards, each one humming with the same wrongness as an individual person's severed thread rather than construct-craft — Ysolde's read, unhesitating: these are fragments of actual human accounts, crystallized and preserved. Not cargo. **Tribute paid in literal pieces of people**, currency between R.'s organization and the burned court.

Rurik made the connection that reframed the entire campaign's central mystery: devils deal in souls, not debts — so if Malphestor, "the Reckoner," was ever genuinely bound to serve the Warden's bargain, it was never truly working for Kathrivex's ledger at all. It was doing exactly what devils always do, using soul-currency the debt framing had been quietly built on top of and around from the start. Rurik pushed the implication one step further: this is very likely why no other power — the Nine Hells specifically — has ever moved against Kathrivex's count, despite its scale. They're not blind to it. They're profiting from it, paid in real souls regardless of whose ledger the transaction runs through. Halvard and Ysolde both confirmed the logic independently, Ysolde noting it directly answers the "have other deities noticed" question raised and left open at the Session 43 council: **yes, and at least one of them is being paid to look away.**

Halvard named Malphestor an urgent thread again rather than an old loose end — the one witness who might know firsthand exactly how deep the Nine Hells' involvement actually runs, and whether it's a wall this alliance can never break, or simply an arrangement that could be disrupted the same way everything else has been.

**One more implication.** Rurik pushed the soul-currency theory further still: if the shards are genuine, and the hub crystal already converts raw material into finished constructs, there's no clear reason the same hub couldn't be crystallizing soul-fragments from the live threads running through it too — meaning the keep might still be an active soul-shard foundry sitting a few floors above where the party stood, not just a construct one. Unconfirmed, but grim enough to reopen the still-unresolved question of what to actually do with the hub.

**The mirror, used.** Rather than sit on the uncertainty, Rurik chose to activate the untested scrying mirror directly — deliberately, with a clear goal: learn how important this site actually is to whoever answers, since that answer would decide whether risking live threads to sever the hub is the right call at all. Wren and Ysolde worked out the activation cleanly. The contact answered expecting a routine check-in from Keeper Ilsa and got Rurik instead.

What followed was a real negotiation. Rurik declared his full identity and reputation outright — Darkhawk, Cage 7, the anti-count, breaker of the anchor — and the contact, a mid-level coordinator who gave his name as **Corren**, visibly recognized every piece of it, admitting the rumors reaching the network were apparently true. Pressed plainly, Corren confirmed the keep genuinely matters to people above him and tried to trade information (real coordinates on the coastal town, more on "the Reckoning House," possibly Aldren and Ilsa's whereabouts) for time. Rurik rejected the trade, called out the mismatch in negotiating skill, demanded contact with someone senior enough to actually speak for R. or K., and closed with a pointed warning: Wren's Fireball is still unused. Corren, rattled but professional, agreed to escalate the request rather than promise anything himself — no timeline given, no guarantee R. or K. would respond at all, but a real channel now genuinely open for the first time.

**Ends here for now:** the mountain keep's true stakes laid bare — a possible active soul-shard foundry as well as a construct one — and, for the first time in this entire campaign, a direct, acknowledged line of contact opened with the network above Kathrivex itself, awaiting whoever Corren manages to reach.

**A plan for what comes next.** Rurik laid out five clear steps: Halvard finishes fully questioning the prisoners, then finalizes his report; Vosk's hunters, Halvard, and the escort prisoners head back to Urskelde; Halvard briefs the full council and opens a real search for Malphestor; and the party itself holds the mountain keep directly, securing every entrance, while awaiting Corren's response — accepting plainly that the hub crystal may not be salvageable if the negotiation fails. Offered the choice to leave with everyone else or stay and hold the keep under real, disclosed risk (including possibly standing next to the hub if it has to be destroyed), **Corbett chose to stay**, wanting the weight of the choice made clearly rather than assumed.

**The full interrogation, and the true scale of it.** With the whole party and Elenna present, Halvard questioned the Hooded Leader and her two remaining soldiers thoroughly. New detail: the waystation is a converted watchtower manned by three or four on rotation; tribute deliveries (ordinary goods) run roughly six or seven times a year on the Leader's route alone; soul-shard deliveries specifically, maybe once a year, at minimum twelve shards per delivery, confirmed running for **decades** — the practice predates the Leader's own five years on the route by a wide margin. Pressed directly by Rurik, the Leader confirmed she suspected but never confirmed this keep specifically was a *source* for tribute, not just a processing point — a suspicion the party's own findings (the cell block, the freed workers, the hub's unaccounted-for live threads) already supported.

Rurik connected it directly to Mirelle — found warded gently rather than harshly in a person-sized crate aboard Corlan's ship, four days off a road — recognizing in real time that her rescue likely pulled her out of exactly this pipeline before it reached its end. The room absorbed that hard, especially Sael. Doing the math openly — twelve shards a delivery, once a year, over decades, on one route out of an unknown number — the working estimate came out at **well over a thousand people**, quite possibly more once other routes and relationships are accounted for. All three prisoners reckoned with the number in front of the whole room, no excuses offered; the Leader named it plainly as the point where her own justification for choosing this path over "the difficult road" stopped holding up.

**Freed and recruited.** Given their honesty, Rurik freed all three outright — swords returned, no conditions — then offered a stark choice: redemption, working directly for him, or nothing at all. All three accepted without hesitation, giving their real names for the first time: **Rennae** (the former Hooded Leader), **Dorst**, and **Petra**. Reunited with the fourth soldier freed earlier, all four are now committed soldiers under Rurik's command. Corbett accepted them as his garrison for holding the mountain keep, oriented them into watch rotations and defense points immediately.

**Deploying the new soldiers.** Rurik checked in personally with each — thanking Vosk for the planning and hunters that made the whole expedition possible before she departed with Halvard; asking Rennae directly what magic she carried beyond Misty Step so the four could be deployed properly; having Corbett sign off on the plan himself. The fourth soldier, still undecided, finally gave his name — **Kael**, a cooper before soldiering — after Rurik asked plainly what he'd do with a second chance at that life. Rurik offered him a real one: help hold the keep now, and a place at Urskelde as a cooper once it's over, no strings attached. The same offer was extended to Rennae, Dorst, and Petra for whatever they want once the keep's business concludes. All four accepted the terms and settled into the garrison rotation.

**A night's rest, and two answers.** With the guard rotation folded in properly — party included — a full night passed at Camp Risk Tier 2, well-prepared: **roll 17, no incident.** In the morning, Rurik sent Mara and Sael to scout the perimeter (confirmed secure, one gap fixed — the northern game trail now has a proper watch post) while Ysolde and Wren studied the hub crystal directly for two answers: what destroying it would cost anyone still connected, and whether all its threads could be safely disconnected first. Both checks landed cleanly (21 and 24): of a dozen-plus total threads, **five are confirmed still live** — the rest construct-linked or running toward the other network sites — and all five can be severed individually and safely with Rurik's established method before the hub itself is destroyed, once the time comes. With that settled, the party chose to hold and wait on Corren rather than act on the hub yet.

**Supply and the freed workers.** Corbett gave an honest accounting: stores built for a skeleton crew now feeding closer to forty people, roughly two to three weeks before it gets tight. Mara and Sael spent a day hunting and foraging together — Sael scouting as a hawk, Mara reading the ground — and came back with a real haul (Natural Explorer doubling the yield), buying real time. Rurik set a standing rhythm: forage every couple of days, stretching to four if the ground starts thinning, Mara and Sael's own call.

Rurik then went to speak with the keep's dozen-plus freed workers directly, led informally by an older forge-hand, **Tolan**. Asked what they wanted once the party heads back to Urskelde, the group split honestly: several with nothing left to go home to asked for passage and a place to start over; several others have someone still out there worth searching for; a few aren't ready to decide at all. Rurik proposed exactly that — everyone travels to Urskelde together, those who stay give back to the settlement, those who don't get help finding their people and word sent ahead that they're safe. All agreed. Rurik and Ysolde then severed all twelve workers' debt-threads to the count in one pass, one at a time — clean, no backlash this time, distance from the hub apparently the deciding factor after the hard reaction severing Corbett's group caused. Tolan felt his own go last, visibly lighter for it.

Tasked with identifying anything worth salvaging from the workshop, Tolan and the others spent a day and a half sorting under guard escort, using the shipment's surviving pack animals to haul. Three things were set aside for Rurik's own eyes: a bolted steel cabinet none of the workers would touch, a drafting table of advanced Auditor-prototype schematics, and a sealed courier pouch stamped with **K.'s mark**, found hidden rather than filed.

**The cabinet.** Forced open bare-handed (Athletics 28) rather than picked. Inside: a strongbox, a folio of personal correspondence, and a second silver ring — Ysolde confirmed the same maker as the first, but a different mark, worn by someone senior to an overseer. The strongbox resisted Mara's improvised pick (no tools, DEX check failed) and was forced open in turn (Athletics 28): 40 gold, a vial of unknown liquid (set aside for Alara to identify back at Urskelde), and an unsent letter, unsigned but for "— I." Read aloud, it confirmed the writer as **Keeper Ilsa** — genuine fear of "R." specifically, contrasted against "K.," who at least still performs like the work matters; and one hard new fact: **the Reckoning House is being built, not just planned**, something that would make "the old, small way of doing things" — the keep, the count, all of it — obsolete once finished.

The correspondence folio held two items of note: an unsigned note warning Ilsa to stop reporting delays and let R. and K. argue their disagreement over the Reckoning House's timeline without her caught between them — evidence of a third party quietly protecting her — and a rough, unlabeled terrain sketch (a river bend, a ridge line, one marked point) that may be the Reckoning House's actual location.

**The drafting table.** Mara's read (Investigation 22) found the schematics were a deliberate redesign, correcting the flaw that let Rurik's severing near the original Auditor prototype cause a hard reaction — this version needs no live hub connection, running instead on a purpose-built housing sized for a soul-shard, carried rather than tethered. A repeated annotation marks a consistent weak point on every page. Rurik connected the scale of it aloud: if even a fraction of the estimated thousand-plus tribute victims fed a finished version of this design, the network could field an army of self-powered constructs. The party talked the fear back down together — this is still an unfinished prototype, by someone visibly still fixing mistakes, and at least one earlier iteration (the Auditor destroyed aboard Corlan's ship) has already been intercepted. Not urgency without time left — urgency with a real head start still intact.

**K.'s letter.** The sealed courier pouch, checked for wards and found (inconclusively) clean, held a shorthand letter and a brass safe-passage token, both from K. directly to Ilsa. Ysolde's deciphering was clean: K. warned Ilsa that R. suspected a leak, enclosed the token as limited cover, and — unprompted — admitted to "noticing who it costs" even while unable to stop. Rurik and the party read it as real, documented evidence of a working fracture between R. and K., not just rumor — and a possible way to reach K. directly later. The letter and token were kept separate from the case bound for Halvard, held close for Rurik's own timing rather than handed off; the plan agreed on was to drop only oblique hints of the fracture when Corren's response finally comes, using the uncertainty as leverage without exposing Ilsa or the source.

**The rest of the shipment.** The crates set aside since the road battle were opened last, checked clean by Mara first (27). Cover cargo in one; eight more crystallized soul-shard fragments in a second — a separate batch from the case's dozen, meant for a different drop; 340 gold in operational coin (added to Rurik's total); a dozen sets of unissued arms and armor for the garrison; and a crate of personal effects — a child's toy, a wedding band, letters, a locket — taken off people before they became a shard, set apart from salvage for a proper memorial at Urskelde, Tolan volunteering to help see to it. A final long case held a single black-iron spear of clearly goliath craftsmanship, matching the style of the broken spear head Sarna and Kell recovered near Urskelde — added to the party's gear, thread unresolved.

**Ends here for now:** the keep's holding pattern intact, its garrison fed, housed, and settled, its freed workers given a real path forward, and the party sitting on real leverage — a documented crack between R. and K. — still waiting on word from Corren.

## Session 45 — 2026-08-20

**Vessa answers.** Six days into the keep's quiet holding pattern — clean nights, a fresh haul from Mara and Sael's foraging, Rennae and Corbett drilling the new garrison smooth — the scrying mirror lit on its own. Not Corren: **Vessa**, speaking directly for R., composed and testing from the first word. Rurik opened by naming the fracture obliquely ("upheaval and disagreements") without revealing the source; it visibly rattled her, and she neither confirmed nor denied it, only warned him not to assume he knew where it ran deepest. Pressed for the actual demand — the count and the soul-shards stop, now — she was honest rather than evasive: not a switch she could throw, because the tribute owed to whatever holds the debt in the Nine Hells doesn't vanish with the ledger, and everyone still counted when it goes silent pays first. She asked what Rurik would offer to make ending it survivable for R.

**The demonstration.** Rurik declined to negotiate further and asked to show her directly what he could do instead. The party carried the mirror bodily up to the hub chamber — Vessa watching the keep's own layout scroll past the whole way up, a cost Mara flagged aloud — and, with her permission and full warning that R. would feel it instantly, Rurik severed one of the five confirmed live threads clean in front of her. It registered on her in real time, visible and involuntary. Rurik then stated plainly this was never a negotiation, only a demonstration: he doesn't need R., K., or Vessa to stop the count — he intends to disconnect every live thread at the hub and destroy it regardless, and neither the soul-shard-powered Auditor redesign nor R.'s wider plans change that.

Vessa, dropping her composure further than at any point in the exchange, confirmed she believed him, warned honestly that the burned court's debt comes due regardless of who dismantles the mechanism servicing it — and that whoever collects will go looking for the easiest target, likely the keep's own garrison, not R. — and asked the only question she thought worth asking: when that debt comes due, does Rurik want it landing on R., who built and profited from it, or on himself, for tearing it down without a plan for what's owed. Rurik answered that his actual target was Kathrivex and whatever devil in the Nine Hells holds the debt directly, and that going through R. and K. to get there was simply something he'd do. He closed by naming his own standing order plainly — mercy, justice, redemption, in that order — and told her to have R. and K. check the count itself for proof. Vessa, visibly moved, agreed to report it as a demonstration and warning rather than a threat requiring immediate response, confirmed a devil once brokered this exact kind of tribute before Kathrivex's network existed, and closed with one unprompted, genuine warning: **"Watch the sky toward the burned court."**

**The hub, ended.** With the negotiation closed, the party severed the remaining four live threads (one resisted — someone who'd forgotten they were more than their debt, per Ysolde — before releasing gently rather than being forced) for five total severed clean. Ysolde warded the chamber; Rurik struck the stripped hub with the same intent he uses severing a thread rather than raw force, shattering it in one blow. The discharge slammed into Ysolde's ward rather than the party — held, barely — with only a brief psychic backlash that Kethrun's Ward blunted before it could land. The mountain keep's true heart is permanently destroyed.

**Abandoning the keep.** Given Vessa's warning and the scale of what's now confirmed, Rurik called it: sever everything, destroy the hub, abandon the site entirely, and take everyone — garrison, workers, all of it — back to Urskelde together. Wren's Fireball, cast into the packed dormant-construct storage room, wrecked the entire rank in one blast — nothing in it moved again. Rennae and Corbett organized the whole column's marching order (workers and Crate 5 centered, Dorst and Petra on the flanks, Corbett's people and Kael holding the rear, the party at point) inside an hour. A final clean night's rest let every resource refresh fully before departure; Ysolde added **Hold Person** and Sael added **Thunderwave** to their prepared lists in the process.

**The road home.** A Tier 3 travel check rolled a natural 1 leaving the keep: a disciplined, sulfur-tinged formation of winged shapes crossed the ridge at altitude, watched but didn't engage, and peeled away east — the first hard confirmation Vessa's warning wasn't metaphor. The column pushed on at full alert; two more Tier 3 legs came up clean. Once onto familiar Tier 2 ground, pushing the pace on Rurik's order, a switchback trail crumbled under the wagon carrying Crate 5 — Rurik caught the rig bare-handed (Athletics 21) before it went over the edge, losing nothing. The rest of the push home was clean.

**Urskelde, and a new council.** The column's return was the largest homecoming yet — a dozen-plus freed workers, four new soldiers, wagons of salvage and evidence, and Crate 5 carried in with real care. Rurik told Kaldur plainly he'd delivered the reinforcements promised. Crate 5's contents stayed private for now, respected without being pressed. Given the scale of what's confirmed — Vessa, the debt, Kathrivex's true theft — Rurik called for a **full regional council** rather than a local one, judging the next step could be an outright declaration of war. Kaldur committed riders to every seat (Kessick's Ford, Ravenshollow, Halloway's Reach) and estimated twelve to fourteen days before everyone could actually be in the room.

**Fourteen days, spent chasing answers.** Rather than wait idle, Rurik asked Halvard and the party to prioritize the open threads; Malphestor — the likely best living source on the Nine Hells' involvement — was judged highest value. Ysolde's research (Religion 26) cross-referenced with Halvard's own dismissed rumors turned up a real, specific lead: **the Sable Crossing**, a crossroads shrine three days southeast, where a cloaked figure had spent weeks repeatedly starting bargains with desperate travelers and stopping himself before finishing them — the exact shape of something arguing with its own nature.

**The Sable Crossing.** Three clean days' ride brought the party to the crossing. At the Four Roads inn, a waitress and the innkeeper both confirmed the pattern — dusk, near the full moon, always unfinished bargains — and flagged a second watcher already staying at the inn. Rurik approached him directly: **Renner**, an agent working quietly for K., independently hunting the same rumor before R. could find it first, and willing to prove good faith by showing Rurik K.'s own mark worn openly, a real cost if the meeting were ever reported back. Rurik confirmed the figure's identity outright — **Malphestor, the Reckoner**, changing because Rurik had once told him devils might be able to rise — and Renner made the connection that reframed everything: old, sealed archive fragments name a "Reckoner" as the original broker who personally struck soul-tribute bargains with the Nine Hells centuries before Kathrivex's network existed in its current form. Renner agreed to relay it to K. honestly, ahead of R., and to hold back from the shrine entirely so Rurik could approach alone.

**Malphestor.** A first watch night passed clean. A daylight scout (Mara, Investigation 27) found scorch-marks from weeks of repeated visits, all facing northeast toward Duskgate; Ysolde's read on the shrine (16) sensed something old, conflicted, caught at a threshold. An afternoon canvassing the crossing (Wren, Persuasion 17) turned up consistent witness accounts — a bargain offered, then refused mid-sentence, the same unanswered question surfacing in different disguises each time. On the second watch night, Malphestor appeared. Rurik went out alone and asked him directly, three times over — can a devil rise, can a person change, can a person be redeemed — and then opened his own mind freely, showing him Dain, Corbett, Rennae's whole group, Osric, Vale and Merrick, the freed workers, Mirelle, and the pattern underneath all of it: truth, met freely, chosen freely. Malphestor answered yes to all three, genuinely, and asked to be brought to the rest of the party and to the council itself.

**Kathrivex's true origin.** Introduced properly to the whole party, Malphestor confirmed the deepest layer of the mystery yet: Kathrivex was once a minor mortal spirit of accounting that watched him broker soul-tribute for centuries, learned the mechanics from him, and eventually stole the entire trade — scaling one-bargain-at-a-time soul-currency into an industrial, automated empire without ever settling with the devil it learned from. Told the count is now literally paying soul-shards to the Nine Hells, Malphestor named it plainly as theft of his own unclaimed, unrenounced trade-craft — real standing to challenge the arrangement directly before Hell's own bureaucracy. Told the Raven Queen already recognizes Kathrivex's whole ledger as a counterfeit of her own domain, he connected the two grievances as a real, joint case few authorities could dismiss.

**A patrol, a watcher, and Greyfeather.** Riding for Ravenshollow with Malphestor visibly among the party, an alliance patrol nearly came to blows over his presence before Rurik's reputation and a direct order stood the riders down (Intimidation 21). That evening, Sael spotted a second, more disciplined watcher on a ridge; a scouting flight (Stealth 24, Perception 24) found only unmarked cloth and cold, professional tracks heading northeast — Malphestor confirmed nothing infernal about them. Later that night, two Greyfeather scouts sprang an ambush; Mara's Dread Ambusher alpha strike killed one outright, Rurik's Reckless Attack downed the second, who surrendered and, under a blade and Ysolde's healing, gave up a real dead-drop location (a hollow stump west of Kessick's Ford) and confirmed the contract on Malphestor's identity had doubled in price within the last two days — a live, reactive order, not a standing one. Rurik sent him back alive with a message: come face him directly, stop sending people who can't finish the job.

**Ravenshollow, and a second audience.** Corvane met the column himself, stood down his own alarmed guards, and heard the full account plainly. He and the party brought Malphestor to House Corvane's chantry, and **the Raven Queen answered a second summons** — recognizing Malphestor's old, honest trade, and the genuine change in him, without prompting. Rurik asked nothing of her, only reported the truth as found. She confirmed she cannot act against Kathrivex directly, but committed to formally recording her own witness of the theft against her domain wherever gods and devils are judged — real weight added to Malphestor's own case before Hell's bureaucracy. Corvane, moved by what he witnessed, agreed to ride to the council himself and give testimony firsthand.

**Home, late.** The urgent ride back to Urskelde pushed hard enough to nearly exceed Kaldur's fourteen-day window — two minor incidents (a stumbled horse, a curious pack of wild dogs turned off by Sael) but nothing worse. The party rode straight into a courtyard already full of banners: the full regional council had begun gathering without them. Kaldur confirmed every seat that could make it, had.

**Ends here for now:** Malphestor, Corvane, and the full weight of everything found since the keep — Vessa's warning, Kathrivex's true theft, the Raven Queen's second witness, Greyfeather's live contract — walking into a council that may end in a declaration of war.

**The council.** Vosk gave her own report first, praising Darkhawk's conduct at the keep directly to the assembled seats — mercy extended even under no obligation to give it. Halvard laid out R./K.'s marks and Greyfeather's organized, reactive nature. Ysolde reported the hub's destruction and her theory that it was likely a soul-shard foundry as well as a construct one. Rurik laid the recovered documents on the table one by one — Ilsa's letter, K.'s letter and token, the Auditor redesign, the terrain sketch — then recounted Vessa's contact in full: the demonstration, his declaration that this was never a negotiation, and her warning to watch the sky toward the burned court. Malphestor addressed the full council himself, naming Kathrivex's theft of his trade plainly in front of the room and asking nothing in return but to be heard. Corvane gave his own firsthand account of the second Raven Queen audience, staking his house's name on it.

Questions followed from every seat — cost, timeline, risk to Urskelde from Greyfeather's live contract on Malphestor — each answered honestly rather than reassuringly. Rurik declined to promise a march on the burned court blind, reaffirming his standing pattern: plan, investigate, then act. Kaldur put a concrete motion to a vote — not war declared outright, but formal commitment to recon toward the burned court and the coastal town, Malphestor's case and the Raven Queen's witness proceeding in parallel, and immediate action on the Greyfeather dead-drop. **Unanimous, five seats aye.**

**Milestone level-ups, earned together.** The hub's destruction, Kathrivex's true origin uncovered, Malphestor's genuine redemption, a second Raven Queen audience, and the council's unanimous commitment together closed a real chapter. Rurik: Barbarian 15, gaining **Persistent Rage** (182 HP). Mara: Ranger 19, +2 WIS to 18 (129 HP). Sael: Druid 17, a 9th-level spell slot, proficiency bonus corrected to +6 (113 HP). Ysolde: Cleric 18, a third Channel Divinity use (121 HP). Wren: Sorcerer 17, a 9th-level spell slot and **Meteor Swarm** learned, plus a previously-missing 8th-level slot corrected onto her sheet (91 HP).

**The Greyfeather dead-drop.** Rurik took the location straight to Halvard, who moved fast — a joint stakeout within the hour, Mara choosing the watch position. A clean Tier 1 ride out (one minor flavor incident) put the party in place by dusk; Mara cast Pass without Trace thirty minutes ahead of the expected pre-dawn window. At first light, a lone courier was tailed cleanly (Stealth 26) to a real location: an unmarked farmhouse a couple of miles off the main road. The party surrounded it — one of Halvard's people made noise moving into the rear position, drawing a wary but not hostile response from inside.

Rather than force the door, Rurik walked up and knocked directly. The woman who answered recognized him on sight; a second voice inside called back that they wanted to talk, not fight. Rurik went in alone and met **Corbin Ashe**, a mid-tier Greyfeather waypoint coordinator — not leadership, three tiers removed from anyone who'd know who ultimately funds the operation. Corbin confirmed the contract on Malphestor is unusually urgent, moving through channels even he doesn't have visibility into, and named the structure above him: an anonymous courier chain reporting to a figure known only as **"the Handler."** He agreed to flag a priority contact request through that same channel and relay Rurik's message word for word: **"Face me. Stop sending assassins."** Rurik ended the meeting without violence; Corbin, visibly relieved, expressed real discomfort with the contract's urgency himself. Halvard chose to leave the farmhouse under quiet surveillance rather than raid it, keeping the crack in Greyfeather's structure open rather than tipping their hand.

**Ends here for now:** the council's mandate secured, the party leveled and stronger, and a second open channel — Corbin's courier line to "the Handler" — sitting alongside Vessa's and Renner's, all three now waiting on someone above the count to actually answer.

## Session 46 — 2026-08-23

**What Malphestor actually needs.** With three open channels still waiting on a response and no one forcing the pace, Rurik asked Malphestor directly what a real appeal before Hell's own bureaucracy would require. Malphestor laid out four things, plainly admitting he doesn't have all of them yet: proof he held the soul-tribute trade before Kathrivex stole it (a mark or bargain-token predating the current network, not testimony — devils don't credit words alone); a sponsor with real standing inside the Nine Hells' own hierarchy, since a devil visibly questioning its own nature can't petition on its own credibility; outside witness, already in motion via the Raven Queen; and the evidence the party's already collected (Ilsa's letter, K.'s letter, the terrain sketch, the shard fragments) to establish scale even though none of it proves his claim directly.

**The sponsor doesn't need to be a friend.** Asked whether the sponsor had to come from the Nine Hells specifically, Malphestor confirmed it's a matter of jurisdiction, not loyalty — any devil with rank and a grudge, principled or personal, against Kathrivex's unlicensed rise would serve. He pointed at the obvious candidate: whatever devil actually holds the debt Vessa referenced, the one receiving tribute through Kathrivex's ledger rather than Kathrivex itself, likely has every reason to resent being cut out of understanding exactly what it's been fed. Ysolde and Halvard tied it to the council's existing recon mandate toward the burned court and the coastal town — find the creditor, and the sponsor and the target may be the same creature.

**The seal, described.** Pressed for detail, Malphestor described his old mark precisely: a black iron disc, two open hands holding a level, balanced scale — the honest original the fist-and-chain, eye-and-broken-chain, and fist-and-unbalanced-scales symbols all descend from, corrupted copies made by people who never understood why the scale had to stay level. A genuine bargain struck under his seal burned its impression into the parchment itself and stayed warm to the touch for as long as the bargain held — going cold, permanently, the moment either side broke it.

**Ysolde's recall — a natural 20.** Asked point-blank whether she'd seen anything like it in the archives, Ysolde placed it instantly: a badly water-damaged loose page pulled from Corvane's relocated archive material, catalogued and set aside months ago as an unexplained curiosity — a "seal of open hands," noted by a long-dead archivist as *"unnaturally warm to the touch, discard for repair or destroy,"* never actually discarded because *"unable to explain why it resists."* She hadn't had a description to match it against until now.

**Chasing the provenance — a rough one.** A follow-up pass through the recovered material (History 10) found the physical page itself back at Urskelde, but transit damage had worsened it further — the one line that would have named where the original object was found is now an illegible smear. What survived: *a seal of open hands, unnaturally warm, found among items held against an old debt.* The word "held" reads as collateral, not craft or purchase — and the page came out of House Corvane's own archive, pointing straight at family history rather than a public record.

**Corvane names the door.** Brought in directly, Lord Edrin Corvane placed it without hesitation once the details lined up: **the Sealed Vestry**, a locked door beneath Ravenshollow's undercroft, older than the current manor, family legend holding that an ancestor once held something against a debt and that reopening it would cost the house more than the debt was worth. Dismissed for generations as a ghost story kept to scare children off a crumbling cellar, it now reads as the likeliest place the genuine seal has sat untouched for centuries. Corvane committed to guiding the party there himself and sent a rider ahead that night to have the undercroft cleared.

**The plan.** Three council mandates threaded onto one trip: Ravenshollow overland (three days) to open the Sealed Vestry and cross-check the archive for an uncorrupted copy of the page, then south by ship — Tavik's vessel, already stationed at Ravenshollow as the first link in Joss's coastal corridor — straight to the poor coastal town for the council's recon mandate. Halvard flagged Malphestor's exposure aboard a ship (a smaller, harder-to-escape space than open road, with his contract price already doubled); Malphestor shrugged it off but agreed to stay below deck and out of port sightlines as a compromise. Halvard set a standing relay so any answer from Vessa, Renner, or Corbin's line to the Handler reaches the party at Ravenshollow or aboard ship rather than waiting on a return to Urskelde. Kaldur, Threnn, Idra, and Torvin hold Urskelde in the party's absence, as established.
