**Ends here for now:** departure set for two days out, giving Halvard's riders, Corvane's rider, and Tavik time to have everything ready — and Rurik two free days at Urskelde first, with Sesha/Harek/Vosk, Dain, Vale and Merrick, the memorial, the workshop, Alara, and the restitution fund all still sitting open for a visit before the road again.

## Session 47 — 2026-08-24

**Two free days, spent well.** Rurik made the rounds properly rather than resting idle. **Alara**: the rage-recovery tonic keeps improving batch to batch; the unidentified vial from the mountain keep's cabinet got a real working theory once she learned where it came from — not poison, not medicine, most likely something used to keep captives compliant right before the worst of what that place did to them. She's testing it properly on a stray dog rather than guessing further. Rurik also asked her to put out feelers among her old trade contacts for an enchanter willing to relocate to Urskelde, sky-iron itself as the draw — the two silver rings from the keep were shown to her first, her read: rank insignia, protective, real teeth to it.

**Family.** Sesha asked plainly whether the offer of unlocking Rurik's last blocked memory, with her present, still stood; Rurik answered honestly that he's good now, genuinely — he knows what he's fighting, no guessing left in it — and isn't reaching for it yet. She accepted that without hurt, the offer left open indefinitely. A sparring match with **Thyra** went to Rurik two hits to one, but her second hit was a clean natural 20 that actually rocked him. A demonstration of what a raging, Reckless-Attacking Rurik does to a practice dummy (24 damage in two swings) settled the question of a "real" rematch — not yet, not like that. Rurik suggested Thyra skip a full plate suit in favor of a lighter, faster-moving set; Perrin and Greta agreed instantly and are building her a fitted breastplate, vambraces, greaves, and open helm, deliberately light on the joints. Weeks out, not days.

**Vale, Merrick, and the coastal notes.** Vale, given real logistics responsibility under Joss, put together what he remembers of the old Circuit's coastal waypoints for the trip south — a hard, useful read: the poor coastal town is likely a corrupted waypoint rather than a chosen source, the same way Ravenshollow started honest before the Warden's bargain corrupted it underneath. Merrick, asked plainly why he specifically was kept on rather than sent elsewhere, got an honest but incomplete answer — that he'd never actually asked to leave, and the door was open if he wanted it. He accepted it, though the deeper truth (that Vale quietly groomed him as the bargain's unwitting successor) stayed untold, still an open decision for the party.

**Joss.** No word yet from Mirelle on the Urskelde dye shop offer — Joss is sending a gentle, no-pressure word rather than chasing an answer. Checked in on the returned soldiers and freed workers: Kael's already shadowing Urskelde's cooper, Petra's trade-ledger sense flagged for later use, Rennae and Dorst still settling into soldiering by choice rather than habit. Joss compiled a running list of Urskelde's remaining skill gaps — an enchanter, a fletcher, a real leatherworker, a second healer, a teacher — for Rurik to work on while traveling.

**Dain Halworth.** A real, warm check-in — Dain's recovery is ahead of what the healers expected, teaching pit-survival to a new generation rather than just recovering alone. Rurik offered his hand and told him plainly it would be an honor to fight alongside him once he's field-ready. Dain took it hard, in the good way, and promised to hold Rurik to it.

**The memorial.** Tolan's slow, deliberate work on Crate 5's contents continues — names carved where known, honest blank space left where they aren't. Rurik told him plainly he comes here before every mission, to remember what he's actually fighting for rather than losing the thread to an abstract war against a ledger too big to picture.

**Elenna.** Asked if she wanted to come south, she worked through it honestly rather than reaching for either easy answer — not willing to be something that needs protecting in a fight again, but not willing to just wait and wonder either. Settled on sailing along and waiting safely offshore, aboard ship, while the party works the coastal town directly. Gave two real local contacts: her mother **Sennet** (a mending stall near the fish market) and **Bren** (a once-honest harbor trader who might know exactly how deep the town's rot runs).

**A quiet night, and something settled.** Mara and Rurik closed the two days at home. Asked whether "Rurik" and "Darkhawk" were two sides of him, Mara answered that they used to feel that way but don't anymore — armor and the name underneath it, finally worn as one whole person rather than two halves held apart. Rurik said it back in his own words: both of them, not two sides of a coin anymore, just who he is. A real, quiet piece of identity work landing without any ritual or working needed to do it.

**The cast of characters artifact updated,** reflecting everyone met and every status changed since early August — Malphestor moved from the enemy list to family, a new "Above the Network" section for R., K., Vessa, Renner, Corbin Ashe, and the Handler.

**The ride to Ravenshollow.** Three clean days, Tier 1 ground the whole way — no incidents on the road or at either camp. Malphestor stayed guarded around any farmstead or crossroads shrine but opened up more than usual around the fire, sharing a piece of his own honest, centuries-old history for the first time unprompted. Elenna and Mara used the quiet time for basic shipboard knots ahead of the voyage to come.

**Ravenshollow, and Brother Aldous.** Corvane met the column and brought in his chantry's own scholar, **Brother Aldous**, to identify what the party was carrying. The two silver rings: one (overseer-tier) grants resistance to necrotic damage and wards the wearer against being tracked, scried, or divined; the other (senior-tier) carries the same privacy ward plus the ability to sense nearby active soul-tribute threads directly — a real tool for finding a site before walking into it blind. The silver-inlaid dueling daggers turned out to carry no active enchantment at all, just genuinely fine, silvered craft. Asked about an enchanter for Urskelde, Aldous named a real lead of his own — **Selwyn Vray**, estranged from his old guild over unlicensed methods, last known doing quiet private commissions on the coast — and agreed to chase it through House Corvane's old correspondence, results routed to Alara.

**The Sealed Vestry, opened.** Malphestor's own black iron door, carved with two open hands holding a level scale, tested everyone who approached rather than simply barring the way — Mara found no physical trap, Sael's primal read confirmed it as an honesty test rather than a hostile ward, Ysolde's usually-reliable Arcana read came back strangely muddled, and Wren's untrained instinct agreed with Sael's on gut feeling alone. Given the uncertainty of how the ward might read a devil mid-redemption, Malphestor waited outside by his own choice. Rurik opened it holding nothing but total honesty — no argument, no proof offered, just himself — and the door read him clean, no cost extracted, swinging open on hinges centuries old.

**What the Vestry held.** Malphestor's true seal — a small black iron disc, the honest original behind every corrupted sigil the network now wears — sitting undisturbed on a pedestal, along with its matching iron stylus and a brittle leather ledger fragment. Ysolde's careful read of the fragment recovered one intact line: an old debt *"held against the debt owed to... the Warden of the Ember Court"* — the first real name for the devil actually holding the tribute debt Vessa referenced, and the leading candidate for Malphestor's still-missing sponsor. Malphestor entered once the door proved honest and reclaimed the seal himself, visibly moved — the first time in centuries he'd let himself believe it might still exist.

**Aldric Corvane, vindicated.** An inscription near the door's inner frame, found by Sael and Ysolde together, resolved House Corvane's own centuries-old family shame: ancestor Aldric Corvane received the seal in a fair, honest trade with Malphestor, and sealed it away specifically so it would never be mistaken for something taken rather than freely given. Corvane, present for the reveal, took it hard in the best way — generations of his own family had turned an act of decency into a ghost story. Malphestor confirmed the memory, faint but real: a man who wanted a fair trade and paid an honest price for it, rare enough to remember the shape of even after everything since.

**The count, taken honestly.** Asked directly whether he had enough for his appeal, Malphestor counted it out: proof of prior claim, done; the Raven Queen's witness, already committed; the evidence establishing scale, already in hand. Only the sponsor remains — and the Ember Court is now a real, named direction to chase rather than a theory.

**Malphestor and Elenna, held safely offshore.** Given the Vestry's uncertainty and the coastal town's unknowns, Rurik asked Malphestor to stay off the ward's test until it was proven, and later asked both him and Elenna to wait aboard ship at sea — not even at the harbor — while the party works the coastal town directly. Both agreed readily, recognizing the sense in it over their own pride. Corvane's offer of a night's rest at Ravenshollow was declined in favor of sailing the same day, minimizing the window for Greyfeather's live contract to find a target.

**The voyage south.** Three clean days aboard Tavik's ship, no incidents. Wren fought seasickness the first day before Ysolde's remedy settled it; Elenna took to the sea fast, finishing her knot lessons with Mara; Malphestor and Elenna spent real time together belowdecks, two people anxiously waiting on word about someone they love. Ysolde cross-referenced what little her order's texts hold on infernal court structures, confirming "court" implies real rank rather than a colorful name. A flock of ordinary seabirds on the final approach briefly recalled Vessa's warning to watch the sky before resolving into nothing.

**Ends here for now:** the ship anchored well offshore of the poor coastal town, Malphestor and Elenna both waiting safely at sea, and the actual recon of the town itself still to come.

## Session 48 — 2026-08-25

**The recon begins.** Rurik put on the senior-mark silver ring before the longboat even launched — a faint, directionless pull toward shore, confirming the town's a live tribute site from well offshore. Sael scouted ahead in hawk form (natural 20 Perception) and mapped the town clean: one real street climbing from the harbor to a well-kept stone building with no signage, two armed watchers at its door who didn't move like fishermen, and a whole town giving the place a wide, unconscious berth — fear worn into habit rather than fresh. Several idle boats sat unused in the harbor, more than the fleet needed.

**Ashore, split deliberately.** Rurik and Mara went in together as ordinary travelers; Sael, Ysolde (vestments hidden under a plain shawl), and Wren peeled off separately to avoid reading as an armed group. At the fish market, Mara found **Sennet** — Elenna's mother — at a mending stall, and after light small talk (a fumbled Deception roll let real emotion slip through, nearly caught by a watching lookout) told her the full truth: Elenna's alive, freed, safe offshore, still looking for Aldren. Sennet held herself together in public and sent the two toward the smokehouse cellar as a safe rally point, spreading the word quietly on her way.

**The lookout, and a debt uncovered.** The man who'd clocked Mara and Sennet's exchange ran rather than fought; Rurik closed the distance and grappled him clean. Under pressure he broke — not for Rurik, whom he judged the lesser threat, but in grief: his daughter is collateral on a debt-thread to the stone house, same shape of coercion the party's freed people from before. Rurik's ring confirmed it directly, a real thread running off the man toward the house. Given ten minutes before his forced report and a promise to get his family clear, he counted out the building's numbers by stopping Rurik's whispered count at **seven** — two door guards, four rotating shifts, and one rarely-seen figure known only as "the Keeper." Rurik gave his name openly — "Rurik Blondin sends his regards, I am coming for them" — and the man recognized it immediately; word of the Reckoner's shadow has clearly reached this far south already.

**The assault.** V formation up the hill, Rurik at point, banging sword on shield and declaring himself by name loud enough to rattle the whole street — a strong Intimidation check gave the two door guards disadvantage on their opening attacks. Offered a chance to flee, the younger guard broke for it; the older one, threatened the same way the lookout had been, chose to die rather than let his family pay for his running. He did, between Mara's opening volley, Sael's bear-shape strikes, and Rurik's raging reckless attacks (one early crit). Four more guards boiled out the door once the fight turned loud — Bless, Hunter's Mark, a friendly-fire-conscious Moonbeam and Flame Strike, a Quickened Haste/Chain Lightning combo, and a readied 8th-level Thunderwave (timed to Rurik stepping clear of the doorway) ground all four down in three more rounds. **Six of the seven counted are dead, one fled** — no one in the party took real damage, Ysolde's Keeper of Souls quietly topping Rurik back up off two of the kills. "The Keeper" never showed himself, even through the noise.

**Searching the fallen.** A thorough joint search (Mara and Sael, near-max roll) turned up 62 gp, unremarkable gear, mundane shift-tracking tags, and — tucked in a boot — the guards' own rough patrol sketch of the house's interior: an entry hall, a side room marked only "holding," stairs up toward wherever the Keeper actually lives, and an unlabeled cellar hatch. Nothing about what's actually behind any of those doors.

**Six captives, freed.** Past the entry hall, a bolted-from-outside door held six chained captives — three men, two women, and a girl, Elsie, no older than eleven. Her father Bastian, the same lookout who'd given up the building's numbers earlier, was among them; Rurik tore the ring-bolt clean out of the floor without needing to roll for it. Bastian confirmed "the Keeper" walks the floor above on no fixed pattern, and that the cellar hatch (past the stairs, unlabeled) is "where they take you if the number's ever wrong." All six were sent toward the smokehouse cellar rally point.

**The Keeper, destroyed.** Up the stairs, a counting floor hung with tally-strings held the Keeper itself — gaunt, wrapped in ledger-skin parchment, quill-blade hands, a single lit tally-mark where a face should be. It named Rurik directly ("I remember the shape of you") before initiative was rolled. Three rounds of combat: Mara's Hunter's Mark volleys and Stalker's Flurry; Sael's Moonbeam (later broken by a failed concentration save) then a full triceratops Wild Shape, Trampling Charge knocking it prone; Rurik's Reckless Attack chain culminating in an Action Surge + Haste six-attack flurry; Wren's Sculpt Spell Fireball, sparing Rurik and Sael from her own blast and confirming the Keeper's **vulnerability to fire** (its parchment wrapping); Ysolde's Command ("Halt") resisted outright. The Keeper's own retaliation, **Debt Collapse**, hit the entire room with a 30-ft necrotic burst — everyone stayed standing, Sael and Ysolde taking the worst of it. Rurik's finishing blows ended it for good; Ysolde's Keeper of Souls (missed in the moment, corrected after the fact) topped her back to full off its death.

**The counting floor and private study.** Search of the dais and ledger stacks (Mara/Sael and Wren/Ysolde) turned up 84 gp, a dozen-odd crystallized soul-shard fragments, the Keeper's own iron stylus, and — critically — a shipment log showing the site's tribute rerouting to **"the Ember Court, by the Warden's own order,"** with one flagged entry: **Aldren Voss**, logged in transit toward it roughly three weeks back, no arrival ever confirmed. A locked private study (tally-coded dial lock, solved by counting carved tally marks — 5-4-1) held 210 gp, uncut gemstones, and two finds of real weight: **Osgood Farrow's ledger**, the Keeper's own diary from before its corruption — an honest factor's clerk consumed by the role, the same shape as Edmund Vale's inherited Warden title — and a **hand-drawn route chart to the Ember Court itself**, the first physical lead the party's had on it.

**The cellar.** Beneath the entry hall, over a hundred sealed clay jars held banked soul-tribute — "held, not grown," exactly as long suspected. Ysolde's reading (assisted once by Wren's Bend Luck) confirmed roughly a third still tethered to living people, the rest already gone, tribute nobody ever came back to close out.

**The town, told everything.** Rather than just report the kill, Rurik brought the gathered town — fifty or sixty people, Sennet and Bastian's family among them — directly through the stone house: the Keeper's remains, the freed captives' room, the cellar itself. Grief and disbelief, not fear, was what was left afterward. A young man asking after his missing brother Corin became the first real test of what came next.

**A safer method than severing.** Weighing the real risk of a mass severing (never attempted, genuinely unknown) against the slow individual method Ysolde and Rurik's own count-sense could offer, Rurik proposed something new entirely: rather than break the threads, **transfer them onto Malphestor's own reclaimed, honest ledger.** Sent for by Sending, Malphestor came ashore with Elenna despite his live, doubled-price contract — Elenna and Sennet reunited in the sand in front of the whole town, which did as much to open the crowd's guard as anything said aloud. Corin's thread was the test case: Rurik reached for it, Malphestor held the other end open rather than cutting, and it turned clean — no ripple, no flare back to Kathrivex's network, nothing like the backlash of the three prior severings. The seal at Malphestor's collar burned warm through the whole exchange.

**"Can a devil rise?"** Rurik asked Malphestor to answer his own Duskgate question, hand on his shoulder, in front of the town that had every reason to refuse him: *"When you are at the bottom and can see no way out, you choose to fall further into oblivion, or you choose to rise. What have you chosen today?"* Malphestor's answer, unprompted and unpolished: *"I chose to rise. Not because falling stopped being possible. Because for the first time, it stopped being the only thing I could imagine doing."*

**Milestone level-up, the full party, together** — earned for the Keeper's destruction, the stone house's captives freed, discovering a genuinely safe method to close the count, and Malphestor's redemption proven in front of witnesses rather than just claimed. Rurik: Barbarian 16/Fighter 4, **character level 20 — max**, taking **Sentinel**. Mara: Ranger 20, max for her class, gaining **Foe Slayer**. Sael: Druid 18, gaining **Beast Spells**. Ysolde: Cleric 19, taking **Resilient (Constitution)**. Wren: Sorcerer 18, gaining **Spell Bombardment**.

**Ends here for now:** the stone house fully cleared, the Keeper gone, a whole town beginning to believe something better than fear is possible, and Malphestor's next steps — the Ember Court route chart, and Aldren Voss's three-week-old trail — both pointing the same direction the party was already planning to go.

**Corren's Landing, closed out.** Over the following days, Malphestor learned he already knew the true shape of thread-transfer — his own oldest craft, just missing the specific reach Rurik taught him — and took over the actual work himself, faster and cleaner than anyone else could manage. Rurik, at Malphestor's own request, left the town's choice about his deeper role entirely up to them rather than asking for it; the town gathered and voted him in as steward on their own terms, not installed. **31 threads confirmed still tethered to living people were transferred safely**; the rest, the majority, were already gone and closed out honestly rather than left banked. The town's real name — **Corren's Landing** — was spoken again for the first time in years. A full town meeting formalized it: Corren's Landing joins the regional alliance, **Sennet** as its voice at council (interim leadership until a proper vote), Malphestor as steward. Elenna chose to stay with her mother rather than continue on. Practical threads opened: Tavik's coastal trade corridor now runs through Corren's Landing too, carrying Sennet's first trade message and Halvard's request for investigators to comb the stone house's remaining ledgers; Ysolde is writing to her own order at Ravenshollow about sending clergy and menders to help the alliance's rebuilding towns generally, starting here.

**Milestone level-up, the full party** — earned for the Keeper's destruction, the stone house's captives freed, discovering the thread-transfer method, and Malphestor's redemption proven in front of a whole town. Rurik: Barbarian 16/Fighter 4, **character level 20 — max**, taking **Sentinel**. Mara: Ranger 20, max for her class, gaining **Foe Slayer**. Sael: Druid 18, gaining **Beast Spells**. Ysolde: Cleric 19, taking **Resilient (Constitution)**. Wren: Sorcerer 18, gaining **Spell Bombardment**. Going forward, milestones for level-20 characters grant a DMG Epic Boon instead of a new level.

**North along the coast, toward the Ember Court.** Departing Corren's Landing by hired boat (Bastian piloting), two days out a pair of **Bone Devils** dropped out of the clouds on patrol — a real fight, Mara's Dread Ambusher and Wren's critical, Spell-Bombarded Fire Bolt opening hard, Rurik's Haste-fueled reckless flurries and a well-timed Chain Lightning finishing both off without any real danger to the party. A scorched flame-within-a-ring brand on one corpse confirmed the direction was right, though both bodies sank before a proper search could be made. A short rest closed the gap on lingering damage; the rest of the run north passed clean.

**The Ember Court, sighted.** A dead volcanic ridge cut into dark stone terraces, streaked with lava-glass, vents steaming even from a distance — no obvious walls, clearly built by something patient. Sael's aerial scout found two ways in: a watched front archway at the base, and a quieter, narrower fissure higher up the ridge, wide enough for one person at a time, shields off. Nothing readable at the top through the heat-shimmer. The party chose the vent, in order: **Rurik first, Ysolde second, then Sael and Wren, Mara covering the rear** — and pulled back for a full rest before committing to the approach.

**Ends here for now:** camped safely out of range, fully rested, the Ember Court's quiet fissure entrance chosen and the order set, nothing spent yet on the approach itself.

## Session 49 (continued) — 2026-08-25

**Into the vent.** Morning departure, Mara's Pass without Trace up before setting foot ashore. The fissure held to scouting: narrow but passable, opening after twenty feet into a wider chamber with a guard post visible ahead by firelight.

**The guard post, ambushed clean.** Two Bearded Devils, relaxed and mid-conversation, never got a turn — full surprise round, advantage on everything. Mara's opening volley (a crit among three hits) dropped the first outright; Sael's triceratops Gore/Stomp combo left the second on 2 HP before Rurik finished it with a plain, un-raged swing. Zero damage taken. A search of the post (26) turned up a **duty roster** ("the Warden's audience — dusk, upper terrace") and a **scratched reference map** of the corridor ahead, one branch marked with an unread warning glyph. Bodies hidden back in the fissure to buy time before the post's absence is noticed.

**The warning branch, investigated.** Mara's Primeval Awareness (mountain terrain, 6-mile range) confirmed both fiends and **elementals** present in the region — distinct signatures, not overlapping. Rurik and Mara scouted the marked corridor together and found a fire elemental contained in a warded basin, not a patrol. Ysolde's read on the glyph-work (24) confirmed it as old, repurposed containment craft — safe to pass at a distance so long as the basin rim and glyphs themselves go untouched. Noted as a viable, careful fallback route rather than tested further.

**The upper terrace, sighted.** A tense near-miss during the approach (a patrol passing just below, never looking up) gave way to a clear vantage over the terrace itself: a dais flanked by low braziers, three devils actively preparing ceremonial implements — an iron bowl, chain lengths, a second cold basin — rather than just standing idle. Off to one side, chained and unmoving but alive: a gaunt man in trader's boots, collared in dull grey metal. Given the shipment log's "in transit, Ember Court-bound" entry, almost certainly **Aldren Voss**. Sael, shifted down into rat form, confirmed the details up close without being noticed.

**Ends here for now:** the upper terrace mapped from a distance, a strong lead on Aldren Voss found alive (or at least still breathing), the Warden's dusk audience shaping up to be the real flashpoint, and nothing committed yet — no fight started, no position blown.

## Session 49 (continued II) — 2026-08-25

**The wait, and the choice.** Given three real options — wait for the audience, move now to free Voss, or find a quiet middle path — the party chose to wait. Two hours in, the guard post's absence was discovered (one of the terrace's three devils called away to investigate), raising the whole complex's alertness without exposing the party's hiding spot. Dusk arrived on schedule, and **the Warden of the Ember Court** itself appeared: tall, basalt-skinned, ember-veined, carrying a chained ledger as a badge of office rather than a weapon — visibly a presiding figure, not a raging one. Sael's rat-form eavesdropping (with a near-miss when a flanking devil's eyes tracked the sound) caught fragments: reference to "the debt still owed from the old broker's theft" (Malphestor), a warning about "a reckoning coming from more than one direction," and a direct order — **"Bring the trader forward. His account closes tonight, one way or another."**

**The charge.** Rurik broke cover and signaled the assault rather than risk Voss to whatever the ceremony intended — full alertness, no surprise round, a real fight against the Warden, two Horned Devils, three Bearded Devils, and a fleeing Imp that escaped to warn "the Court Above." Five rounds of genuinely the largest fight the party's faced: Mara's opening triple-shot (a crit among them) nearly dropped a Bearded Devil outright; the fiend consecration on the party's sky-iron weapons (rediscovered and put to real use for the first time in a while) bypassed devil resistance entirely; Rurik's Action-Surged, Hasted six-attack rounds tore repeatedly into the Warden; Wren's Sculpt-Spelled Prismatic Spray caught four targets at once (Horned Devil B's poison ray wasted on its immunity, the rest devastating); Ysolde's Banishment sent Horned Devil B back to Hell outright, removing 125 HP of threat from the board in one save; Sael's triceratops form was ultimately overwhelmed and reverted mid-fight, carrying 17 damage over into her own HP. The Warden's own Fire Aura and three-hit Quill-Blade Multiattack landed hard on Rurik every round, but raging resistance and two separate Keeper of Souls triggers (38 Hit Dice off the Warden's own death) kept him standing without real danger. Final blow: Rurik's own hands, finishing the last Horned Devil after the Warden fell.

**Aftermath.** The Warden's remains yielded little (a surviving quill-blade, untested, now carried) but the dais search turned up 340 gp, uncut rubies, and shipment manifests referencing **"the Reckoning House — final intake."** The Warden's own chained ledger, secured by Ysolde, corroborated Malphestor's theft claim independently ("the inherited claim, unresolved") and named a new, unplaced authority above even the Warden: **"the Court Above."** Voss's collar — a live binding thread — came free clean under Rurik's own hand, backed by Ysolde and the bear-spirit's protection; with the Warden already dead, the binding had nothing left anchoring it, and released without any backlash at all.

**The split.** Ysolde escorted a healed, walking Voss back toward Corren's Landing and Elenna. Rurik, Mara, Sael, and Wren pushed deeper under a second Pass without Trace, following the fled Imp's route into a proper administrative chamber — and found the Imp reporting to an unnamed figure in dark robes, who answered only: **"Tell the Court Above the audit stands incomplete. I'll handle the rest personally."**

**Ashworn.** Rather than stay hidden, Rurik stepped into the open and named himself directly — full name, spelled out, daring a reaction. He got one, but not the kind expected: the robed figure introduced itself calmly as **Ashworn, Auditor of the Court Above**, agreed openly that the Warden's methods were "old and cruel and embarrassing," and pressed a real question rather than drawing a weapon — what, exactly, is meant to replace what's being torn down? Rurik answered that he always has a plan; Ashworn's response made clear the Court Above is already watching closely: it named Corren's Landing's new steward, the safe thread-transfer method, and the regional council directly, unprompted, as things it already knows about. No violence offered on either side. Ashworn's closing line: it would genuinely like to hear the plan, before the rest of the Court Above decides it's simpler to burn it down from a distance instead of asking.

**Ends here for now:** a real conversation open rather than a fight, Ashworn's name and title on the record, and confirmation the Court Above has been watching the party's whole campaign far more closely than anyone realized. Nothing decided — the next words are still Rurik's to choose.

## Session 49 (continued III) — 2026-08-25

**"I am creating balance."** Pressed on what he actually offers in place of the systems he's tearing down, Rurik didn't describe a new structure — he named the thing underneath all of it: balance, the literal meaning of Malphestor's own seal. It landed harder than anything else said tonight. Ashworn confirmed the seal's significance directly and admitted the Court Above deals in ledgers and counts that are all, even the honest ones, still *systems* — Rurik was describing their absence, and calling that the actual plan.

**The truth about the hierarchy.** Pressed on whether the Court Above's resistance to real balance was ignorance or self-interest, Ashworn answered honestly: both, in different proportions it genuinely can't yet weigh. Imbalance is profitable — the whole structure is built on it, the same trick Kathrivex industrialized rather than invented. Asked directly who the Court Above itself answers to, Ashworn named **Voreth, the Ledger-Sovereign** — a real devil of standing in the Nine Hells, holding the actual tribute debt Vessa warned about, residing at **the burned court**. Confirmed R. and K. as "field negotiators, tier two," both reporting to the Court Above independently rather than to each other — hard confirmation of the fracture already suspected.

**"It's a fucking pyramid scheme."** Rurik's blunt summary of the whole structure (Voreth off Kathrivex, Kathrivex off Malphestor, the Warden off the towns, the Court Above off the Wardens) genuinely broke Ashworn's composure — real, surprised laughter, and open agreement that nobody in three centuries of formal audits had ever said it that plainly.

**A new informant, and a clear line.** Ashworn asked for a standing channel — accurate information both directions, mirroring Vessa and Renner — in exchange for early warning of whatever the Court Above decides. Rurik named it plainly ("informant") and Ashworn accepted the word without flinching. Rurik closed with an unambiguous warning to carry back: no negotiation, no redemption, no discussion if the Court Above chooses to come after him — Ashworn promised to deliver it word for word.

**The office, searched.** A cooperative search of Ashworn's own chamber (22) turned up a full regional accounting of the Court Above's tribute network (including sites never before known to the party), confirmation of R./K.'s "tier two" standing, a rough terrain sketch of the burned court marked "don't go alone" in a second hand, 480 gp, fire-touched black opals, and — found separately by Wren (23) — **a matched pair of Sending Stones**, almost certainly left deliberately as the actual informant channel rather than requiring a formal arrangement. Rurik took one, confident the other belongs to Ashworn.

**Regroup and return.** The four rejoined Ysolde at the shore, briefed her fully on the way back, and returned to Corren's Landing — Voss and Elenna already reunited, Sennet organizing the town's next steps, Malphestor's cellar work continuing steadily in the background.

**Ends here for now:** the Warden dead, Aldren Voss home safe, a real informant inside the Court Above, and a name and rough location for the true top of the hierarchy — **Voreth, the Ledger-Sovereign, at the burned court** — the clear next destination whenever the party's ready for it.

## Session 49 (continued IV) — 2026-08-25

**Taking the name to Malphestor.** Told of Voreth directly, Malphestor confirmed the name from his own old memory of the trade — old, powerful, known before Kathrivex ever existed. He reframed the hierarchy plainly: Voreth doesn't oppose Kathrivex's theft, he profits from it, sitting above it rather than beside it — which means Voreth was never a natural sponsor, only ever the wall the case was always going to run into. Asked who in the Nine Hells might actually oppose Voreth, Malphestor had no name to give freely, only a real question worth researching: who's been shorted by Voreth's quiet skimming, and would enjoy watching him answer for it.

**The plan sharpens.** Rather than investigate quietly, Rurik proposed going public — a formal accusation, on the record, forcing Voreth to answer rather than the party needing to build a case around his cooperation. Ysolde and Malphestor confirmed the mechanism was real: Hell's bureaucracy runs on contracts and formal accusation, and the Warden's own ledger already independently corroborates the theft. Burden of proof shifted onto Voreth by design.

**The Raven Queen's finding.** Traveling to Ravenshollow (clean, controlled corridor now that Corren's Landing is allied), the party returned to House Corvane's chantry. Rurik formally petitioned on Malphestor's behalf; the Raven Queen gently corrected the accusation's precision (Voreth didn't create Kathrivex — he found an existing theft and became its landlord) before hearing Malphestor speak for himself for the first time, presenting both his seal and the Warden's ledger as evidence. **Her judgment, formally recorded and binding: Voreth has knowingly profited from an ongoing theft against her domain.** Not accusation — finding. She confirmed this doesn't alone satisfy Hell's sponsor requirement, but that a divine finding this solid makes staying neutral costly for any devil positioned to gain from opposing Voreth publicly — real, practical leverage. She also acknowledged Malphestor's change at Corren's Landing directly, unprompted, as something that mattered to her even outside the evidence.

**Getting it into Hell's own record.** Malphestor identified two channels: Ashworn's Sending Stone (fast, procedurally respected) and his own case filing, already in motion, which he'll formally attach the finding to once back at a proper desk. Rurik sent immediate word to Ashworn — not a request, a formal summons: **Ashworn and a representative of the Court Above are to meet the party at Corren's Landing to answer the charges against Voreth, with Ashworn providing a neutral audit.** Ashworn acknowledged receipt, promised to carry it exactly as sent, and confirmed it would use the stone again once there's a real answer — no promises on what the Court Above actually sends, or whether anyone comes at all.

**Ends here for now:** a divine finding against Voreth permanently on record, a formal summons delivered and acknowledged, and the ball now genuinely in the Court Above's own hands — the burned court still waiting, but for the first time, Voreth's the one who has to decide what happens next.

## Session 49 (continued V) — 2026-08-25

**Word to Urskelde, and a real thread.** Lord Corvane sent riders to Kaldur, Threnn, and Halvard directly with the news. Halvard arrived at Corren's Landing three days later with Corim and a satchel of cross-referenced notes, and cross-referencing the regional accounting against his own Greyfeather evidence produced the investigation's biggest break yet: **Greyfeather is confirmed as the Court Above's own enforcement arm**, kept deliberately separate on paper, its payment routing matching known hits (Halloway's Reach, Bram, the doubled Malphestor contract) exactly. Further theorized: **the Reckoning House**, under active construction, is likely being built as Greyfeather's permanent headquarters, not just another tribute site. Held back deliberately as leverage rather than acted on immediately.

**Downtime, real and earned.** Days spent helping Corren's Landing properly rebuild — Rurik on the docks, Mara teaching trackers, Sael reshaping the land, Ysolde structuring the town's governance with Sennet, Wren speeding repairs and delighting the town's children. Ysolde confirmed real progress from her order: three healers en route within the month, and a lead on enchanter Selwyn Vray worth pursuing. A quiet, genuine character moment between Rurik and Ysolde — his first real acknowledgment of faith, hers of what that faith says about him rather than about her god.

**The deadline, and the arrival.** Rurik issued Ashworn a formal four-day ultimatum via the Sending Stone: send someone, or the party goes to the burned court itself. On the final day, confirmation came back — a representative was already en route. That evening, **Solvane, Chancellor of Accounts**, arrived alongside Ashworn aboard a Court Above vessel, sent with real standing to answer for Voreth.

**The confrontation, opened.** Rurik stepped back and let Malphestor speak for himself — a full, unflinching statement of his case: the original theft, Kathrivex's origin, Voreth's knowing profit, the Raven Queen's binding finding, and a direct demand that the Court Above answer honestly rather than keep profiting from what it knows is stolen. Rurik followed immediately with the held-back leverage — the Reckoning House's true purpose as Greyfeather's headquarters — visibly breaking Solvane's prepared composure for the first time. Pressed on whether it actually understood who it was negotiating with, Solvane admitted, with unusual honesty for someone in its position, that it had been briefed on facts but not on what those facts actually meant — and that it was beginning to suspect nobody above it fully understood that either.

**Ends here for now:** Solvane's composure genuinely cracking in real time, the full weight of Malphestor's case and the Greyfeather revelation both landed in the same conversation, and the actual answer — whatever the Court Above intends to do about any of it — still entirely unspoken.

## Session 49 (continued VI) — 2026-08-25

**The whole story, told plainly.** Asked whether Solvane truly understood who it was negotiating with, Rurik answered by telling the entire campaign from the beginning — the Ashpit, the betrayal, Thundertree and his reclaimed name, breaking the Warden's bargain at Duskgate through mercy rather than violence, reconnecting all four sealed sites, freeing the mountain keep and Halloway's Reach, and finally Malphestor's own redemption. Ysolde, Sael, and Wren each added their own piece. The point landed without needing to be stated outright: every part of the structure the Court Above protects has already lost to this party, every time, because they keep choosing something the structure was never built to survive being asked about honestly. Solvane's response: **"I need to reconsider what I was sent to negotiate."**

**Ashworn asked to actually be neutral.** Rurik called on Ashworn directly to fulfill its stated role rather than simply observe. Ashworn delivered a formal professional assessment — independently confirming the ledger, the Greyfeather routing codes, and the party's own conduct — and laid out three concrete asks Solvane could actually act on: formal acknowledgment of the theft, an immediate halt on Greyfeather operations against the party and its allies, and a genuine (if not immediately deliverable) answer on whether the Court Above intends to keep profiting from what it knows is stolen.

**Real concessions won.** Solvane granted the first two immediately, on its own authority: **formal acknowledgment that the theft is real and Voreth has knowingly profited from it**, and **an immediate provisional halt on all Greyfeather operations against the party and its allies, pending review**. The third answer — whether the Court Above will actually settle what's owed — goes back with Solvane for formal consideration, everything presented tonight attached exactly as given.

**The offer, and the line.** Rurik offered to travel to the Court Above directly and present the case in person if it would help. Solvane and Ashworn both counseled patience — the offer noted and valued, but held in reserve as an escalation rather than spent immediately. Rurik made clear the offer wasn't weakness: he was forged in something worse than Hell can threaten him with, and has no fear of it. Solvane, and Ashworn, both took this as genuine rather than posturing. Rurik closed with an absolute line: **any threat to a settlement or person under his protection, any identified assassination attempt, ends the negotiation entirely — and Wren gets her wish** (Meteor Swarm, held in reserve all session, finally cashed as the explicit consequence). Solvane accepted the condition without qualification.

**Ends here for now:** the Court Above's ship gone from Corren's Landing's harbor, real concessions won, Greyfeather formally halted pending review, an unmistakable line drawn and understood by everyone involved, and the Court Above's final answer — whether it will actually settle the debt — still to come.

## Session 50 — 2026-08-26

**Checking in with Halvard.** Before leaving Corren's Landing, Rurik found Halvard working out of a room above Sennet's stall alongside Corim. Three updates: the Greyfeather halt appears real so far (two known waypoints went dark within a day of Solvane's concession); the regional accounting now ties Greyfeather to three confirmed prior hits by matching payment-routing codes, hard proof rather than theory; and the Advanced Auditor schematics recovered from the mountain keep are confirmed workable on paper — soul-shard powered, no hub needed — and belong in front of the full council before the burned court, not after.

**Home to Urskelde.** Decision made to carry the news back in person rather than by rider. Malphestor stayed at Corren's Landing to continue his steward's work and his case; Halvard traveled with the party, sending Corim ahead to Urskelde by rider so the council wouldn't be blindsided. The return followed the same corridor as the trip out — three clean days by ship (Tavik's crew, Corren's Landing to Ravenshollow) and three clean days overland (Ravenshollow to Urskelde) — four risk checks rolled, all clear, no incidents on the road or at camp.

**The full council, briefed.** Kaldur called the long hall together the same evening on hearing there was good news for once. Rurik opened with the headline — direct contact with the Court Above, an immediate halt on all hostility including Greyfeather — and let the room feel it before handing detail to Halvard. Corvane, whose own family carried out the Ashpit order, was visibly shaken hearing it named halted in this room specifically. Halvard laid out the full chain for the table: the Ember Court, the Warden's death, Aldren Voss freed, Ashworn and Voreth named, the Raven Queen's binding finding, Solvane's arrival, and his own confirmation that Greyfeather is the Court Above's own enforcement arm — while being careful to flag the halt as provisional, not won outright.

**The schematics, settled.** Halvard raised the Advanced Auditor schematics as their own agenda item rather than let the good news bury them. After discussion, the council settled on both of Halvard's proposed paths at once: Corim and Perrin study the original at Urskelde to identify the flagged structural weakness, while a true copy goes to House Corvane's archive at Ravenshollow for safekeeping, warded the way the Sealed Vestry was.

**Other business, touched lightly.** Vosk confirmed he'll carry the alliance's ratification question to Thundertree's elders himself within days. Joss and Alara noted the coastal corridor holding steady and the enchanter search (Selwyn Vray, Alara's old contacts) still open with no bite yet. Dain Halworth spoke briefly, glad to hear tonight's news secondhand rather than the hard way. Council adjourned clean.

**The plan for the burned court.** Rurik proposed sending no lone envoy — instead, the full party, fully provisioned, making a visible camp at a deliberate distance from the burned court itself: close enough to be seen, far enough not to read as a threat, and then waiting. The table debated it seriously: Halvard and Ysolde favored restraint (the recovered terrain sketch is marked "don't go alone" in a second hand, and Solvane's review is already moving through the channel Rurik himself set up), Corvane suggested the Sending Stone alone might apply the same pressure more safely, Mara was ready to back a firmer push. Consensus landed on Rurik's plan as proposed, with conditions: Sael scouts the actual ground from the air before any campsite is chosen, and camp discipline stays at "well-prepared" (full watch rotation, wards active) every night, not "adequate."

**Rest, ordered.** Rurik called the party together and set two full days of real rest before departure — Ysolde specifically told to leave the books closed for once. Session closes on a rare quiet note: the party whole, a genuine council win behind them, and the burned court still ahead.

**Ends here for now:** Urskelde fully briefed and unanimous behind the next move, the Advanced Auditor threat being studied rather than ignored, a deliberate plan set for approaching Voreth's territory without lone-envoy risk, and two days of real rest starting now before the party heads for the burned court.

## Session 50 (continued) — 2026-08-26

**Alara's workshop.** Checked in on the rage-recovery tonic and the unmarked keep vial. The latest tonic batch tested live — Rurik raged deliberately, then drank it — and came back noticeably better than any prior batch, though not yet the finished article; Alara wants another round or two of refinement. The vial's still unconfirmed but she's now confident enough to stake her reputation on it: a compliance draught, not poison, meaning some of the network's "volunteers" over the years may never have volunteered at all. No bite yet on an enchanter from either her contacts or Brother Aldous's Selwyn Vray lead.

**Perrin and Greta's forge — the Auditor's real weak point found.** Corim and Perrin were mid-argument over the Advanced Auditor schematics (exposed shard housing vs. thin feed conduit) when Rurik called Thyra in for a second read. Thyra reframed the whole question as a feint — nobody leaves a vital exposed by accident — which sent Corim back through the schematic looking for what the bait was hiding. He found it: a discharge trap tied to breaching the housing **while the feed conduit is still live**. The safe kill order is confirmed — **conduit first, then housing** — and Rurik had it sent to Halvard immediately for distribution alliance-wide (Kessick's Ford, Halloway's Reach, Corren's Landing), with Perrin sketching a diagram to travel alongside the written warning.

**Thyra's sky-iron set, delivered.** The commissioned breastplate, vambraces, greaves, and open helm (ordered Session 47) were finished and waiting. Thyra was called in to see it fitted, visibly moved by the gift; a live drill afterward confirmed the lighter joints work exactly as argued for — full mobility, no loss of protection. Thread closed. She and Threnn both separately committed to doubling Urskelde's watch and ward discipline while the party's away at the burned court.

**A quiet word with Threnn.** Away from council business, Rurik checked on Threnn as a friend rather than a leader being managed — Threnn admitted, honestly, that the weight of being the one who decides whether it's safe to believe the peace holds doesn't fully leave him, even now. In turn, Rurik voiced his own real fear for the first time out loud: not the fight at the burned court itself, but what it could reach for if it goes wrong — the children, Urskelde, the people he loves. Threnn didn't talk him out of the fear, naming it as the same instinct Vessyn and Kethrun's ward already tested, and instead offered something concrete: doubling the watch and extending Corim's corruption-check protocol while the party's gone, so the fear has an actual answer rather than just weight to carry.

**Home to Mara.** Told her the same fear plainly. She confirmed she's carried it alongside him since the Standing Ring, and renewed her own standing vow — not distrust, but a promise not to let him pay any price alone.

**The feast, called and thrown.** Rurik proposed a full settlement feast for the next evening, purely to celebrate rather than to plan anything — Mara immediately backing it. The day of preparation pulled in the whole settlement: Joss running the stores, Alara producing a saved cask of proper mead, Threnn's people cooking goliath-kin dishes served without explanation first, Perrin and Greta converting the workshop's fire to an open-pit roast, and Wren's well-meant "help" with the cooking fire triggering a harmless Wild Magic flare of rainbow flame — immediately immortalized as "Wren's boar," toasted rather than scolded. Sael quietly reshaped the gathering square (softer ground, an early ring of wildflowers) and Ysolde spent the day making sure several people who'd never been properly thanked finally were.

**The feast itself.** A packed square — Kaldur, Threnn, Vosk, Corvane, Joss, Perrin, Greta, Dain Halworth, Sesha and Harek, Thyra in her new armor, the whole settlement present. Rurik opened with thanks to everyone who built the night, drawing a real cheer for each in turn (and a full toast to "Wren's boar"), then raised a second, solemn toast to the living and the dead — Corvane, Dain, and Sesha and Harek each visibly carrying their own weight of it, and Tolan stepping forward unbidden to set a recovered piece from his Crate 5 memorial on the table in silent tribute. After a long, real silence, Rurik broke the mood deliberately — a mock-serious "one last order," then downing a mug and shouting "DRINK AND BE MERRY" — and the square erupted into unguarded celebration, grief and joy both honestly held in the same night.

**A quiet moment at the cairns.** Slipping away unnoticed (or so he thought), Rurik spoke a private prayer to any listening god — asking nothing for himself, only that whoever he loves be protected whatever the outcome at the burned court. No voice answered, but a raven settled nearby, watched him a long moment, and flew off north — toward the burned court's direction — without any of the usual bird-startle at his presence. The Honest Ledger stayed silent: no counterfeit bargain offered, nothing owed for the asking. Mara had quietly followed partway and let him have the moment, and confirmed the same raven passed directly over her on its way out. Told her plainly what the prayer was for; she reaffirmed her vow not to let him carry it alone, and the two rejoined the feast together.

**Ends here for now:** the Advanced Auditor's real weak point identified and already spreading through the alliance, Thyra's armor delivered and proven, Urskelde's watch set to double while the party's away, a full settlement feast held and genuinely felt, and a quiet, unclaimed sign at the cairns that someone, somewhere, may have heard a prayer asking for nothing but the safety of the people Rurik loves. Two days' rest complete — the burned court is next.

## Session 51 — 2026-08-27

**The ward-ruin camp, established.** Sael scouted the approach from the air and found three candidate sites; the party chose a collapsed watch-post ruin over an exposed ridge and a safer streambed. A ground search (Mara leading, Ysolde reading the site's own old abjuration ward via Detect Magic) confirmed it genuinely blinds outside scrying — foreign craft, unidentified origin — and turned up old, violence-free remains in a collapsed corner: prior travelers who hid successfully but never made it back out, a warning about time rather than monsters. Ysolde layered her own hostile-intent ward alongside the ancient one rather than risk merging into unfamiliar craft. Rurik reinforced the collapsed wall into a real chokepoint; Sael and Mara secured water and food; Wren, thinking like the thief she once had to be, seeded the camp's blind approaches with tripwires and noisemakers and picked her own lookout perch. A hidden alcove find (Mara) turned up a dead traveler's journal: *"if it doesn't answer soon, we go home, we don't wait for the answer to come to us."*

**A three-day ultimatum, sent and answered.** Rurik used Ashworn's Sending Stone to announce the party's presence with deliberate dry humor ("bird spotting"), then, once the stone recharged, gave a formal three-day deadline before the party would "see the sights" themselves. Ashworn confirmed receipt and asked, genuinely, what "moving in" would actually mean if the deadline passed unanswered. Full council was briefed on this the same evening at the fire: Rurik's answer — front gates, no forcing it early, but total, no-holds-barred violence if violence came first — was heard and understood by the whole party, Ysolde making sure everyone chose it knowingly rather than by momentum. Ysolde laid out her actual protective kit for the walk: Death Ward pre-fight (no concentration cost), Bless for the opening exchange, Antilife Shell plus Mass Cure Wounds (freshly re-prepared) held in reserve if the fight turned hard.

**A patient, four-day patrol study.** Rather than press closer, the party spent several days tracking the valley's "irregular" devil movement from range — Sael's aerial passes, Mara's ground tracking, Ysolde synthesizing the pattern. It wasn't random: two-hour patrol shift-changes with thin windows between them, and once daily, at dusk, the *entire* valley pulled back toward the court itself for about an hour — almost certainly Voreth's own version of the Warden's dusk audience. A genuinely useful piece of intelligence, held in reserve rather than acted on immediately.

**Tested directly, and it held.** On the fourth night, Ysolde's ward flared for real — two Bone Devils, unordered household probes drawn by the fire, closed on the camp. A hard-fought but clean fight: Mara's opening volley (a crit plus two more hits) all but broke the first Bone Devil before it ever swung back; Rurik, hasted by Wren and blessed by Ysolde, Sael charging twice in triceratops form (Trampling Charge landing once, dropping Bone Devil A prone for a bonus stomp), finished both devils in three rounds. Ysolde's **Keeper of Souls** — missed in the moment, corrected after — healed Mara and Rurik 15 each from the two kills. No losses, no lasting conditions. Ashworn confirmed by stone, immediately and without hedging, that the attack was **not sanctioned** — three of Voreth's own household acting on their own initiative, undisciplined afterward, which told its own story about how much control Voreth actually holds over his house. A second, quieter test followed two nights later: an Imp scout caught trying to slip the perimeter, deliberately let go rather than captured — one more piece of proof, left for Voreth's side to find on their own.

**The deadline, and Voreth's answer.** On the appointed morning, Ashworn confirmed what he'd promised to find out: Solvane's own recommendation (full acknowledgment, real restitution) was heard by the Court Above and personally overruled — by **Voreth himself**, who chose to meet Rurik in person rather than hide behind intermediaries. Dusk, at the gate. Before departure, Rurik proposed opening with Wren's Meteor Swarm directly on the court itself as a preemptive strike; Ysolde, Sael, Mara, and Wren all pushed back, honestly and without deference, and Rurik stood down on his own judgment rather than his companions simply overruling him — noted, gratefully, for the record. Ysolde cast Death Ward the moment the walk began; the party approached in full V formation, in the open, exactly as planned, uncontested the whole way.

**Voreth, met at the gate.** Tall, composed, entirely unlike the household devils under him — he answered every question Rurik put to him honestly, at real visible cost each time. Confirmed the theft is real and that he has profited from it directly for three centuries, building the Court Above specifically to formalize the arrangement. Confirmed, when asked plainly, that **the original Ashpit order came from his own authority** — not personal malice, but an "audit flag": young Rurik was already registering as a soul that "didn't tally correctly" by their own ledger-logic, years before Thundertree, and someone close to Voreth's own seat ordered him removed rather than simply redirected. Confirmed, further, that nothing was ever moved against him openly *after* Dain Halworth's mercy closed his account — not restraint out of kindness, but fear: a debt ended by a free choice rather than payment or death is proof the ledger isn't absolute, and making that famous by killing him publicly would only spread the precedent. **This resolves the long-open mystery of why the original hit was ordered with such unnaturally precise targeting.**

**"What do I have to do to make you stop."** Voreth's honest answer: reparations and dismantling the network wouldn't be enough. Real change requires him to publicly sponsor Malphestor's claim and stand against Kathrivex directly — a confession, from a Ledger-Sovereign, that the whole trade was stolen from the start — which would end Greyfeather's halt instantly and turn Kathrivex, R., and K. against him personally. Rurik proposed honest single combat instead: if Rurik won, Voreth sponsors Malphestor's case and stands against Kathrivex; if Voreth won, the claim dies permanently. Rurik refused to offer any further personal stake beyond his own life — "you don't get to take anymore" — and Voreth, genuinely moved rather than insulted, accepted those exact terms rather than press for more, and sealed it with a handshake that rang like a bell through both parties, binding by infernal contract-magic.

**The duel.** A brutal, honestly-run fight, both sides briefed and unaided by their own parties as agreed. Voreth's opening Multiattack alone nearly ended it — a critical hit downgraded by the sky-iron helm's once-per-rest insurance, then reduced further by Stone's Endurance, still landing for 88. Rurik answered with Rage and Action Surge for four attacks in return. The fight ground on for four brutal rounds, Voreth's Legendary Actions (a fear-inducing Baleful Glare plus a bonus Scythe Strike every round) keeping constant pressure even between his own turns. **Death Ward** absorbed what should have been a killing blow in round 3, dropping Rurik to 1 rather than 0. A second knockdown followed almost immediately; **Relentless Rage** held once (DC 10) — then failed the second time (DC 15), dropping Rurik fully unconscious.

**The bear-spirit's answer, and Wren's reaction.** Rather than accept it, Rurik — unconscious, the fight already formally called by Voreth, who chose *not* to deliver a killing blow to a fallen man who'd told him the plain truth all night — reached for the bond one final time: an unscripted plea to rise, echoing the same bond that first pulled him out of the Ashpit's pit. The saving throw (DC 20) came up one point short — until **Wren's Bend Luck**, thrown as a reaction in real time, pushed it over. Rurik woke mid-collapse, breathing, twelve hit points and fully conscious, to a valley gone completely silent with shock — Mara, Ysolde, Sael, Wren, and Voreth himself all staring at something none of them had a category for.

**The wager, and what came after it.** Voreth held the line honestly: the contract was sealed before the miracle happened, and he wouldn't insult either of them by bending it after the fact. **Malphestor's claim through Voreth is closed, permanently, exactly as wagered.** But what Voreth witnessed shook him past the fight itself — he confirmed real things exist older than three centuries of devildom that he has no framework for, admitted the encounter changed how he sees "what kind of man" he'd been fighting, and volunteered, unprompted and outside the wager's actual terms, that Kathrivex's true ledger has a real, exploitable weakness he knows of but isn't offering tonight. He gave his word — direct, personal, unbound by any contract — that Corren's Landing, Urskelde, and everyone standing with the party are safe from him regardless of how the rest of this plays out. Rurik answered plainly: *"I have to balance the count. I have to stop you and Kathrivex. That is not a threat. That is a promise."* Voreth received it as exactly that, and let the party withdraw without further contest.

Rurik collapsed the moment the adrenaline had nowhere left to go, carried back to the ward-ruin camp by the rest of the party and slept through into the next day, safe.

**Milestone, full party.** Solving the Ashpit's founding mystery and surviving the Voreth duel earned the whole party a milestone together, same as always. Rurik and Mara, both already at their class caps, each took an Epic Boon instead of a new level: Rurik the **Boon of the Bear's Last Stand** (custom — Relentless Rage's DC no longer escalates and can now answer even without raging), Mara the **Boon of Peerless Aim** (ranged attacks ignore cover, no more long-range disadvantage). Sael reached Druid 19 (+2 CON), Ysolde reached Cleric 20 — max, Divine Intervention now automatic — and Wren reached Sorcerer 19 (+2 CON, 19 sorcery points).

**Ends here for now:** Malphestor's formal claim through Voreth is closed — a real, painful loss, honestly earned and honestly lost — but the mystery of the original Ashpit order is finally solved, Voreth himself is genuinely shaken and newly uncertain which side he'll ultimately land on, a real lead on Kathrivex's true ledger exists and is being dangled rather than given, Rurik's own nature — something the Nine Hells themselves have no name for — is now impossible for the Court Above to un-know, and the bear-spirit's answer tonight has become a permanent, reliable part of who he is rather than a one-time miracle.

## Session 51 (continued) — 2026-08-27

**The morning after.** Rurik woke properly the next day, physically whole but honest with the party that he wasn't okay — the loss of Malphestor's claim landing as a personal failure rather than the honestly-fought defeat it actually was. Mara, Ysolde, and Sael each pushed back on the self-blame directly rather than just comfort him past it: he hadn't lost through weakness, he'd made a devil of real standing doubt itself and walked out shaken. Rurik walked out alone to process it properly; the bear-spirit, unreached-for this time, offered its own truth unprompted — *"You measure the fight by what it took from you. We measure it by what you carried out of it alive. Both are true. Only one of them is the whole of it."*

**Ashworn's visit, and three real questions.** Rurik called Ashworn to camp to ask plainly: where things stand with Voreth (genuinely shaken, uncommitted, neither ally nor enemy, but his personal word that Corren's Landing, Urskelde, and the party's allies are safe from him is one Ashworn believes); who R. and K. actually are (still unknown even to Ashworn — they report past his own oversight, deliberately insulated, though he's committed to actually digging into Court Above personnel records now rather than respecting the boundary that's kept him blind); and how to find a route into Kathrivex's own operation (no direct access, but the Reckoning House, R./K., and Voreth's own insider knowledge of Kathrivex's ledger are the three real threads).

**The proposal: balance between three, not two.** Rurik reframed the entire conflict — he was never wrong to trust Voreth or Malphestor, he was wrong to think stopping *both* was the answer. Sitting with the party, he articulated the deeper truth: Voreth didn't invent the theft, he **replaced Malphestor** in the same role, further from the original crime and therefore able to call it profit rather than theft outright. Malphestor's honest count lacked any opposing weight to check it, which is precisely the vacuum Kathrivex exploited completely — and Rurik himself is the anomaly that total imbalance produced. The fix isn't destroying Voreth or crowning Malphestor — it's two honest, mutually-checking counts, built together, with Kathrivex as the actual target rather than either devil. Rurik proposed this to Ashworn as an offer to carry back: help Voreth separate his operation from Kathrivex, in genuine partnership with Malphestor, rather than destruction or a takeover.

**Voreth's answer, and the meeting inside the camp.** Ashworn returned that evening: Voreth said yes, and asked to meet at the party's own camp rather than his gate, framing it as Rurik's call to make. Rurik chose to let him inside — "we have fought to the death, that earns respect and an element of trust." Voreth arrived the next midday, visibly humbled by the invitation itself, and when Rurik laid out the "replaced Malphestor" realization directly, it landed as the single most significant thing said to him all week — a genuine reframe of his own nature, not as an enemy to defeat but as a corrupted version of a role someone else has already proven can be practiced honestly. He committed to meeting Malphestor directly, as equals building the practice together rather than a devil granted mercy. Ysolde reached Malphestor by Sending immediately; he's traveling to the ward-ruin camp now, by his own choice, leaving Corren's Landing in Sennet's hands.

**The endgame plan, taking real shape.** With Voreth gone to set things in motion on his own side, the party worked through what "removing Kathrivex" actually requires: not a fight, a **severing** — total and simultaneous, at the one true central hub the whole network roots through (still unidentified; the Reckoning House, R./K., and Voreth's insider knowledge are the leads). Ysolde's scholarship confirmed Kathrivex's power is transactional, not faith-based — cut the network completely and it doesn't get banished, it withers into what it started as, a spirit rather than a god. Rurik identified the second half: gods rarely manifest directly on the mortal plane by choice, bound by the same law the Raven Queen respects; forcing Kathrivex into physical intervention to defend its own domain would expose it to genuine vulnerability, and potentially to formal action from any god who's already recorded a finding against it. The plan's real danger was named too: severing the hub without Malphestor and Voreth both ready to simultaneously transfer every still-living thread across the *entire* network risks a Debt Collapse at a scale beyond anything the party's faced, and holding a siege against Kathrivex's avatar (should it manifest) will demand far more endurance than the single duel with Voreth ever did.

**Thossia's judgment.** Rurik asked Ysolde whether Divine Intervention could put a direct question to her own goddess: does something standing in false judgment over mortals still owe a debt? Now freshly 20th level, her call succeeded automatically. Thossia answered directly, through her: Kathrivex's authority is not metaphorically illegitimate but structurally so — a mortal spirit wearing a stolen office, and what it owes is total, to be answered before the one it stole from. She named the target without naming a place: *"the seat where every debt not yet an anomaly still resolves"* — Kathrivex's true ledger, not the Reckoning House's infrastructure. Rurik noted the deeper win: Thossia, whose exact office Kathrivex has been counterfeiting, now holds direct, unprompted awareness of the crime — a second divine witness alongside the Raven Queen, with no promise of intervention, but no more darkness to hide the debt in either.

**Milestone, full party (corrected).** The DM's original write-up gave Rurik alone an Epic Boon; caught and corrected, the whole party leveled together as always. Rurik and Mara, both already at character level 20, each took an Epic Boon: Rurik the **Boon of the Bear's Last Stand**, Mara the **Boon of Peerless Aim** (ranged attacks ignore cover, no long-range disadvantage). Sael reached **Druid 19** (+2 CON), Ysolde reached **Cleric 20 — max** (Divine Intervention now automatic), and Wren reached **Sorcerer 19** (+2 CON, 19 sorcery points).

**Ends here for now:** a real, structural plan against Kathrivex finally taking shape — Voreth and Malphestor walking toward genuine partnership, two divine witnesses now aware of the theft, a working theory for how to actually strip a god of its power, and a hard, honest warning about the scale of care the rescue side of that plan will demand. Malphestor is en route to the ward-ruin camp. The true ledger's location is still unknown — the next real question to answer.

## Session 51 (continued II) — 2026-08-27

**A second, organized strike.** While waiting on Malphestor, the camp was hit again — a Horned Devil and two Bearded Devils, deliberate and coordinated rather than a household accident. Sael's readied max-level Thunderwave (10d8) caught the Horned Devil at 15 feet, dealing 50 damage and shoving it clear of Rurik's own readied attack. Mara's opening volley and Wren's readied Chain Lightning (10d8 to all three targets, none saving) gutted the fight before it properly started — Bearded Devil B dead outright, the other two finished a round later by Mara. Zero damage taken. A search of the remains (with the party's help, granting advantage that didn't end up mattering) turned up a half-melted signet: **R.'s own mark**, not Voreth's household — confirmed by Ashworn, who committed to tracing whether it was R.'s personal order or another unauthorized strike.

**Malphestor's arrival, and the historic meeting.** Malphestor reached the camp days later, disbelieving until he heard the whole account firsthand — the duel, the "Voreth replaced Malphestor" reframe landing on him as hard as it had on Voreth, and the plan taking shape since. Voreth and Ashworn arrived within the hour of being summoned. Rurik opened the meeting by asking both devils to trust him enough to sit and listen, honestly, without hostility — both agreed, sealed by Malphestor's own bargain-instinct and Voreth's plain word.

**"What do you want."** Asked directly, Malphestor answered: to practice honestly without limit, the theft named plainly, and the role itself made safe for whoever holds it next. Voreth answered just as honestly: to stop fearing his own reflection, to know if change is real for something like him, and protection for the real risk of choosing it openly.

**The endgame, laid out in full.** Rurik's actual proposal: once Kathrivex is removed, Voreth's operation continues, Malphestor's restarts alongside it, and each passes to the other any debt that doesn't fit their own practice — no more cherry-picking, every account handled honestly by whichever side actually can. Both devils recognized it immediately as the structural fix to the exact vulnerability that let Kathrivex corrupt Malphestor's original, unchecked count in the first place. Both accepted, provisionally, pending proof in practice.

**The Reckoning House, finally named in full.** With the endgame's shape agreed, Voreth gave the last piece he'd been holding back since the wager: the Reckoning House isn't just Greyfeather's headquarters, it's being built specifically to permanently house **Kathrivex's true ledger** — a living accounting-anchor, not a physical object, currently exposed because construction isn't finished. Once it is, the window closes. Time remaining unknown, but close.

**Deniability, built deliberately.** Rurik insisted neither devil's hand can be seen in what comes next — it must look like the party acting alone against Kathrivex, or the rest of the infernal hierarchy answers a "coup" with total force against both of them. He further chose not to reveal the actual plan to either devil at all, protecting them from ever being compelled to betray it. Both received this as a genuine kindness rather than mere caution. R. and K. need to be dealt with first and separately, ideally before the Reckoning House move, to pull Kathrivex's full attention onto the party alone by the time they arrive.

**R. and K., named — but not spoken aloud.** Voreth confirmed he knows both true names and standing, and agreed to pass them through Ashworn's ordinary Auditor's work rather than say them in the open valley, protecting the channel itself. Delivery expected within days.

**The staged falling-out.** As a final piece of cover, Rurik and Voreth deliberately performed a public break — mock fury, Voreth's cold contempt, Rurik offering Malphestor "as payment," Malphestor's convincing flinch — loud enough to be believed by anyone watching or reporting, before Voreth and Ashworn withdrew over the ridge. The whole party recognized it as theater the moment they were gone. Malphestor departed for Corren's Landing; the party for Urskelde.

**Ends here for now:** a genuine, protected alliance built from nothing but honesty, the Reckoning House confirmed as Kathrivex's true seat with a closing window, a clear sequence set (deal with R./K. first, alone, then strike the ledger before it's sealed), and the last piece of the puzzle — R. and K.'s true names — arriving quietly through Ashworn within days. Two devils now walk away from this fire believing in something neither of them thought possible a week ago.

## Session 51 (continued III) — 2026-08-27

**Home to Urskelde.** The six-day return journey came back clean on every leg but one — mid-voyage, Ashworn's Sending confirmed R. and K.'s names were verified and ready, but he declined to speak them over the stone itself, judging the exchange not private enough for something this dangerous. Kaldur met the party at the gate with the full council already gathering; Rurik asked for a two-day postponement to get the names properly and in person first, and to actually process everything before briefing the room. Kaldur agreed without hesitation, covering for it as ordinary rest.

**A private word with Kaldur.** Before anything else, Rurik gave Kaldur the full account alone — the duel, the alliance, the Reckoning House, the reasoning behind the delay — and was asked plainly whether he himself was actually alright. Rurik's honest answer ("No... but I have to be, someone has to do it") drew real pushback rather than easy sympathy: Kaldur naming that instinct as the same one that runs good people into the ground, without judgment, before simply telling him to go rest properly and be honest with the people who actually love him.

**Two days, spent honestly.** Rurik kept out of the settlement entirely, and the deepest conversation of the whole arc happened at home with Mara — the coldness and jumpiness she'd noticed named directly: fear, specifically the fear of helplessness, of control being taken away entirely in a way it never had been before Voreth's first exchange. Mara held both truths at once without flinching from either: that facing Voreth alone was tactically correct (none of the others could have survived what he absorbed) and that watching it cost her regardless. Rurik admitted feeling "the cold oblivion reach out" during the failed Relentless Rage save, and voiced real uncertainty about whether the fear would resurface in a future fight. No easy resolution was offered — just honesty, and Mara's plain commitment to face whatever comes next together rather than have him carry it alone again.

**The names, delivered in person.** Mara scouted a secure hollow near Urskelde and cast Pass without Trace for the trip out; Ashworn met them there, unarmed, as promised. **R. is Ravask** — cold, methodical, genuinely believes the network is a service rather than theft, holding the position under a century. **K. is Kellidra** — older, trained Ravask into the role originally, and has shown real, consistent protectiveness toward her own subordinates (Ilsa, and sending Renner to investigate honestly rather than for advantage) that reads as someone quietly managing damage from inside the structure rather than someone at peace with it. Ashworn's honest read: Kellidra may be reachable through direct, honest confrontation the same way Voreth was; Ravask reads as already having made peace with what he is, and reasoning with him is likely as unlikely to work as reasoning with Kathrivex itself. Rurik thanked Ashworn plainly for the personal risk he's taken across this whole arc — visibly moving him.

**Fear examined, not exorcised.** In the thirty minutes before the council, Rurik deliberately sat with the fear rather than fighting it — tracing it to its actual root (helplessness, not death itself) and recognizing it as the same instinct that's always kept him choosing mercy and partnership over pride: not an enemy to defeat, but an old warning system finally being listened to.

**The full council, told everything.** Rurik walked the entire council through the whole arc, holding nothing back — the duel and dying three times, Voreth's confession about the original Ashpit order, the refusal to make it right afterward, the choice to challenge him anyway, what pulled him back from wanting to give up ("friends, family, people who love me and I love back"), the reframe, the alliance, the Reckoning House, and finally Ravask and Kellidra's names. The room absorbed it in stages — grief, alarm, then genuine hope — with Corvane in particular moved past his own old family shame by finally understanding the true shape of the order that struck at the Ashpit.

**Tasks set.** Halvard: begin patient, long-hold surveillance on the Reckoning House to establish its actual construction timeline, and start tracking down Ravask's and Kellidra's real seats of operation now that names replace symbols. Kaldur: begin gear and armor preparation with Perrin and Greta for a possible future confrontation with an avatar of Kathrivex, and start quietly planning what a real Urskelde mobilization would require — under Urskelde's own banner, deliberately, since Rurik wants Kathrivex's attention fixed fully on him and Urskelde specifically rather than scattered toward suspicion of Voreth or Malphestor.
